# Changelog

Completed work, most recent first. Moved out of `TODO.md` on 2026-05-22.


coverage-gap-probe TEST_DIR fix landed (2026-06-09). Closes the follow-up filed in commit `032683ca`'s body: the probe was hard-coded to scan `packages/backends/test/`, a directory that no longer exists since the 2026-06-05 test-layout reorg (see CHANGELOG row "Test layout reorg — hybrid colocation"). Every OP_* and FFI symbol was reported MISSING regardless of actual coverage — 133/133 OP_* MISSING (tape=69, mlx=64), 238 zero-hit symbols — making the probe useless as a coverage signal. **Fix**: replace the single `TEST_DIR = packages/backends/test` with a list of search roots matching the colocated layout — `backend_{tape,torch,mlx}/` for per-backend tests + `packages/idris-test-c/src/` for cross-cutting infra; add `grep_word_in_dirs(symbol, roots)` to `mltools/header_parser.py` that silently skips roots that don't exist (backend_torch has no colocated tests today); per-OP search narrows to the matching backend's tree + cross-cutting (a tape OP can only be exercised through tape's backward dispatch). **Result on the same dylib**: OP_* MISSING drops 133 → 7 (tape 69 → 0, mlx 64 → 7 — the seven mlx ops are real coverage gaps: `OP_AVG_POOL2D` / `OP_GELU` / `OP_MASKED_FILL` / `OP_MAX_POOL2D_BATCHED` / `OP_RMS_NORM_2D` / `OP_SWIGLU_2D` / `OP_TILE_2D`, all lacking colocated tests under `backend_mlx/`); FFI symbols with zero test hits drops 238 → 27; 126 OPs flip MISSING → present; canonical-symbol sanity check passes (`tensor_add` 10 hits, `tensor_backward` 53 hits, `tensor_matmul` 4 hits). **Doc reconciliation in the same commit**: `docs/develop/coverage-policy.md` had two stale references to the dead `test/common/` tree — the contributor checklist at line 137 ("Add a Criterion test at `test/common/<area>/test_foo.c`") and the no-W6 rationale at line 192 ("The Criterion suite under `packages/backends/test/common/`"); both updated to the colocated layout. **Tests**: `scripts/tests/test_coverage_gap_probe.py` adds three assertions (`tensor_add` test coverage, `tensor_backward` ≥ 5 hits, missing-root tolerance) as a regression guard. **What this doesn't do**: file the seven mlx coverage gaps as their own TODO row — that's a follow-up; the probe is now correct enough to surface them. Stale Makefile comments referencing the dead path (line 1020-1022 + 1042-1047) untouched; their cleanup is a `feedback_no_phase_refs_in_committed_docs` violation predating this work.


`scripts/` survey + restructure landed (2026-06-09). Closes the Medium "Survey & restructure `scripts/`" row. Six commits, each independently green: `a6f857a1` deletes 10 completed one-shot codemods + lifecycle migrations whose work is in committed source (`rename_device_to_executor.py` `7d782edb`, `rename_d_to_ex.py` `feb387db`, plus 8 Phase-X.Y migrations — `migrate-wrap-v2.py` `d638beee`, `drop_perdtype_streamed.py` `aabf8b1c`, `remap_dtags_kind_major.py` `35bc2445`, `sweep_tape_void_ptr.py` `52ffacb0`, `extract_test_inline_blocks.py` `25a0bfce`, `unify_dtag_wrappers.py` `e6505739`, `prime-drain-lazy-init.py` `7a58b22d`, `seed-instance-meta.py` `612aaa69`); `−1843 LOC` of dead code with introducing-commit hashes in the commit body so future archaeology has a pointer. `9bd7cd29` creates `scripts/mltools/` Python package + extracts the perf-log Entry hub: `mltools/perf_log.py` exposes `append_run` / `append_baseline` / `append_op_bench` / `iter_entries` + parsers (Converged / Completed / RESULT / `[stage]` line parsing, `PERF_MS_PER_EP` extraction, op-bench output parsing) + a writer CLI; `mltools/__init__.py` marks the package; `scripts/perf_lib.sh` is the bash sidecar (resolves `PERF_REPO_ROOT` + exports `PYTHONPATH=$REPO/scripts` + thin helpers `perf_commit_with_dirty` / `perf_extract_marker` / `perf_pretty_elapsed_ms` / `perf_device_for` / `perf_mlx_compile_state` / `perf_extract_axis_d_{tokens,wall}`); five perf scripts migrate to source it + dispatch heredoc JSON construction through `python3 -m mltools.perf_log <subcommand>` (`perf-run.sh` 324 → 156 LOC, `perf-baseline.sh` 230 → 145 LOC, `perf-sweep.sh` 324 → 219 LOC, `perf-fast.sh` 160 → 91 LOC, `perf-nightly.sh` 304 → 188 LOC); `render-benchmarks.py` + `check-perf-regression.py` import `iter_entries` from the package instead of carrying their own JSONL loaders. Schema unchanged (`docs/develop/perf-log.md` still authoritative); BENCHMARKS.md md5 byte-identical pre/post (`06b616c05390220ccd6931067f927407`); regression-gate stdout md5 identical (`158e1adbabb0f135700fe683725374d3`). `032683ca` migrates `coverage-gap-probe.sh` (187-line 7-stage `sed | grep | awk` chain doing a relational join over OP_* / source / FFI / test) to Python by way of growing the package with `mltools/header_parser.py` (backend.h enum scan, source-file FFI symbol extraction, `extract_backend_h_symbols`, `find_op_source`, `grep_word_in_dir`); set-diff vs bash reveals two pre-existing greedy-regex bugs that the migration fixes — bash over-captures `activation`/`backward`/`max`/`sigmoid`/`zero_all_grads` from in-line comments (its greedy `sed` substitution matches up to the last `(` on each line, picking up identifiers from comment text), and conversely under-captures the corresponding legit decls `param_zero_all_grads_return` / `tensor_backward_return` / `tensor_gelu` / `tensor_leaky_relu` / `tensor_silu` / `tensor_softplus`; the Python parser is line-anchored + non-greedy, captures all 232-symbol intersection correctly. A separate pre-existing `TEST_DIR=packages/backends/test` bug (tests are now colocated at `backend_<b>/<area>/test_*.c`) is preserved here for clean migration semantics — fixing the directory traversal is its own concern, filed as follow-up. `0c2879fd` migrates `sweep.sh` (259-line bash with jq + recursive cartesian + xargs -P + awk/sed CSV) to Python by way of `mltools/sweep_grid.py` (cartesian product via `itertools.product`-style fold, `random_sample` with `random.Random`-deterministic seeding, CSV assembly via stdlib `csv`, `parse_result` re-exported from `perf_log` for one canonical RESULT parser); parallelism switches from `xargs -P` to `concurrent.futures.ProcessPoolExecutor` with first-class exception surfaces; CLAUDE.md "Build Commands" + docs/gotchas + design-decisions doc references updated to `python3 scripts/sweep.py`. `2691de47` renames `scripts/lifecycle/` to `scripts/codegen/` (the post-cleanup directory is purely FFI codegen + lint cluster; "lifecycle" carried over from the one-shot migration era) and relocates `gen-rename-headers.py` into the cluster so all five codegen scripts (ffi_manifest hub + gen-executor-instances + gen-rename-headers + ffi-convert-to-scheme + check-ffi-wrap-template + check-non-io-side-effects) sit together; `gen-rename-headers.py`'s `REPO_ROOT = parents[1]` adjusts to `parents[2]` for the extra level; Makefile (5 hits) + CLAUDE.md + gotchas + tensor-lifecycle + design-decisions + backends/README path refs updated; `packages/backends/rename_{tape,torch,mlx}.h` regenerated with the new auto-generated comment line. Closing convention sweep + scripts/README.md ships in this commit: 3 `#!/bin/bash` stragglers normalized to `#!/usr/bin/env bash` (`dataset_mnist.sh` / `dataset_tinyshakespeare.sh` / `test_cuda_colab.sh`); `scripts/README.md` documents the directory map + the bash-for-orchestration / Python-for-meaning rule + the package boundary + output destinations; `set -euo pipefail` not retrofitted onto the `check-*-gate.sh` scripts that deliberately use `set -u` only (they invoke idris2 expecting compile failure as a negative test result and would break under pipefail). **First-principles framing**: bash invokes; Python means. Bash is the right glue for `make → binary → env-var → exit code`; the moment a script needs to *understand* data — parse JSON, classify thresholds, construct structured records — it should be Python, and the home for shared Python logic is a package, not a pile of `python3 -c` invocations. The `mltools` package is the foundation: perf_log (writer + reader), header_parser (backend C/C++ primitives), sweep_grid (grid expansion). Future shared concerns (e.g. cross-cutting manifest access for coverage-gap-probe) join as additional modules rather than spawning standalone helpers. **Verification**: `make check-rename-headers` clean (rename_*.h re-emitted with new auto-generated comment); `make check-executor-method-drift` green; `make test-integration-lint-ffi-wrap-template` "clean. 4 files, 530 FFI decls scanned"; `make gen-executor-instances --check` "no changes"; render-benchmarks + check-perf-regression byte-identical output pre/post; coverage-gap-probe.py emits same OP_* counts (133 MISSING tape=69 mlx=64) modulo the regex-bug correction (-5 over-captures + 6 under-captures the bash version had); `python3 scripts/sweep.py --task copy --skip-build` rejects missing exec correctly. Per-package `scripts/` directories (`packages/idris-transformers/scripts/` 21 files, `packages/idris-ml-examples/scripts/verify_dtypes.py`, `packages/pytorch/torch_ref/scripts/`) intentionally untouched: cohesive + in active use + package-local. The `ffi_manifest.py` decomposition by interface family is parked as a separate refactor with its own design questions (auto-derivation from `backend.h`? per-interface modules?). Original row deleted from TODO.md.


Round 2 of the `Executor/Core.idr` audit closed (2026-06-09). Eight commits land three new opt-in slices, two op promotions, a public-ABI cleanup, and explicit closure rationales for the candidates that don't move. Round 1 (2026-06-08, see entry below) had landed `UserExecutorOptimizations` and pre-registered `Streamed` / `MemoryHygiene` / `Diagnostics` in `scripts/check-executor-method-drift.py`'s `OPT_IN_SLICES` — Round 2 fills in the three slices the escape hatch was waiting for, plus mops up the remaining row-1 candidates. `02f46025` extracts `UserExecutorStreamed` and demotes `deviceStreamTag` out of `UserExecutorCore`; the new slice is a superclass of `UserExecutorTraining` + `UserExecutorInference`, so the five `Tensor.idr` call sites threading `(deviceStreamTag {ex})` into `dtCreate{1,2,3,4}d` + `primSetInitSeedStreamed` resolve unchanged. Tape/torch keep the `deviceStreamTag = 0` body; mlx keeps `streamTag s`. BYO backends without a stream concept can now omit the instance entirely. `8e8b701b` extracts `UserExecutorMemoryHygiene` and demotes `primEpochBegin/End` + `primReleaseAllPersistent` + `primResetForEval` out of mandatory `UserExecutorProfiling`. The four methods are asymmetric (mlx implements meaningfully, tape/torch keep cheap arena reset / `free_intermediates` / no-op bodies); the opt-in surface now clarifies that for BYO authors. `3f6f9de0` extracts `UserExecutorDiagnostics` and demotes `primLiveCount` + `primPeakLiveCount` + `primPerfOpCount`; `UserExecutorProfiling` is now down to its three genuine op-timing methods (`primProfileReset` / `primProfileReport` / `primPerfReset`). `0589e27a` does the public-ABI sub-cleanup: `primLiveCount` / `primPeakLiveCount` flip from `Int -> Int` to `PrimIO Int` — the `(int dummy)` constant-folding workaround was always papering over the real issue (FFI-result-depends-on-mutable-state should never have been typed pure). C-side `tensor_live_count(int)` / `tensor_peak_live_count(int)` lose the dummy arg; the four `Train.idr` call sites restructure into `liveH <- primIO (primLiveCount {ex})` before the `logInfo` string interpolation. `97a3d669` promotes `primCrossAttention` from mandatory `UserExecutorNN` into opt-in `UserExecutorOptimizations` — aligns its placement with `primSdpa2d` (its sibling fused multi-head attention kernel, in opt-in since Round 1); the two are NOT subsumable (different abstraction levels: tensor mask + scalar scale vs head dims + isCausal flag), so the audit closes "kept, aligned placement." `269b5d86` promotes `primTile2d` from mandatory `UserExecutorLinear` into `UserExecutorOptimizations` — the documented 2026-05-15 mlx perf regression on the reshape3d-based alternative shows it's a true fused fast-path, not a primitive; pattern-match with the other promoted fused kernels. `272d2392` adds a TODO-defer marker on the LSTM-cell pair-handle plumbing (`primLstmGatesPair` + `primPairFirst` + `primPairSecond`) — collapsing the three interface methods to one would require either a per-backend C entry-point with output-pointer semantics or a custom multi-return Scheme wrapper outside the manifest template; both larger than the audit's budget. Deferred to a future LSTM/recurrent-cell refactor that already needs to restructure the cell-state plumbing. `d03b2bad` closes `primOneHot` + `primCosineSimilarity` with kept-with-rationale comments on each method: the perf-gate the audit row contemplated returns "keep" by call-site arithmetic (one-hot pure-Idris would be N+1 FFI hops vs 1 fused per call on the LLM training hot path; cosine pure-Idris would be 8 lazy-graph nodes vs 1 in NTM's per-timestep loop under `withNoGrad` on mlx — MTLBuffer pressure compounds). Neither method is a fused multi-op kernel that fits `UserExecutorOptimizations`'s pattern; both are individual primitives in their natural homes. **RED before each commit**: each opt-in extraction had to place the new instance AFTER the `UserExecutorCore` instance in each backend (the superclass constraint requires Core first), surfaced on the first attempt at `02f46025` when a fresh `make BACKEND=tape install` failed with `Can't find an implementation for UserExecutorCore TapeExecutor` at the new `UserExecutorStreamed TapeExecutor where` site. Fixed by swapping the instance-block order. **Verification**: `make check-executor-method-drift` green at every commit; `make gen-executor-instances --check` clean after each manifest update; idris-ml installs clean on tape + torch + mlx after each commit; `make BACKEND=tape test` 10/10. **What Round 2 explicitly did not do**: the LSTM pair collapse (C) and the perf-measured pure-Idris replacements (D / E) — see the closure rationales above. No follow-up TODO row replaces row 33; the audit is closed.


`UserExecutorOptimizations` opt-in slice landed for fused inference + RL ops. Same shape as `HardwareClassed` — categorical opt-in surface with no defaults expressed via Core/Linear/NN primitives. Backends with native fused kernels declare an instance; backends without don't, and callers requiring a fused op carry a `UserExecutorOptimizations ex =>` constraint. The new slice is a superclass of both `UserExecutorTraining` and `UserExecutorInference`, so existing call sites using either aggregate resolve unchanged. Five commits, each gated by `make check-executor-method-drift` + `make BACKEND=tape install` + `make BACKEND=tape test`: `f9dfd1aa` adds an `OPT_IN_SLICES` escape hatch to `scripts/check-executor-method-drift.py` so slices populated with `Optimizations` / `Streamed` / `MemoryHygiene` / `Diagnostics` skip cross-backend union-checking; mandatory slices (`Core`, `Linear`, `NN`, `Conv`, `Autograd`, `ParamRegistry`, `Optimizer`, `Serialize`, `Profiling`, `TensorCreate`) continue to enforce all-three-backend presence. `5e53ebd1` introduces `UserExecutorOptimizations` and promotes `primSdpa2d` / `primRmsNorm2d` / `primSwiGlu2d` out of `UserExecutorNN`; `RmsNorm` and `SwiGLU` PyTorch-F64 reference tests stay at max-abs-diff 0.0 with the relocated declarations. `31326c37` promotes the 8 fused `primCreateParam{1,2,3,4}d{Normal,Const}Streamed` methods out of `UserExecutorTensorCreate`; the `UserExecutorOptimizations` backend instance had to relocate to after the param-init `%foreign` declarations (post-line-565 in tape) so the generator's `primCreateParam... = prim__createParam...Tape` lines don't forward-reference unbound names. Tape / mlx still abort at the FFI boundary on these methods — the audit relocates, doesn't fix the abort. `bc88c6a9` promotes `primPolyakBlend` out of `UserExecutorParamRegistry`. Drive-by: strip `(added 2026-MM-DD)` markers from comments in `Executor/Core.idr`, `Tensor.idr`, `HfBert.idr`, `ffi_manifest.py`, `gotchas.md` — per the rule that absolute dates / ephemeral plan labels don't belong in committed prose. `9a347a21` closes the audit with TODO-defer markers on the Profiling-side asymmetric methods (`deviceStreamTag`, `primEpochBegin/End`, `primReleaseAllPersistent`, `primResetForEval`, `primLiveCount/PeakLiveCount`, `primPerfOpCount`) — candidates for `UserExecutorStreamed` / `UserExecutorMemoryHygiene` / `UserExecutorDiagnostics` opt-in interfaces, blocked on a drift-gate change to recognise default-impl methods on mandatory slices (the OPT_IN_SLICES escape hatch covers slice-level opt-in, not per-method defaults). `primParamSaveByName{,Renamed}` similarly defers to the LoRA / PEFT work. **What the audit explicitly did not do**: the seven removal-candidates from the original TODO row (`primPairFirst/Second` LSTM-cell plumbing, `primLiveCount/Peak` `Int -> Int` workaround, `primOneHot`, `primCrossAttention` vs `primSdpa2d` duplicate, `primCosineSimilarity`, `primTile2d`) — that's a "round-2" audit with a different framing (per-method removal vs slice-level reorganization); the round-2 row replaces the original and tracks both clusters. RED before each promotion commit: the previous-commit's `OPT_IN_SLICES` escape hatch is the prerequisite (without it, the partially-implemented `UserExecutorOptimizations` instance would trip the drift gate); with both in place every relocation passes `make check-executor-method-drift` + `make BACKEND=tape install` + the relevant per-method unit test. **Verification**: `make check-executor-method-drift` green at every commit; `make gen-executor-instances --check` clean; idris-ml + idris-transformers install clean on tape; `make BACKEND=tape test` shows the RmsNorm + SwiGLU PyTorch-F64 reference tests still pass at max-abs-diff 0.0. The original TODO row is replaced by a "round 2" row carrying the seven unfinished candidates plus the new asymmetric-method cluster.


Per-epoch activation dumps via SafeTensors landed, gated by `IDRISML_LOG_LEVEL=trace` (2026-06-09). Closes the Medium "TensorBoard-style file sink for debugging" row. The row's original framing (new `tensor_save_npy` C primitive in all three backends + TensorBoard TFEvent target) was misaligned with the post-checkpoint-layer infrastructure: the registry-keyed SafeTensors writer already shipped 2026-05-21, and modern Python debug gravity is on `safetensors.numpy.load_file` + matplotlib, not TFEvent protobuf. Five commits land the feature: `5156ef51` adds the Idris-side `getLogLevel : IO Int` FFI binding (`Util/Log.idr`) — first consumer of the cached `idrisml_log_resolve_level` reader the C side already implemented — plus `levelSilent` / `levelError` / … / `levelTrace : Int` exported constants so call sites compare via `>= levelTrace` instead of magic numbers. `f71c68f9` is a small `scripts/lifecycle/ffi_manifest.py` cleanup: drops the stale `mnist_get_image` Entry + INIT_FFI listing (removed in source by `aa371d9f` but the manifest still had them — running `gen-executor-instances` would have re-added a broken typeclass assignment), drops the stale `tensor_create → primCreate` mapping (same drift class, surfaced by `36194c07`), and adds the missing `param_save_by_name` + `param_save_by_name_renamed` entries that lived in source as hand-written FFI bindings but were never in the manifest. Source diff: zero — the executor instance files already match the cleaned manifest's generator output. `b21f7792` adds `param_erase_by_prefix(const char* prefix)` to the shared `param_registry.c`: drop every registered param whose paramId starts with `prefix`, releasing each handle's retain via the existing `tensor_release_handle`. Empty prefix is a defensive no-op (would otherwise erase everything). Plumbed through `backend.h`, the three `rename_<b>.h` headers (auto-regen via `make rename-headers`), the `UserExecutorParamRegistry` interface method, and the three per-backend `prim__paramEraseByPrefix<X>` FFI bindings. Three new Criterion tests cover the erase semantics (`erase_by_prefix_drops_matching`, `_no_match_is_noop`, `_empty_prefix_is_noop`); `make test-unit-c-tape` 232/232 → 235/235. `7bcf1237` expands `forwardVarTraced` in `packages/idris-ml/src/Layer/Core.idr` from a single-mode stderr tracer into a three-branch log-level-gated walker: `< DEBUG` is a pass-through to `forwardVar` (the only overhead is one cached-int FFI call), `DEBUG` keeps the existing per-layer min/max/mean/NaN summary but routes it through `logDebug` (closes the gap where `logDebug` / `logTrace` had no live callers), `TRACE` adds the activation dump — each per-layer output is registered under a synthetic paramId `__act/<label>/<i>`, flushed via `primParamSaveByName` to `${IDRISML_ACTIVATION_DIR:-./activations}/<label>-<seq>.safetensors`, then erased via `primParamEraseByPrefix` BEFORE the forward returns so the optimizer's full-registry walk never sees the synthetic entries. Per-`label` sequence counter lives in a Chez `top-level-value` slot keyed `idrisml-act-seq` (dodges the Idris-Chez "zero-arg %noinline def is CSE'd to a constant" gotcha by taking a dummy `Int` arg; the body's argument-list arity matches Idris codegen's `(lambda (dummy) …)` call shape — the wrong shape caused a runtime "incorrect argument count" exception on first attempt, fixed in commit `c965038c` below). `IDRISML_ACTIVATION_EVERY_N` env var (default 1) throttles file generation. Label sanitisation replaces non-`[A-Za-z0-9_-]` chars with `_` so user-supplied labels are safe path segments. The TRACE branch requires the dylib built with `IDRISML_LOG=trace make backend install` because the build ceiling (`backends/log.c:30`) clamps the runtime cap — a default INFO build can't be flipped to TRACE at runtime no matter what env var is set. Test.ActivationDump exercises the load-bearing register → save → erase round-trip directly (mechanics independent of log level). `c965038c` wires the eval-loop call site in `packages/idris-ml-examples/src/Example/Mnist.idr` (`forwardVar` → `forwardVarTraced "mnist_eval"`), fixes the Scheme FFI dummy-arg issue surfaced above, and adds a `docs/develop/perf-changes.md` entry recording the zero-INFO-overhead property + the expected TRACE per-dump cost. Verified end-to-end on tape: INFO-level 5-epoch MNIST converges to 97.6% accuracy in 56s (matches pre-change baseline within VM noise); TRACE with `EVERY_N=200` and a `IDRISML_LOG=trace` dylib produces 6 `mnist_eval-<seq>.safetensors` files in `/tmp/idrisml-mnist-act/`, each ~200KB containing 8 keys matching the network layers (Conv2d, ReLU, MaxPool x2, Conv2d, ReLU, MaxPool, Dropout, Linear), loadable via `from safetensors.numpy import load_file`. Min/max values in the SafeTensors files match the stderr DEBUG summary lines exactly, confirming the dump-vs-summary consistency. `__act/` is now a reserved paramId prefix (documented in `docs/develop/gotchas.md` "Architecture & Infrastructure" alongside the env vars + a note about the build-ceiling clamp). Optional follow-up parked: a Jupyter notebook walking through "train mnist at TRACE → load via safetensors.numpy → plot per-layer histograms" — not required for the feature to land; covered by the broader user-docs row.

MNIST primitives out of the core executor interface + dead `primCreate` removed + rejected CUDA `Fin` lift documented (2026-06-09). Closes both the Medium "Reconsider MNIST primitives" and Medium "Revisit CUDA `Fin` vs `Nat` in `TCuda`" rows. Five commits: `033fdef6` introduces `packages/backends/idx.c` + `idx.h` (compiled once into `libidrisml.dylib`, not per-backend, no rename header) with `idx_load` / `idx_count` / `idx_label_at` / `idx_image_doubles` / `idx_free` — generic IDX-format dataset loaders, lifted from the old MNIST helpers but renamed to match the file format rather than the dataset. `aa371d9f` swaps the Idris side over: drops `primMnistGetImage` from `UserExecutorTensorCreate` (`Executor/Core.idr`), drops the three per-backend `prim__mnistGetImage<X>` Scheme bindings + their typeclass assignments (`Executor/{Tape,Torch,Mlx}.idr`), renames the file-IO FFI bindings to `prim__idxLoad` / `prim__idxCount` / `prim__idxLabel`, adds a new `prim__idxImageDoubles` + an `idxImage` Idris convenience helper that constructs the flat 1-D image tensor via `dtCreate1d` (the intermediate `[1, 28, 28]` shape that the old `mnist_get_image` produced was always immediately reshape-flattened at the call site, so the 1-D shape saves an FFI hop without changing the user-visible data flow), and updates `Example/Mnist.idr`'s five call sites mechanically. `6ff08cb9` deletes the now-dead C surface: `packages/backends/mnist.c`, the MNIST block in `shared_utils.{c,h}` (`mnist_load` / `mnist_count` / `mnist_get_label` / `read_be32` static helper / `MnistDataset` struct), the `/* ---------- MNIST data loading ---------- */` block in `backend.h`, the `mnist_get_image` + `mnist_free` `#define` lines in `rename_{tape,torch,mlx}.h`, the `mnist_load` / `mnist_count` / `mnist_get_label` EXCLUDE entries in `gen-rename-headers.py`, the `$(BUILD)/mnist_$(PRIMARY).o` entry + build rule in `Makefile`, and the README's `mnist.c` file entry. `36194c07` drops the dead non-streamed `primCreate : AnyPtr -> AnyPtr -> Int -> Int -> AnyPtr` from `UserExecutorCore` (a repo-wide grep across `idris-ml/src` + `idris-ml-examples/src` + `idris-transformers/src` + `idris-gym/src` found zero callers — every live creation path goes through `dtCreate*` over the streamed family); removes the per-backend `prim__createTape` + `prim__createMlxStreamed` (the rank-aware mlx-streamed-tag variant, NOT the dtype-streamed family) + `prim__createBYO` FFI bindings + typeclass assignments. `prim__createTorch` stays alive — `prim__createFromHostTorch` in `UserExecutorTransfer` still uses it; only the typeclass method that drove it from `UserExecutorCore` was dead. `bf27527c` records the rejected `TCuda : Fin n -> TorchHwDev` lift in `docs/develop/design-decisions.md` next to the existing `Discrete actions: \`Nat\`, not \`Fin n\`` entry: `n` (the host's GPU count) is a runtime fact `HwConfig.idr`'s build-time generator can't reach without coupling `BUILD_KEY` to host hardware; the runtime EAFP gate (`attemptOn` / `availableDevices builtinDevices`) is the right layer for a fact whose truth value changes between processes on the same binary. **Required-bar**: the user explicitly OK'd the IDX C-side helpers as an "optional extension" provided they sit outside the core backend interface. The bar is met: zero MNIST/IDX declarations in `backend.h`, zero per-backend MNIST compilation, `idx.c` compiled once like `shared_utils.c`. **Hot-path note**: `idx_image_doubles` allocates-and-copies (caller-owned `double*` consumed by the streamed creator path which `free()`s its input — convention enforced uniformly across tape's `tensor_create_1d_f64` + torch's `tensor_create_1d_f64_torch` + mlx's equivalent). A borrowed-pointer first attempt caused an Abort trap 6 on the first training step (the streamed creator freed a slice of the dataset buffer); the alloc-and-copy fix is documented in `idx.h`. **Followups opened**: a single Medium TODO row "Audit `Executor/Core.idr` for dubious interface entries" bundles seven remaining candidates (LSTM pair plumbing, `primPolyakBlend`, `primLiveCount/PeakLiveCount` `Int -> Int` workaround, `primOneHot`, `primCrossAttention` vs `primSdpa2d` duplicate, `primCosineSimilarity`, `primTile2d`) so the perf gate is one cross-cutting snapshot instead of piecemeal regression hunts. Verification: tape `example-mnist` 97.6% accuracy (matches pre-change), torch `example-mnist` 97.9-98.4%, multi-link `BACKEND=tape,torch,mlx backend` links clean, `test-unit-c-tape` 232/232, `test-integration-lint-rename-headers` green. Both original rows deleted from TODO.md.


idris-transformers adapter package DRY-up landed (2026-06-08). Closes the Medium "DRY-up the `idris-transformers` adapter package" row. Five incremental commits, each gated by `test-unit-idris-transformers` (paramCount + name-catalogue + FFI-registry + forward-pass smoke): `507bf0bf` extracts `HfCommon.decoderBlockPreNorm` — the `norm → attn → residual → norm → mlp → residual` skeleton common to `HfLlama.applyBlock` + `HfBitNet.applyBlock`; both reduce to a 6-line closure-passing call (per-arch attention's BitNet `attn_sub_norm` + `Quant` constraint stays inside the closure). `62430d22` extracts `HfCommon.projectTiedLmHead` — the zero-bias `tlinear2d embedWeight hFinal` expansion used by Llama (forward + cached step), BitNet (forward), and Gpt2 (forward) collapses from 5 lines × 4 sites to 1 line × 4 sites; Bert's MlmHead stays on its own path because it has a real learnable bias. `b75531c5` extracts `HfCommon.forBlocks n mk = mk 0 ++ … ++ mk (n-1)` — the per-block fan-out that was either `concatMap mkBlock (rangeNat numLayers)` with a `rangeNat` helper duplicated verbatim in Llama/Gpt2/BitNet, or a hand-written recursive accumulator in Bert; all four `hfXxxParamNames` now use the same one-line combinator. Per-component string lists stay per-adapter (load-bearing HF on-disk contract per `CONVENTIONS.md` rule 2). `3cfc181b` extracts `Test.HfCommon.assertHfModelParamCount` — the 5-line `testParamCount` body was identical-shape across all four adapter tests; the helper takes `(label, names, expected)` and reproduces the PASS-message format exactly so test output is bit-identical. Lives in `idris-transformers-tests` ipkg so the runtime library carries no test-package coupling. `27926afd` drops the inline `RopeBase = 500000.0` / `RmsNormEps = 1e-5` constants from `Example.HfLlamaInference` + `Example.HfBitNetInference` — both `LlamaConfig` and `BitNetConfig` already carry these as fields; the examples now reference `llama32_1B_Config.ropeBase` / `bitnet2B4T_Config.rmsNormEps` etc. Also adds `HfLlama.llama32_1B_RopeScaling` (alias for `Layer.RoPE.llama3Scaling`) so the per-arch RoPE scaling parameter is co-located with the per-arch config — mirrors `HfBitNet.bitnetRopeScaling`. `MaxPos` stays per-example: it's at type position in `RoPETables MaxPos HeadDim ...` and both examples deliberately clamp it well below the model's `maxPosition` to keep cos/sin tables small. Skipped from the original 8 candidates: (a) RMSNorm + (h) inference example scaffolding already shipped earlier (`applyRmsNorm2dRaw` on 2026-06-03 and `Example.Common.HfInferenceHelper` respectively); (c) GQA attention plumbing left per-adapter because of per-arch divergence (BitNet's post-SDPA `attn_sub_norm` before `o_proj`) — revisit only if a third Llama-shaped arch appears. Verification gates: `make test-unit-idris-transformers` green at every commit; `test-integration-lint-hf-llama-inference` + ad-hoc lint of `HfBitNetInference` green at the final commit. Original row deleted from TODO.md.


idris-gym Env.reset now threads a Seed + randomizes per Gymnasium (2026-06-08). Closes the Medium "Thread a seedable Rng through `idris-gym` `Env.reset`" row. Three commits: `cc770d2e` (library refactor + env bodies + tests + caller updates), `09e5dbe8` (paired torch_ref pin removal), `e7cbbb27` (reference-alignment doc section). `Gym.Env.reset` changes from a pure value `state` to `Seed -> (state, Seed)`, mirroring Gymnasium's `env.reset(seed=...)` contract — caller-side PRNG flows through every reset and is advanced on return so per-episode resets diverge naturally. Per-env distributions match Gymnasium verbatim: CartPole `U(-0.05, 0.05)^4`, MountainCar `pos ~ U(-0.6, -0.4) × vel=0`, MountainCarContinuous same as MountainCar, Pendulum `θ ~ U(-π, π) × θ̇ ~ U(-1, 1)`, Acrobot `U(-0.1, 0.1)^4`; Taxi randomizes taxi/passenger/destination with `dest != pass` enforced; FrozenLake + Blackjack seed their internal stochastic-step RNGs from the input Seed; CliffWalking deterministic and pass-through. `Gym.Vector.resetAll : Seed -> (VecEnv n state, Seed)` folds the seed through n sub-resets; `stepAutoReset` advances it once per terminated env. Example call sites pull a per-call Seed from `Compat.Random.randomInt32` (already seeded via `srand cfg.seed` at startup) and pass it into `resetAll` — no IORef plumbing or signature churn beyond the per-`main` line. Wrappers (`TimeLimit`, `Record`, `Normalize`, `Action`) unchanged — they accept pre-built inner state and don't call reset themselves. Tests updated end-to-end: old `reset is zero` style asserts become range checks ("within Gymnasium U(-0.05, 0.05)"), plus new determinism asserts (same seed → same state) and randomization asserts (different seeds → different state). Paired-side: `torch_ref/models/{reinforce,sac,mountain_car,mountain_car_cont,ppo}.py` no longer set `env.unwrapped.state = ...` after reset; the `reset_to_*` / `_reset_to_pi` helpers stay (call-site stability) but now just return the natural Gymnasium reset obs. Auto-reset branches that overrode `next_obs[i]` with hardcoded pinned values are dropped — SyncVectorEnv auto-reset already produces the randomized obs in next_obs. `torch_ref/models/taxi.py` intentionally unchanged — the matching `Example.Taxi` calls `Gym.ToyText.Taxi.defaultStart` directly (not via Env.reset), so the pin stays to keep Q-table reproducibility paired. Smoke gate: `reinforce`/tape (10 epochs, avg_return=94/200), `a2c`/tape (50 epochs, avg_return=200/200) — both run end-to-end on the new path. Full-sweep multi-seed convergence available via `scripts/sweep.sh` if needed; not run in this landing since smoke + bit-identical tests cover the implementation correctness. Also fixes a pre-existing `Test.Reinforce` compile failure from commit `a6e74996` (wraps `Vect n CPState` in `MkVecEnv` to match the post-refactor `rolloutEpBatched` signature). RED captured in `cc770d2e` body: deterministic `MkCP 0 0 0 0` returned by the old `reset`; new tests asserting randomization within `U(-0.05, 0.05)^4` + different states across seeds 0/1 would have failed against the old impl. Original row deleted from TODO.md.


mlx-gpu BF16 vs F32 HfLlama-1B inference wall gap closed by re-measurement (2026-06-08). Closes the Medium "Localize mlx-gpu BF16 vs F32 wall gap on HfLlama-1B inference (was: audit mlx fused-op constant pool)" row. The +62% BF16-slower-than-F32 measurement at `6bf2ca8+dirty` (2026-05-31) is gone at `e658faa9` — warm-tree pure-decode walls are F32 ~48s vs BF16 ~43s, well inside the ±15-20% VM-noise band (`feedback_vm_perf_noise`). Four runs taken: F32 cold 29m 54s (partially-cold mdtF32 tree, post off-policy HwConfig flip); F32 warm 1m 10s; BF16 cold 25m 16s (fresh mdtBF16, full mlx C++ recompile + ttc); BF16 warm 49.9s. The cold-vs-warm gap is dominated by Idris ttc-cache regen + mlx C++ recompile (~100 source files); the per-op generation work itself is now BF16 ≈ F32 within noise. Plausible attribution: the 2026-05-30..2026-06-03 fused-op work — SDPA (`6850366`), RMSNorm (`416c011`), SwiGLU (`24517c8`), embedding (`ca6f9ab`), and `c09d374`'s all-heads RoPE that cut op count 18,410 → 2,634/step (-86%) — cut per-op overhead enough that the BF16-vs-F32 per-kernel ratio is no longer the wall-clock-dominating factor. Phase 2b (per-op instrumentation) and 2c (upstream mlx ticket) skipped; the trigger condition (+60%+ persists) doesn't fire. **Token-sequence note**: BF16 diverges from the F32 oracle at position 6 (`"a"` vs `"Paris"` — argmax flip at a close-runner-up logit), as expected from BF16's 7-bit mantissa vs F32's 23-bit. The model isn't broken in BF16; the roundtrip test compares against the F32-frozen oracle and exits 2. F32 stays the right choice for workloads needing deterministic token-by-token parity; BF16 is now a viable equal-speed alternative for those tolerant of slight stochasticity. Full motivation + impact table + perf-log cross-refs in `docs/develop/perf-changes.md` "2026-06-08 — mlx-gpu BF16 vs F32 HfLlama-1B inference gap re-measured → closed". Original row deleted from TODO.md.


Batched policy forward ported to all remaining RL examples (2026-06-08). Closes the Medium "Port batched policy forward to remaining RL examples" row. Six examples ported in sequence with paired Idris + PyTorch updates: **A2c** (`aff7eb72`, CartPole, 5/5 seeds converge on both sides to avg_return=200), **Ppo** (`3874d917`, Acrobot, mean ~-82 across 5 seeds on both sides), **Dqn** (`bfe1520e`, CartPole, 3/5 on Idris and 4/5 on PyTorch ≥ solve threshold), **MountainCar** (`9bf21134`, MountainCar-v0, both sides converge to avg_return ~-104), **MountainCarCont** (`e05fd23c`, MountainCarContinuous-v0, both sides converge to avg_return ~96 near goal +100), **Sac** (`e05fd23c`, Pendulum, Idris converges to avg_return=-362). All six share the same VecEnv pattern: `NumEnvs=4` parallel envs in lockstep, one batched (actor[/critic]) forward per outer step, per-env auto-reset, per-env episodic return tracking. On-policy ports (A2c/Ppo) preserve total-sample budget per update by setting per-env RolloutLen = pre-batched RolloutLen / NumEnvs. Off-policy ports (Dqn/MountainCar/MCC/Sac) carry the replay-ratio dynamics shift (1 update per outer step → 1 update per 4 env-steps): this lifts convergence on small workloads (Dqn at seed=42: 124 → 187) by giving the agent 4× more environment experience per gradient update. **Latent pre-existing bug fixed in the same landing**: both `Example.Sac` and `Example.MountainCarCont`'s `runBatchUpdate` had `_ <- pure (nativeTrainStep opt loss)` — `pure` wraps the IO action but never executes it, so Q1/Q2/actor gradient steps NEVER FIRED in either example since they were written. All perf-log entries prior to `e05fd23c` for these two examples have `null` avg_return because of this bug. Convergence now works on both sides; one residual gap on Pendulum-SAC PyTorch (avg_return=-1168 vs Idris -362) is a divergence to investigate, not a port-correctness issue (the same code path converges on Idris). Six commits' worth of perf-changes entries cover per-example characterisation + 5-seed sweeps where the wall budget allowed. Original row deleted from TODO.md.


Tape optimizer AdamW foreach landed (2026-06-08). Closes the Medium "Tape optimizer foreach fast path" row. Two commits: `4da11736` adds `adamw_foreach_param` as an opt-in fast path for F64 AdamW params (BLAS-1 `cblas_dscal` + `cblas_daxpy` for the m-update on params with numel ≥ 256; v-update + bias-correct + weight update + decoupled weight decay stay scalar but operate on direct double pointers, no per-element dtype branch), plus paired Criterion test `training_optimizer_adamw_foreach::matches_scalar_on_256_elem_param` asserting |scalar - foreach| < 1e-12 over 50 AdamW steps. RED captured before the impl commit by swapping β1↔β2 in the m-update: max_diff=8.928118e-01 at idx=2, scalar=-0.598228298058 vs foreach=-1.49104014296. The follow-up commit (this one) collapses to default-foreach with `TAPE_OPTIMIZER_FOREACH=0` as a debug/test opt-out, after measurement on bert-mlm-finetune confirmed a **~43% wall reduction** (warm scalar 11.107s/10.939s vs foreach 6.486s/6.155s across two pairs, both runs converging to bit-identical loss=2.9052). Smaller AdamW workloads (gpt, VocabSize=65) saw no measurable wall delta — the optimizer is below the noise floor on tiny models. SGD/RMSprop/Adam paths unchanged; F32-tagged AdamW params still go through the scalar inner switch case 3 (mixed-dtype foreach out of scope; the BLAS overhead doesn't justify a widen-narrow staging pass at F32). Full motivation + impact table + perf-log cross-refs in `docs/develop/perf-changes.md` "2026-06-08 — Tape AdamW foreach". Original row deleted from TODO.md.


Debug logging build-flag row closed as completed-and-evolved (2026-06-08). Closes the Medium "Gate debug/observability logging behind a build flag" row. The row called for a binary `IDRISML_DEBUG_LOGGING` gate excluding RSS + handle-count logging at compile time. What shipped is more general: the 5-level `IDRISML_LOG` scheme (`silent` / `error` / `warn` / `info` / `debug` / `trace`) landed in commits `d31f2595` (2026-06-06, C-side infrastructure) and `22367313` (2026-06-07, Idris-side `logInfo` wiring). `runTrainingIO`'s `logEpoch` at `packages/idris-ml/src/Train.idr:313-317` builds the `memSuffix` (`peak=`/`cur=`/`handles=`/`peakhandles=`) unconditionally via `getRssMB` / `getCurrentRssMB` / `primLiveCount` / `primPeakLiveCount`, then routes the full log line through `logInfo`. The level gate happens at the `logInfo` call: `make IDRISML_LOG=warn` (or higher-severity ceiling) ships without per-epoch summaries — achieving the row's production-build goal via runtime suppression instead of compile-time exclusion. The deferred bit: C-side bodies (`get_rss_mb`, `tensor_live_count`, `tensor_peak_live_count` in `shared_utils.c` / `backend_mlx/core/lifecycle/lifecycle_core.cpp` / `backend_tape/training/shims.c`) still always compile — no `#ifdef IDRISML_DEBUG_LOGGING` guard. The cost is a few cheap counters and is below measurement threshold; revisit only if profiling surfaces the counter cost. Per-Tensor `g_*_peak_live` update in backend ctors stays unconditional for the same reason. The row's "exclude code at compile time, not just skip at runtime" framing is the more aggressive variant; the runtime-gated variant covers the practical production-build need at lower complexity. Original row deleted from TODO.md.


Build / coverage output noise row closed as decision-no-action (2026-06-08). Closes the Low "Make build / coverage output less noisy (think about it properly)" row. The 2026-05-26 conclusion captured in the row body — "verbose IS the make convention, not a defect" — stands; no workflow has surfaced since to argue for kernel-style suppression. The asymmetric pain point cited (`coverage-backend-torch` dumping `llvm-cov report` on top of an already-noisy compile + link) hasn't recurred either. The row was kept as a "think about it properly" placeholder, but ~2 weeks of normal use without complaint is a stronger no-action signal than the row itself. Closed deliberately rather than left open; reverse-decision trigger captured here: revive if (a) a CI lane needs cleaner logs to be diff-able for failure triage, or (b) an interactive user reports the verbose default makes a workflow painful enough to switch tooling. Until either, the row's own "don't reach for this until a real workflow tells you which mode wins" advice closes the question. Original row deleted from TODO.md.


`*_ARGS` mechanical lookup row closed (2026-06-08). Closes the Low "Unify `*_ARGS` convention for example targets" row, shipped in commit `cfa30e59` (2026-06-08, this session). The hardcoded `case "$$e" in example-foo) extra_args="FOO_ARGS=…"` mapping in the `test-e2e-examples` recipe (Makefile ~2562-2598) was replaced with bash indirect expansion: `args_var=$$(echo "$${e#example-}" | tr 'a-z-' 'A-Z_')_ARGS`. The recipe asserts `grep -qF "\$$($$args_var)"` against the Makefile so a missing per-example recipe slot fails loud rather than silently falling back to defaults — the silent-fallback bug class the original row called out. The single non-canonical outlier (`NTM_RECALL_ARGS` for `example-ntm-associative-recall`) was renamed to `NTM_ASSOCIATIVE_RECALL_ARGS` in the recipe at `Makefile:1874` and in three perf scripts (`scripts/perf-run.sh:47`, `scripts/perf-baseline.sh:59`, `scripts/perf-sweep.sh:65`) so the mechanical derivation works without an alias-map override. Convention is now `<EXAMPLE_NAME_UPPERCASED_WITH_UNDERSCORES>_ARGS` derived from `example-<name>` mechanically — new examples need only define the recipe slot and `test-e2e-examples` picks it up automatically. Original row deleted from TODO.md.


macOS CBLAS deprecation row closed as already-shipped (2026-06-08). Closes the Low "Investigate macOS CBLAS deprecation warnings" row. `Makefile:298` carries `tape_CFLAGS := -DACCELERATE_NEW_LAPACK` with the explanatory comment block at 296-297 ("ACCELERATE_NEW_LAPACK is a compile-time #define (gates BLAS API version); the framework flag is link-time."). The flag is applied unconditionally — safe on Linux/OpenBLAS where the define is a no-op (the symbol guards code only Accelerate exposes). All 29 `cblas_dgemm`/`cblas_dgemv` call sites across `backend_tape/linear/linalg/{mm,mv,linear,linear_2d,bmm,bmm_3x3}.c` + `backend_tape/conv/conv2d_batched.c` compile through the new-LAPACK API. Other backends (torch, mlx) don't directly use CBLAS — no transitive concern. Original row deleted from TODO.md.


GRU standard reset-gate row closed as already-shipped (2026-06-08). Closes the Medium "GRU C kernel: implement standard reset gate" row. Investigation found all three C kernels and the PyTorch reference were already on the standard form `n = tanh(ih_n + r * hh_n)`; the row's claim that "both sides agree on the simplified variant" was stale (likely never updated after the 2026-05-09 alignment work). Verified directly at this commit: `backend_tape/nn/recurrent/gru_cell.c:65` (`nG[i] = tanh(tape_load_d(ih, 2*o+i) + rG[i] * tape_load_d(hh, 2*o+i))`); `backend_torch/nn/recurrent/gru_cell.cpp:16` (`torch::tanh(ih.slice(0, 2*o, 3*o) + r * hh.slice(0, 2*o, 3*o))`); `backend_mlx/nn/recurrent/gru_cell.cpp:27` (`mx::tanh(mx::add(ih_n, mx::multiply(r_gate, hh_n)))`); `packages/pytorch/torch_ref/models/rnn.py:154` (`n = torch.tanh(ih[2*o:3*o] + r * hh[2*o:3*o])`). `Layer/Gru.idr:50` routes through `tgruCell` and never re-implements the gate math itself. No code change required; row stays closed unless future divergence is detected. Original row deleted from TODO.md.


HF Llama model id swapped from `meta-llama/Llama-3.2-1B` to `unsloth/Llama-3.2-1B` (2026-06-08). Closes the "HF auth for `hf_llama_inference.py` paired ref" row. `unsloth/Llama-3.2-1B` is a public mirror of Meta's BF16 weights — same safetensors, no license-accept, no `HF_TOKEN` required. Config-shape verification before swap (`curl -sL https://huggingface.co/unsloth/Llama-3.2-1B/resolve/main/config.json`): `hidden_size=2048`, `num_hidden_layers=16`, `num_attention_heads=32`, `num_key_value_heads=8` (GQA 4:1), `vocab_size=128256`, `intermediate_size=8192`, `max_position_embeddings=131072`, `rms_norm_eps=1e-5`, `rope_theta=500000.0`, `model_type=llama`, `torch_dtype=bfloat16` — identical to the meta-llama config the codebase was built against. Code touches: `packages/idris-ml-examples/src/Example/HfLlamaInference.idr` (ModelRepo constant), `packages/pytorch/torch_ref/scripts/hf_llama_inference.py` (MODEL_ID), `packages/idris-transformers/scripts/save_oracle_llama{,_generate}.py` (MODEL_LOCAL + MODEL_ID), `Makefile` (3 config.json dependency paths, 2 comment refs). CI gate change: the two `test-e2e-hf-llama-{,generate-}roundtrip` workflow steps in `.github/workflows/test.yml` previously skipped when `env.HF_TOKEN == ''` (forks without the token saw the step skip cleanly); both now run unconditionally on ubuntu — `unsloth` is public, so no skip path is needed. Specfile + generated yml regenerated via `python3 scripts/gen-ci-workflow.py`. Docs updated: `docs/develop/llm-inference.md`, `docs/users/idris-transformers.md`, `packages/idris-transformers/README.md`, `packages/idris-transformers/src/HfLlama.idr`, `packages/idris-transformers/src/Test/HfLlama.idr`, `packages/idris-ml-examples/src/Example/HfLlamaInference.idr` (header docstrings). Cross-references in the form "mirror of `meta-llama/Llama-3.2-1B`" preserved so the lineage stays discoverable. Existing on-disk cache directory `models/meta-llama/Llama-3.2-1B/` becomes orphaned (~3 GB); first run after the swap repopulates `models/unsloth/Llama-3.2-1B/`. The `Makefile:1654` HF_TOKEN gate keyed on `^meta-llama/` stays in place for any future gated meta-llama model that might land. Original row deleted from TODO.md.


Idris-2 JIT / JAX backend investigation closed (2026-06-08). Closes the "Investigate Idris-2 JIT / JAX backend (separate question marks)" research row. Deliverable: `docs/develop/idris-jit-jax-investigation.md` with two sections (one per question), each backed by quoted Chez / Idris-2 / OpenXLA docs + measured numbers from `perf-changes.md` 2026-05-14 / 2026-05-27 / 2026-06-03 + commit hashes. **Q1 verdict (Idris-2 JIT): no.** Chez Scheme has no dynamic JIT / tier-up mechanism (confirmed via Chez User's Guide §6/§11 and OS-level community references); Idris-2's Chez codegen already uses the maximum static knob — `(parameterize ([optimize-level 3] [compile-file-message #f]) (compile-program ...))` per `idris2-0.8.0/src/Compiler/Scheme/Chez.idr:525-529`, exactly matching the `compileChez` script written into this codebase's build tree. Chez compiles to native machine code (not bytecode), so the "switch to native code" lever is already pulled. Other Chez compile knobs (`cp0-effort-limit`, `cp0-score-limit`, `cp0-outer-unroll-limit`) tune inlining aggressiveness with defaults set by Chez authors; raising them trades compile time for runtime with no public report of a measurable Scheme-side win. The dominant 7.6 ms/op cost is in Idris-emitted Scheme between FFI calls (existential `AnyLayer` dispatch, typeclass dictionary resolution, `Tensor` record packing, `Vect` shape arithmetic, GC pressure) — not in Chez's interpreter overhead. Already cataloged on the sibling "Idris-side per-op overhead — partial" row (TODO.md), which is the right home for that work. The one Chez-side leak that did exist (per-call `S_foreign_entry → dlsym`) was closed at commit `2385e3f` (2026-05-27, "perf(ffi): cache foreign-procedure per-FFI, eliminating per-call dlsym"). **Q2 verdict (JAX/XLA backend): no.** JAX itself is Python-first; there is no embeddable "JAX C++ API". The stable C++ integration surface is **PJRT** (plugin ABI) + **StableHLO** (the IR JAX emits, designed for backward compatibility) + **XLA FFI** (custom-call API). XLA FFI is explicitly experimental — "the custom-call API/ABI uses PJRT-style versioning, however at this point it is still experimental and can be broken at any time" (OpenXLA docs). The compile-the-whole-step win JAX delivers (trace once, replay as fused kernel) is exactly what `mx::compile()` does on Apple Silicon (per ml-explore.github.io + deepwiki); `mx::compile()` integration is already a tracked follow-up in the mlx project memory + mlx-survey doc — same value proposition with zero new instance code vs a 4th backend's ~3-8 KLOC C/C++ + ~1.5 KLOC Idris (per the 22-method `UserExecutorCore` + 10 sister interfaces). JAX/XLA's unique advantage is TPU coverage; idris-ml's roadmap does not include TPU. Flip-criteria recorded in the doc: (a) idris-ml acquires a "we need TPU" goal — at which point the right framing is "PJRT-client backend emitting StableHLO", not "JAX backend"; (b) `mx::compile()` lands and underdelivers vs XLA on identical Apple Silicon hardware; (c) XLA FFI stabilises AND a non-Python framework maintains a PJRT client we could template off (PyTorch/XLA is the obvious candidate). Original "Investigate Idris-2 JIT / JAX backend" row deleted from TODO.md.


LoRA / parameter-efficient fine-tuning for HF-loaded BERT landed (2026-06-07). Closes the "LoRA / parameter-efficient fine-tuning" row added at FT4. Five shipped commits. **(L1)** `fbe348c2` `feat(layer): LoraLinear low-rank adapter wrapper` — new `Layer/LoraLinear.idr` with `LoraLinearState i o ex dt g` wrapping a base `LinearState` + `A : [r, i]` (Gaussian init, std = 1/sqrt(r) per peft) + `B : [o, r]` (zero init). Forward computes `W·x + b + (α/r) · B · (A · x)` via `tlinear` + two `tmv` hops + `tmulScalar` + `tadd`. B's zero init makes the t=0 LoRA delta identically zero — wrapped layer bit-matches the bare base at construction. Paired `Test.LoraLinear` with two assertions: `testInitZeroEquivalence` (max-abs-diff 0.0 across tape/torch/mlx-cpu) and `testNonZeroDelta` (manually constructed B with a single 1.0 entry produces the analytic delta `[0, 2.0, 0]` for the chosen A and x). **(L2)** `a87807b7` `feat(train, checkpoint): suffix freeze + saveModelMatching` — adds `freezeBySuffix` / `unfreezeBySuffix` to `Train/Freeze.idr` (mirror of `byPrefix`; needed because LoRA adapters live under the same `bert.*` prefix as backbone weights). Adds `saveModelMatching path predicate` to `Checkpoint.idr` for filtered checkpoint writes; policy in Idris (registry walk + predicate filter) + mechanism in new C-side `param_save_by_name(path, names_nl, count)` FFI. Extends `param_save_core` to optionally take an indices array; existing `param_save` becomes a thin wrapper. Paired `Test.SaveModelMatching` uses CheckpointSubset's round-trip pattern: save matching params, re-register with 99 sentinels, loadModel + verify which got restored. 4/4 assertions on tape/torch/mlx-cpu. **(L3)** `d7618708` `feat(hfbert): loraInjectBert + hfBertForwardWithLora` — new `packages/idris-transformers/src/HfBertLora.idr` with `LoraAdapter (i, o, r)` and `BertLoraAdapters numLayers hidden r` records (per-layer Q + V slots, shared rank + alpha). `loraInjectBert pfx numLayers rank alpha` constructs adapter slots under HF-aligned paramIds (`bert.encoder.layer.{i}.attention.self.{query,value}.lora_{A,B}`, peft's canonical `target_modules=["query","value"]` default). `hfBertForwardWithLora` parallels `hfBertForward` with a `Maybe (BertLoraAdapters ...)` arg: `Nothing` routes through the unchanged base forward; `Just ads` composes per-layer LoRA deltas into Q + V outputs via `primMm + primTranspose2d + primMulScalar + tadd` (single ioRerun block, no zero-bias tensor needed). `hfBertSeqClassifyForwardWithLora` wraps the classifier head identically. Exports surface for HfBert helpers extended: `applyLN2d`, `oneHeadCtx`, `buildHeads`, `applyPooler`, `applyEmbeddings`. Paired `Test.HfBertLoraInject` with two assertions (`testInjectBitMatchAtInit` max-abs-diff 0.0; `testInjectNonzeroDelta` max-abs-diff 3.56e-4 with manually-set nonzero B). **(L4)** `2f678016` `feat(transformers): peft-compatible adapter I/O` — new `packages/idris-transformers/src/HfLoraIO.idr` round-trips LoRA adapter directories with HuggingFace `peft`'s on-disk layout (`adapter_config.json` + `adapter_model.safetensors`). `idrisToPeftName` / `peftToIdrisName` wrap/strip peft's `base_model.model.` prefix + `.default.weight` suffix at the safetensors-IO boundary. `saveLoraAdapter outputDir cfg` writes both files via new `saveModelMatchingRenamed` (which forwards to a new `param_save_by_name_renamed` C FFI accepting two name lists in lockstep — lookup names + on-disk names). `loadLoraAdapter outputDir` reads adapter_config.json into a `LoraAdapterConfig`. Hand-rolled JSON emitter mirrors peft's strict schema (peft_type, task_type, r, lora_alpha, target_modules, lora_dropout, bias, fan_in_fan_out, modules_to_save, inference_mode). `peft>=0.14` added to pyproject. Paired `Test.HfLoraIO` with three assertions (config round-trip; peft-wrapped keys present in safetensors via register-under-peft-names then loadModel; idrisToPeftName/peftToIdrisName round-trip + reject non-peft inputs). Optional Python validator `validate_lora_adapter.py` opens an idris-saved adapter into `PeftModel.from_pretrained` for cross-tool round-trip evidence. **(L5)** `<this commit>` `feat(example): SST-2 BERT LoRA fine-tune + peft ref + closure` — `packages/idris-ml-examples/src/Example/BertClassifySst2Lora.idr` (~330 LOC) mirrors `BertClassifySst2Finetune.idr` but with LoRA injection: warm-starts the bert-tiny backbone via `loadModelPrefixAllowCast _ "bert."`, constructs `BertLoraAdapters` via `loraInjectBert "bert" 2 8 16.0`, freezes the backbone via `freezeByPrefix opt "bert."` then unfreezes adapters via `unfreezeBySuffix opt "lora_A"` + `unfreezeBySuffix opt "lora_B"` (classifier stays trainable). Forward via `hfBertSeqClassifyForwardWithLora`. CLI flags `--lora-rank --lora-alpha --save-adapter`. AdamW(lr=1e-4, wd=0.01, clip=1.0) — higher LR than full FT's 2e-5 because only the adapters update (peft tutorial recommendation). Paired `bert_classify_sst2_lora_finetune.py` uses `peft.LoraConfig(task_type=SEQ_CLS, r=8, lora_alpha=16, target_modules=["query","value"], lora_dropout=0.0, bias="none")` + `get_peft_model`. Default subset (256 train / 256 dev / 3 epochs / batch=8): Idris tape loss 0.346 → 0.337, dev acc 0.539 → 0.570. Trainable param count drops from ~4.4M (full FT) to ~6K (~0.13% of model). Adapter file ~80KB vs ~17MB full safetensors. `bert-classify-sst2-lora` perf-run case arm (`BERT_SST2_LORA_ARGS`) wired. Makefile gains `example-bert-classify-sst2-lora`, `ref-bert-classify-sst2-lora-finetune`, `validate-lora-adapter` (cross-tool gate using ADAPTER_DIR var). Convergence on the 256-subset matches the bound established by the prior RT3 closure ("demo proves path works on a small subset; full SST-2 convergence needs hours on tape"). Original "LoRA / parameter-efficient fine-tuning" row deleted from TODO.md.


BERT MLM continued pretraining example landed (2026-06-07). Closes the "BERT MLM continued pretraining example" follow-up row added at FT4. **(B1)** `6467c236` `feat(example): BERT MLM continued pretraining + masking + selective loss` — new `Example/BertMlmFinetune.idr` (~320 LOC): loads the bert-tiny backbone WITH the MLM head via `loadModelAllowCast` (44 params: 39 backbone + 5 MLM head), reads a WordPiece-tokenized Tiny Shakespeare corpus at `data/tinyshakespeare/input.bert-tiny.tokens` (~289K tokens via `google/bert_uncased_L-2_H-128_A-2`'s WordPiece), samples random sliding windows of SeqLen=32 tokens, applies HF's 80/10/10 masking (15% of positions masked: 80% → [MASK]=103, 10% → fixed mid-vocab id=200, 10% → keep; CLS / SEP at ids 101 / 102 are never masked), runs position-selective cross-entropy ONLY at masked positions. `mkMaskedTargetOneHot` builds the `[SeqLen, Vocab]` target via `primOneHot` + `primReshape2d` then multiplies by a `[SeqLen, 1]` mask via broadcast (one-hot rows at masked positions, zero rows elsewhere). `bertMlmLoss` reduces by `numMasked` (defensive denominator: `max(numMasked, 1)` for the rare no-mask window). Forward via `hfBertMlmForward _ _ _ _ Nothing` (no attention mask — sliding-window samples never hit a padded tail). New Make target `data-tinyshakespeare-bert-tiny` runs `tokenize-text-corpus.sh` against the BERT WordPiece tokenizer. Paired ref `bert_mlm_finetune.py` reads the SAME token file via `AutoModelForMaskedLM.from_pretrained` + builds HF `labels` with `-100` at unmasked positions; HF's `CrossEntropyLoss(ignore_index=-100)` gives the same position-selective semantics. New `bert-mlm-finetune` perf-run case arm (`BERT_MLM_ARGS`). Convergence on 50 steps: Idris tape EMA 5.15 → 4.90 / 8.2s, Idris torch 5.15 → 4.90 / 1m13s, Idris mlx-cpu (seed=99 via perf-run-quiet, different sample stream) 7.3s, PyTorch CPU EMA 5.51 → 5.21 / 0.6s. Per-step variance is high because each window has a different (random) number of masked positions; EMA smooths it. **(B2)** `<this commit>` `docs: close out BERT MLM continued pretraining row` — this entry; "BERT MLM continued pretraining example" row removed from `TODO.md`; reference-alignment row added.


GPT-2 LM continued pretraining example landed (2026-06-07). Closes the "GPT-2 LM continued pretraining example" follow-up row added at FT4. **(G1)** `721c84f4` `feat(example): GPT-2 LM continued pretraining on Tiny Shakespeare` — new `Example/Gpt2LmFinetune.idr` (~285 LOC): loads the distilgpt2 backbone via `loadModelAllowCast "models/distilgpt2/model.safetensors"`, reads the pre-tokenized Tiny Shakespeare corpus from `data/tinyshakespeare/input.distilgpt2.tokens`, samples random sliding windows of (SeqLen=32 + 1) tokens, runs next-token cross-entropy against the shifted-by-1 target via a 2D `[seqLen, vocab]` one-hot path. Forward via `hfGpt2ForwardLm` (tied wte LM head; no new head to wire). AdamW(lr=5e-5, wd=0.01, clip=1.0). The loss function `gpt2LmLoss` mirrors `Example/Gpt`'s `allPositionsCELoss` (logSoftmax2d + elementwise multiply against one-hot + sum + negate + mean over seqLen) but starts from the 2D logits directly. Target one-hot built via `primOneHot` + `primReshape2d` (the C-side onehot returns the flat `[seqLen*vocab]` layout the from-scratch GPT uses; reshape to 2D so the loss multiplies against `[seqLen, vocab]` logits). New `scripts/tokenize-text-corpus.sh` wraps `transformers.AutoTokenizer.encode` to emit a flat comma-separated token-id stream (no per-example structure — the corpus is one document). New Make targets `data-tinyshakespeare-distilgpt2`, `example-gpt2-lm-finetune`, `ref-gpt2-lm-finetune`. New `gpt2-lm-finetune` perf-run case arm (`GPT2_LM_ARGS`). Paired ref `gpt2_lm_finetune.py` reads the SAME token file via `AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained("distilgpt2")` + same AdamW + same sliding-window sampling. Convergence on 50 steps (seed=42): Idris tape EMA loss 5.26 → 4.89 / 1m13s, Idris torch 5.26 → 4.89 / 24.6s, Idris mlx-cpu EMA loss ~4.3 (different seed in perf-run-quiet, different sample stream) / 3m19s, PyTorch CPU EMA loss 5.04 → 4.80 / 7.3s. Loss-step trajectories aren't bit-aligned cross-backend because the random window-start sampling differs (`Generate.randomInt` vs Python `random.randint`); EMA reduction washes out the per-step variance. **(G2)** `<this commit>` `docs: close out GPT-2 LM continued pretraining row` — this entry; "GPT-2 LM continued pretraining example" row removed from `TODO.md`; reference-alignment row added.


Real-text HF fine-tuning landed (2026-06-07). Closes the "Real-text dataset support for HF fine-tuning" row added at FT4. Three shipped commits + two suspected pre-existing bugs reproduced and fixed alongside. **(BUG-A)** `f18c8705` `fix(test): defer mkInput allocation via ioRerun in MixedLayerLike` — torch backend crashed in `tensor_linear_torch` → `torch::mv` with `c10::Error: vector + matrix @ vector expected, got 1, 2, 0` (rank-0 input handle). Root cause: the documented `feedback_pure_typed_ffi_reorders` gotcha — `Test/MixedLayerLike.idr`'s `let input = mkInput xs` form let-bound a pure-typed `bulkToTensor` call, and an earlier sibling `lin <- mixedLinearLayerAny` IO action caused Idris/Chez to reorder the C-side tensor allocation past it. Fix: lift `mkInput` to `IO (Tensor [n] ...)` with `ioRerun (\_ => bulkToTensor xs)` so the allocation is sequenced. `[MixedLayerLike] 4/4 PASS` on BACKEND=torch,tape post-fix. **(BUG-B)** `1d4f3fd8` `fix(test): relax SwiGLU oracle tolerance on mlx (Accelerate F32-precision floor)` — `[SwiGLU]` failed on `BACKEND=mlx,tape` with max-abs-diff 1.14e-7 > tol 1e-9 against the PyTorch F64 oracle. Root cause: mlx-cpu's silu/multiply lands in Accelerate kernels that intermediate-cast to F32 even at F64 storage. Fix: make `tol` backend-aware via `TestPrimaryBackend` — `1e-6` on mlx, `1e-9` elsewhere. **(RT1)** `03153bc0` `feat(hfbert): thread optional attention mask through encoder` — adds `Maybe (Tensor [seqLen, seqLen])` as the final arg of `hfBertForward` / `hfBertMlmForward` / `hfBertSeqClassifyForward`; internals (`applyEncoder` / `applyLayer` / `applySelfAttn` / `oneHeadCtx` / `buildHeads`) thread an unwrapped `Maybe AnyPtr` and `primMaskedFill scores mask (-1.0e20)` fires between matmul and softmax when `Just`. `Nothing` is bit-identical to the pre-RT1 path. New `Test.HfBertAttentionMask` with 2 assertions (zero-mask bit-matches Nothing; ones-mask differs measurably from Nothing). Existing callers updated to pass `Nothing` explicitly. **(RT2)** `47f07855` `feat(transformers): HfDataset TSV loader + hf-download-dataset.sh` — new `packages/idris-transformers/src/HfDataset.idr` with `loadHfDataset`, `padToSeqLen`, `toAttentionMask2d` (1D HF-convention mask → 2D row-major flat); new `packages/idris-transformers/scripts/hf-download-dataset.sh` wraps HF `datasets.load_dataset` + `transformers.AutoTokenizer.encode` and writes `<label>\t<id1,id2,…>` TSVs into `data/hf-datasets/<repo>/`. Choice of TSV over JSONL: keeps the Idris parser to `Data.String.split` — no JSON dependency. Pre-tokenizes at download time so the Idris side never pays the ~1s/call subprocess startup of `Tokenizer.idr`. Test fixture `test-fixtures/sst2-mini/mini.tsv` (3 hand-curated lines) + `Test.HfDataset` (5 assertions across 2 buckets). New Make targets `data-sst2` and `clean-datasets`; `datasets>=3.0` added to `pytorch/pyproject.toml`. **(RT3)** `<this commit>` `feat(example): SST-2 BERT classify fine-tune + PyTorch ref` — `packages/idris-ml-examples/src/Example/BertClassifySst2Finetune.idr` warm-starts the `google/bert_uncased_L-2_H-128_A-2` backbone via `loadModelPrefixAllowCast _ "bert."` (39/41 params: the 2 missing are MLM-head params under `cls.*` which the classification model doesn't need), loads `data/hf-datasets/glue-sst2/{train,validation}.tsv`, pads via `HfDataset.padToSeqLen` to seqLen=32, builds the 2D attention mask via `toAttentionMask2d`, forwards through `hfBertSeqClassifyForward _ _ _ _ (Just mask)`, runs AdamW(lr=2e-5, wd=0.01, clip=1.0). Paired `packages/pytorch/torch_ref/scripts/bert_classify_sst2_finetune.py` reads the SAME TSV files + same hyperparameters via `transformers.BertForSequenceClassification.from_pretrained` + `BertConfig`. Default subset (256 train / 256 dev / 3 epochs / batch=8) is tuned for fast iteration — convergence is bounded by the subset (Idris tape 59.4%, torch 60.9%, mlx 56.3%; PyTorch CPU 52.0%). Full SST-2 + 3 epochs would converge to ~80%+ (HF tutorial) but takes ~hours on Idris tape; the demo's contribution is proving the real-text path works end-to-end on all 3 backends. Perf-log entries land on tape/torch/mlx-cpu. Original "Real-text dataset support" row deleted from TODO.md; the variable-length real-text path is now demonstrable.


HF model fine-tuning surface landed (2026-06-07). Four commits closed the "Support fine-tuning workflows on HF-loaded models" feature: (FT1) `2e396f13` `feat(checkpoint): add prefix-filtered subset load + freeze-by-prefix` — extends the safetensors loader with `param_load_with_prefix(path, allow_cast, prefix)` (skips keys whose name doesn't start with `prefix`; `param_load_core` helper shares the load loop with the pre-existing `param_load_with_policy`), surfaces `loadModelPrefix path pfx` / `loadModelPrefixAllowCast` in `Checkpoint.idr`, and ships `freezeByPrefix opt pfx` / `unfreezeByPrefix` in new `Train/Freeze.idr` (pure Idris over the existing `primParamCount`/`primParamName`/`primOptimizerSetParamLr` registry FFIs). New `param_load_with_prefix` registered as a `UserExecutorSerialize` typeclass method, manifest entry added, per-backend `%foreign` decls landed in `Executor/{Tape,Torch,Mlx}.idr`. Unit test `Test.CheckpointSubset` exercises the round-trip: save 4 params under `ft1.aa.*` + `ft1.bb.*`, re-register all 4 with value 99.0, `loadModelPrefix _ "ft1.aa."`, assert `aa.*` restored + `bb.*` untouched at 99.0 (5 assertions + 1 freeze smoke = 6 assertions, all green on tape / torch / mlx). (FT2) `47b12342` `feat(transformers): BertForSequenceClassification head module` — new `packages/idris-transformers/src/HfBertForClassification.idr` mirroring the existing MLM-head pattern at `HfBert.idr:689-743`: `BertClassifierHeadState (hidden, numClasses)` wraps a single Linear under `classifier.*`, `BertForSequenceClassificationState` aggregates base + head, `hfBertForSequenceClassification "bert" "classifier"` returns a constructed state, `hfBertSeqClassifyForward` composes `hfBertForward` (pooled `[CLS]`) with the head's `tlinear` for `[numClasses]` logits. Pure-Idris `classifierHeadParamNames` + `bertForSequenceClassificationParamNames` provide the on-disk-naming catalogue (`["classifier.weight", "classifier.bias"]`; combined catalogue is 41 = 39 backbone + 2 head at `bertTinyConfig` + numClasses=3). 4-bucket test suite `Test.HfBertForClassification` mirrors `Test.HfBert` exactly (5 assertions: catalogue, combined catalogue, FFI registry order, forward shape + finite). HF dropout-between-pooler-and-classifier deliberately omitted in v1 head — the FT3 worked example trains without it and converges. (FT3) `54b6bf43` `feat(example): BERT classification fine-tune + PyTorch ref` — `packages/idris-ml-examples/src/Example/BertClassifyFinetune.idr` runs a tiny BERT (vocab=64, hidden=32, layers=1, heads=2, headDim=16, intermediate=64, maxPos=8, typeVocab=2) with the new `BertForSequenceClassification` head against a synthetic 3-class task (label-token at position 1: class 0→token 11, class 1→token 13, class 2→token 17; CLS=0, SEP=1, distractors random in [20, 60]); custom `epochBert` closure passed to `runTrainingIO` (HF models aren't Network-shaped, so the existing `epochVar*` runners don't apply — the example demonstrates the closure pattern). Converges to 100% accuracy on all 3 backends at seed=42 (Idris tape 1582 epochs / 11s wall, Idris torch 1016 epochs / 18s wall, Idris mlx-cpu 1143 epochs / 2m46s wall; PyTorch CPU 1691 epochs / 2s wall) and 5/5 on tape across seeds 1-5. Paired PyTorch ref `packages/pytorch/torch_ref/scripts/bert_classify_finetune.py` uses `transformers.BertForSequenceClassification` with matching `BertConfig` + matching dataset generator + AdamW(lr=1e-3, wd=0.01, clip_norm=1.0) + identical patience/early-stop semantics. Wired `bert-classify-finetune` into `scripts/perf-run.sh` (`BERT_FINETUNE_ARGS`); Makefile targets `example-bert-classify-finetune` and `ref-bert-classify-finetune`; alignment row added to `docs/develop/reference-alignment.md` with the full hyperparameter table. (FT4) `<this commit>` `docs: close out HF fine-tuning row + park follow-ups` — design-decisions subsections, user guide section, CLAUDE.md note, this closure entry, four follow-up TODO rows (real-text dataset support + attention masking; LoRA / parameter-efficient fine-tuning; GPT-2 LM continued pretraining example; BERT MLM continued pretraining example). Original row deleted from TODO.md.


Backend/device DRY-up + conceptual rework — row fully closed (2026-06-07). Three commits landed across the residual surface (`69750251` docs, `e1deb6b0` runtime machine verification, `612aaa69` manifest-driven instance methods + `84be8cc7` CI wire) and the original row's 5 pain points are now individually resolved or recorded as deliberately not-pursued. (1) **Cross-backend drift** — solved earlier via `scripts/check-executor-method-drift.py` + CI wire. (2) **N+1 sites per primitive** — closed by `612aaa69`: `scripts/lifecycle/ffi_manifest.py` MANIFEST entries promoted to a frozen `Entry` dataclass (args/ret + slice + idris_method + c_symbol + per-backend flavor), expanded 170 → 215 entries to cover the simple-FFI surface (optimizer_\*, param_\*, profile_\*, epoch_\*); `scripts/lifecycle/gen-executor-instances.py` walks MANIFEST and emits per-backend method body lines between `-- >>> GENERATED FROM ffi_manifest.py >>>` / `-- <<< END GENERATED <<<` markers in each instance block in `Executor/{Tape,Torch,Mlx}.idr`; 13 instance blocks per file, ~345 method body lines now generated, ~10 bespoke methods preserved below END marker (Torch device-aware primCreate/primCreateScalar/primIntraMigrate, Mlx unstreamed primSdpa2d/primRmsNorm2d/primSwiGlu2d, Mlx BitLinear oracle variants). Adding a new op now edits ~4 sites instead of ~7. `--check` mode wired as `make check-executor-instances` and gated in `.github/workflows/test.yml`. Bonus fixes folded in: WRAP_HANDLE_FILES paths updated `Device/` → `Executor/` (the rename happened in a prior commit; the manifest's path list had silently disabled lint coverage), and the wrap-template lint filter to only require Scheme wrapping when the entry is Tensor-touching. (3) **Device/Backend/Hw triple hand-waved** — solved earlier by the open-kind reshape (Machine / Hardware / Backend / Executor distinct kinds with RunsOn / RunsVia / Provides relations, see design-decisions.md "2026-06-06: Machine / Hardware / Backend / Executor open-kind reshape"). (4) **Typeclass multiplicity** — the originally-proposed "collapse into one interface" direction (a) was rejected and recorded in design-decisions.md "Sub-slice split rather than monolith collapse"; instead landed the sub-slice split at commit `ceda8f30` — the aggregate `UserExecutorTraining` keeps consumer constraints unchanged, only the implementer-side shape changed; sub-slices carve at real joints. (5) **Compatible × RuntimeDType** — direction (d) "unify Compatible + RuntimeDType" rejected as conflating orthogonal axes (compile-time admissibility vs runtime FFI selector); recorded in design-decisions.md "Compatible and RuntimeDType are orthogonal"; 54 explicit Compatible instances kept as-is, carve-outs (MGpu F64, MPS F64) are intentional and have hardware reasons. Bonus runtime gate landed via `e1deb6b0`: `MachineRuntimeCheck` typeclass + per-Machine instances + C-side host probes (`packages/backends/probes.{c,h}` — uname OS/arch + Metal-framework + libcuda presence) + `requireMachine {m = ChosenMachine}` wired into 44 example `main` bodies; three strictness modes via `IDRISML_MACHINE_CHECK=warn|off`. Defines a third gate (program-startup) complementing the existing compile-time `Linked` gate and the first-use EAFP via `toExecutorChecked`. Verified end-to-end: all 3 backends install green; tape tests pass; supervised example converges 5/5 on tape, torch, and mlx (perf-logged via `perf-run-quiet.sh`); `check-executor-instances --check` returns 0; `check-executor-method-drift.py` returns 0. Original row deleted from TODO.md.


Executor forwarder DRY-up closed as no-op (2026-06-07). Drops the planned `packages/idris-ml/src/Executor/Common.idr` extraction (FFI dispatch combinators `unaryFFI` / `binaryFFI` / `unaryStreamed` / `binaryStreamed` + ~168-forwarder-per-backend rewrite). Survey of the three instance files showed the premise was wrong: `Executor/Tape.idr` has **168/168** forwarders already in point-free `primAdd = prim__addTape` form, `Executor/Torch.idr` has **165/168** point-free (the 3 bespoke ones — `primCreateScalar`, `primCreate`, `primIntraMigrate` — do real work a combinator can't absorb), and only `Executor/Mlx.idr` has **97/168** with explicit-arg bodies because `(streamTag s)` must be appended after the call arguments (`primAdd a b = prim__addMlxStreamed a b (streamTag s)`). A `streamedN tag f a b = f a b tag` combinator family would make Mlx point-free (`primAdd = streamed2 (streamTag s) prim__addMlxStreamed`) but at identical line count and worse readability (the digit suffix encodes arg-count-before-tag, which becomes opaque at `streamed10` for `primBatchNorm`). The originally-cited drift concern is also already structurally precluded: per-symbol Scheme `%foreign` wrappers are autogenerated from a single FFI manifest (`scripts/lifecycle/ffi_manifest.py` + `ffi-convert-to-scheme.py`), and `scripts/check-executor-method-drift.py` runs in CI for interface-level divergence. Per CLAUDE.md "three similar lines is better than a premature abstraction" + "don't refactor beyond what the task requires", landing Common.idr would churn already-minimal files for ~zero LOC delta and a Mlx-only cosmetic shift. Recorded here so the conclusion doesn't get re-derived.


tape symmetric F32 grads — Row 38 closed (2026-06-06). Migrates the tape backend's grad buffer allocation + every grad-touching backward site from the legacy F64-only path to a dtype-aware typed path: F32-tagged tensors now get a `numel * sizeof(float)` grad buffer (half the memory of the prior F64 default); the typed accessors `tape_grad_load_d` / `tape_grad_add_d` / `tape_grad_store_d` (inline in arena.h) dispatch on `t->dtype_tag` to read/write the right buffer width. `ensure_grad` rewritten in `arena.c` to use `tape_grad_elem_size(dtype_tag)`. Migration touched ~150 grad-touching sites across ~70 files: every elementwise/scalar/reduction backward (add/sub/mul/div/pow/exp/log/sqrt/sigmoid/tanh/softplus/abs/neg/mul_scalar/add_scalar/clamp_min/sum/mean), linalg (mm/bmm/linear/linear_2d/matmul/dot/outer/transpose/tile/concat/gather/scatter_add/select/narrow/reshape/cumprod), conv+pool (conv1d/conv1d_circular/conv2d/conv2d_batched/max_pool*/avg_pool*), nn (rms_norm/layer_norm/batch_norm/dropout/softmax/log_softmax/embedding/masked_fill/bce_with_logits/cross_attention/cosine_similarity/swiglu/silu/gelu/leaky_relu), recurrent (lstm_gates_pair/gru_cell), plus the cross-cutting surfaces: backward driver's loss-grad init (`backward.c:47-53`), optimizer M/V buffer scaling (`optimizer.c:362`), shared param-registry grad_read/grad_write/zero_grad in `adapter.c`, training/diagnostics.c grad readout. Bulk migration done via a Python script that grep-substituted `((double*)X->grad)[Y]` patterns; multi-line statements + nested-bracket index expressions + 2-level dereferences (`meta->X->grad`) + local pointer aliasing (`double* ag = (double*)a->grad`) caught by hand. No parallel API maintained — the dtype-aware `ensure_grad` replaces the F64-only version in place (per the no-backcompat directive). RED before this commit: `tape_f32_gradcheck_oracle`, `tape_f32_non_elementwise_coverage`, `tape_f32_rnn_conv_coverage` all asserted `w.grad_f32[i] ~ w.grad_f64` and were passing pre-migration only because both legs ran through F64 grad buffers; after the API change, unmigrated sites would memory-smash (write 8 bytes into 4-byte slots) and fail. The migration was iterated until all three coverage tests + `prop_f32_grad_matches_f64` (Idris property) pass. GREEN: `make BACKEND=tape test-unit-c` 230/230 + `make BACKEND=tape test` all-green.


CUDA BF16/F16 verification row folded into the High-Priority CUDA catch-all (2026-06-06). Deletes the standalone Medium row "BF16 / F16 GPU-fast paths — CUDA tensor cores (mlx-Metal landed 2026-05-31)" — its substance (verify BF16/F16 wall_ms < F32 wall_ms on tensor cores, hypothesis untested on libtorch MPS within noise + mlx-Metal +62% regression) belonged inside the High-Priority CUDA row's runbook, which already covers the smoke test, perf-sweep, and multi-GPU enumeration steps that the BF16 verification depends on. The folded step becomes the fourth runbook item: "BF16 / F16 tensor-core perf verification" — re-runs the perf-sweep (step 2) under BF16/F16 dtypes and checks for `wall_ms < F32 wall_ms` on the larger examples; this is the regime where the "BF16 runtime dominates F32" hypothesis is plausibly true, given MPS and mlx-Metal both ruled it out. Compatible instances + uniform torch dispatch + autocast/GradScaler all already shipped; the only missing piece is running it on CUDA hardware. Cross-references updated to point at the consolidated row.


tape conv2d_batched F32 forward: im2col + cblas_sgemm (2026-06-06). Closes the "Switch tape OP_CONV2D_BATCHED F32 forward to im2col + cblas_sgemm" Medium row. The F32 forward branch in `backend_tape/conv/conv2d_batched.c` was a direct 6-loop computation (`for b, oc, oh, ow, ic, kh, kw`) accumulating in `double` and narrowing on store — chosen at Phase 3b time for numerical-stability headroom and to ship without touching `conv2d_im2col`'s F64-typed signature. This commit mirrors the F64 im2col + `cblas_dgemm` path: new static `conv2d_im2col_f32` helper (parallel float-typed version of `conv2d_im2col`) + `cblas_sgemm` call with the same parameter shape as the existing F64 `cblas_dgemm` site (`CblasRowMajor, CblasNoTrans, CblasTrans, M, outC, K, ...`). `__APPLE__` guard with portable-C fallback matches the F64 pattern at the same call site (and the precedent set by `eaec5b39` for the Ubuntu lane). Backward unchanged: the F32 backward path already widens F32 inputs to F64 temporaries and reuses the F64 dgemm chain (per the existing comment block at `tape_backward_conv2d_batched`); skipping that widening is a larger refactor out of scope for this row. New paired F32-vs-F64 forward test in `test_conv2d_batched.c` covers a representative CNN-shape (B=2, inC=3, H=W=8, outC=4, kH=kW=3); tolerance `1e-4 * |ref| + 1e-5` bounds the sgemm F32-mantissa accumulation error vs the F64 reference.
