Calloc-zero buffers decode as all-ternary-zero — so freshly-allocated sub-byte storage has the right semantic default. (2) New C test `packages/backends/test/common/core/dtype/test_ternary_pack.c` with three roundtrip assertions (length-12 byte-exact encoding, length-5 padding, all-zeros). RED before the stubs were replaced: byte 0 expected 0x71 got 0xAA, unpack at index 0 expected -1 got 0, all-zeros expected 0x00 got 0xAA. Green on all three backends (tape/torch/mlx criterion `ternary_pack/*` 3/3 each). (3) `packages/idris-ml/src/DType/Core.idr` gains `data Ternary` + `data Binary` types as zero-value-set tags (no inhabited values reach runtime — they're kind-level slots like `Float n`). `IsDType` instances carry `dtypeBytes = 0` as a deliberate sentinel ("buffer-size code that asks `numel * dtypeBytes` on a packed dtype crashes loudly with a zero-byte buffer rather than silently undersizing"). New `LosslessTo Ternary to` / `LosslessTo Binary to` instances for `FloatPrecision to` (covers F16/F32/F64/BF16, since `{-1, 0, +1}` and `{-1, +1}` are exactly representable in every IEEE float and brain-float) and for `{m : Nat} -> LTE 2 m => LosslessTo Ternary (IntN m)` (signed 2-bit width 2 covers `-2..1`; same edge for Binary). The instances ship without `FloatPrecision`-style metadata because the value-set IS the precision — the open-typeclass shape from the 2026-06-01 LosslessTo→UpcastableTo bridge (commit `8e99747`, closed #412) was deliberately designed to admit them. The bridge `LosslessTo from to => UpcastableTo from to` threads every new edge into `tcast` resolution: `tcast tern_t {to=F32}` typechecks; the lossy reverse (`tcast f32_t {to=Ternary}`) refuses to compile (no instance, requires explicit `tcastUnsafe`). (4) `packages/idris-ml/src/Tensor.idr` extends the RuntimeDType ABI table: `Binary` -> dtag 24, `Ternary` -> dtag 25. The dtag layout docstring grows a new "family 6: sub-byte quant" row noting that 24/25 are slots in that family with named lanes (not arithmetic). (5) `Compatible` instances per the existing per-backend pattern: `Compatible TapeDev Ternary/Binary` (tape pioneers sub-byte arena); `Compatible (TorchDev TCpu) Ternary/Binary` and `{n : Nat} -> Compatible (TorchDev (TCuda n)) Ternary/Binary` (CPU + CUDA only — libtorch MPS lacks sub-byte storage, mirrors the Int* / Bool exclusion); `Compatible (MlxDev MCpu) Ternary/Binary` (CPU stream only — mlx Metal sub-byte arrives with the future BitLinear-kernel commit). (6) Tape arena enum extension in `packages/backends/backend_tape/tensor.{h,c}`: new `DT_BINARY` + `DT_TERNARY` tail entries on the internal-dtype enum; `tape_elem_size` returns 0 as a sub-byte sentinel for callers that need to consult a future `tape_packed_bytes` instead; `tape_tag_from_dtag` maps ABI dtags 24/25 to the new internal tags so a future cast-into-Ternary has a deterministic abort site rather than UB. (7) Idris-side regression coverage: `packages/idris-ml/test/src/Test/Lossless.idr` gains six new `%search`-based witnesses (`proofTernaryToF32`, `proofTernaryToBF16`, `proofTernaryToF16`, `proofTernaryToI8`, `proofBinaryToF32`, `proofBinaryToI8`) plus a `ternaryAndBinaryResolve` row; `[Lossless]` group goes from 4 to 5 tests, all PASS. **Tests**: `make BACKEND=tape test` `[Lossless] 5/5` (was 4/4); `make BACKEND={tape,torch,mlx} test-backend-criterion` 207/207 / 197/197 / mlx-green respectively (the +3 each is the new `ternary_pack` group). `make check-lossy-cast-gate` + `make check-int-overflow-cast-gate` + `make check-rename-headers` all unchanged-green (the new pack/unpack helpers live in `shared_utils.h` not `backend.h`, so the rename surface doesn't grow). RED before this commit: see (2). **What this commit deliberately doesn't do**: no `BitLinear` layer, no `tensor_bitlinear_fwd` per-backend kernels, no HF BitNet adapter, no actual `Tensor [..] Ternary` construction path on any backend (the `dtCreate*` paths abort on dtag 24/25 at the per-backend dispatch site until the BitLinear-kernel commit lands). Cross-references: `docs/develop/dtype-parameter.md` (FloatPrecision + LosslessTo section); `docs/develop/perf-changes.md` (the ternary-dtype-foundation entry, 2026-06-01).


Type-safe mixed precision shipped (closed 2026-06-01, TODO #410, 10 commits `eda3bfb`..`64a84ad`). The "Autocast region + GradScaler (decoupled param/act dtype for mixed-precision training)" row closes with idris-ml's type-system-visible answer to PyTorch's runtime autocast. Step-by-step: (A0 `eda3bfb`) new `LayerLikeMixed` interface in `packages/idris-ml/src/Layer/MixedCore.idr` parallel to `LayerLike` but carrying two dtype slots (`paramDt` + `computeDt`); `AsMixed` wrapper bridges any `AnyLayer` into the mixed-precision world with `paramDt = computeDt`, so all 15 existing layers slot into `NetworkMixed` chains via `liftAnyLayer` / `liftNetwork` without code changes. (A0.5 `bddaa3f`) new `FloatPrecision` typeclass in `DType/Core.idr` exposing `mantissaBits` + `exponentBits` per float dtype (F16: 10/5, F32: 23/8, F64: 52/11, BF16: 7/8); `LosslessTo from to` definitional pair-of-LTE-proofs witnesses cross-family lossless float casts; negative-compile-test gate `LossyDirectionRejected.idr` + `scripts/check-lossy-cast-gate.sh` (wired into CI) refuses `F32 → BF16` at the type level. Drive-by: fixed `scripts/check-gradmode-{gate,aliasing}.sh` stale `.idris2` paths from before the multi-build-key refactor — both gates now actually run via `IDRIS2_LOCAL` env-var passthrough. (#412 filed `fa68e2c`) `LosslessTo → UpcastableTo` bridge follow-up filed High Priority — A0.5 shipped the witness but didn't wire it to `UpcastableTo`, so `tcast bf16_t {to=F32}` still doesn't compile (closed F1 of the follow-up plan). (A1 `66eca8f`) autograd-aware `tcast` verified on all three backends — torch's `at::Tensor::to(ScalarType)` and mlx's `mx::astype` + `OP_CAST_DTYPE` tape entry were already autograd-aware; the fix was tape-only — `tape_cast_dtype_dtag` was silently `rg=0` on every non-F64-identity path, dropping autograd lineage at every dtype boundary. New `OP_CAST_DTYPE` enum + `tape_backward_cast_dtype` with identity gradient flow (cast is locally linear; both `r->grad` and `a->grad` are F64 buffers via `ensure_grad` regardless of value storage). New `cast_grad_propagation` C test (F64 source rg=1 → F32 cast → F32 mul → backward → assert `src.grad == 3.0`) passes on all three backends after the fix. (A2 `dbbe501`) first concrete `LayerLikeMixed` user — `LinearMixed i o d paramDt computeDt g` record holds weight + bias in paramDt; `applyVarMixed` casts both to computeDt via `tcastUnsafe` then `tlinear`; the lossy edge (typical F32 master → BF16 compute) is at the layer boundary, code-visible. Required adding `IsDType pDt` / `IsDType cDt` to the `LayerLikeMixed` interface signature so `tcastUnsafe` resolves. (A3 four-commit completion `c4a4f31`..`1cb1204`) shared `native_train_step_scaled` C function for tape (NaN-sentinel return = overflow detected, step skipped, caller halves scale) + per-backend ports in `backend_torch/training/optimizer.cpp` (libtorch's `g.mul_(1/scale)` + `at::isfinite(g).all().item<bool>()`) and `backend_mlx/training/optimizer.cpp` (mlx's `mx::multiply` + `mx::all(mx::isfinite(g))` + `mx::eval`); new `primNativeTrainStepScaled` in `UserDeviceTraining`; new `GradScaler.idr` module with IORef-based growth/backoff state machine matching PyTorch's `cuda.amp.GradScaler` defaults (initScale=65536, growthFactor=2.0, backoffFactor=0.5, growthInterval=2000). (A4 `64a84ad`) new `epochVarMixed` in `Backprop.idr` parallel to `epochVar` — takes `NetworkMixed` + `GradScaler`, threads through `forwardVarMixed` + `applyScale` + `trainStepScaled`. End-to-end smoke `epochVarMixedSmoke` on tape verifies every A piece in series (LayerLikeMixed bridge → LinearMixed forward with autograd-aware cast → mean-reduce → `applyScale` → `trainStepScaled` → growth/backoff advance) — returns finite loss 1.7165 on default seeded data. **Tests**: `make test-idris` `[MixedLayerLike] 4/4`, `[GradScaler] 3/3`, `[Lossless] 1/1`; `make test-backend-{tape,torch,mlx}` 204/204, 194/194, 196/196 including the new `cast_grad_propagation` + `native_train_step_scaled_unscale` assertions; `make check-lossy-cast-gate` neg gate refuses F32 → BF16. **Lessons learned recorded in `docs/develop/gotchas.md` Idris-2 and Architecture sections**: (L1+L7) higher-order `l` as data-parameter doesn't propagate erasure annotations — use concrete wrappers through existing existentials; (L2) named auto-implicits + `@{%search}` slot-pinning to disambiguate when type parameters unify; (L3) any new C symbol needs `make rename-headers`; (L4) shared `optimizer.c` only links into tape — torch/mlx need per-backend `optimizer.cpp` ports. **F-block follow-ups** (all shipped 2026-06-01, commits `823da09`..`8782639`, 7 commits): **F0** (`823da09`) doc catch-up — CHANGELOG / design-decisions / dtype-parameter / gotchas + closed the #410 TODO row. **F1** (`8e99747`, **closes #412**) — `LosslessTo` converted from a definitional pair to an open per-pair typeclass; bridge `LosslessTo from to => UpcastableTo from to` threads cross-family lossless edges into the existing `tcast` resolution; new instances for float/BFloat cross-family (BF16→F32, F16→F32, F32→F64, …), IntN n → Float m (n ≤ mb+2: I8/I16→F16, I32→F64), UInt n → Float m (n ≤ mb+1), Bool → any Float/BFloat (trivial), Bool → IntN m (m≥2), Bool → UInt m (m≥1); deletes redundant per-family `UpcastableTo (Float m) (Float n)` / `UpcastableTo (BFloat m) (BFloat n)` instances (now derived via bridge); new `check-int-overflow-cast-gate` neg-test wired into CI (asserts I64 → F32 refuses to compile, LTE 64 25 unsolvable). The open-typeclass shape was deliberately designed to admit Ternary/Binary instances for #411 BitNet without `FloatPrecision`-style metadata. **F2** (`ba618f6`) — `epochVarTensorMixed` + `epochVarTensorBatchMixed` in `Backprop.idr` for Transformer/GPT-style pre-tensored + batched mixed-precision training, mirroring the existing `epochVarTensor` / `epochVarTensorBatch` shapes. **F3** (`dadad02`) — Supervised `--mixed-precision` flag selects `epochVarMixed`; eval ladder factored into `evalOneDefault` / `evalOneMixed` helpers; smoke 5/5 on tape with bit-identical loss to default. **F4** (`aaa8322`) — `scripts/sweeps/supervised-mixed.json` + RESULT `correct=N/5` + `final_scale=S` counters + `IDRIS2_LOCAL` env-var override in `sweep.sh`; 10/10 structural-parity proof on tape (`--mixed-precision true` produces bit-identical loss to `--mixed-precision false` at every seed when `paramDt = computeDt`). **F5** (`21dd1a3`) — Supervised `--param-dtype` flag pins `paramDt = F32` independently of `ExampleDType`; `runMixedGeneric` factored as polymorphic helper, each typed sub-program (`runMixedNative` for `paramDt = computeDt = ExampleDType`, `runMixedF32Master` for `paramDt = F32`) dispatched from a runtime string via `case` since Idris-2 can't dispatch types at runtime. **F6** (`8782639`, **stretch acceptance HIT**) — BF16-vs-F32 convergence sweep on torch-mps. 15-config matrix (5 seeds × 3 modes: plain BF16 / mixed-native / mixed-f32) measured: **plain BF16 0/5 reach 5/5 eval** (matches 2026-05-31 3/5 baseline); **mixed-native 0/5 at 5/5** (4/5 nudged to 4/5 from GradScaler's structural pass-through, despite no real dtype decoupling); **mixed-f32 5/5 at 5/5 eval, loss ~0.14 (within 4% of tape-F64 0.1361, ~30% below plain BF16's ~0.20)**. The F32-master / BF16-compute decoupling is the actual lever — both ingredients matter: the autograd-aware tcast (A1) propagates BF16 grad back through the cast onto the F32 master, and the F32 optimizer step on F32 weights accumulates per-step updates losslessly even when individual grads are small in BF16. `final_scale=65536.0` on every mixed config (no overflow tripped backoff; growthInterval=2000 > epochs=1000, scale stays at init) — the win is genuinely from the F32 master, not from loss scaling. **#411 BitNet b1.58 is now structurally unblocked.** Three prereqs all in place: `LayerLikeMixed` for the two-dtype-slot interface, `LosslessTo` as an open per-pair typeclass that admits Ternary/Binary instances without `FloatPrecision`-style metadata, autograd-aware `tcast` for the Ternary → cDt activation cast at forward time. Cross-references: `docs/develop/design-decisions.md` "LayerLikeMixed bridge" entry; `docs/develop/dtype-parameter.md` FloatPrecision + LosslessTo section; `docs/develop/perf-changes.md` 2026-06-01 entries (F4 structural-parity table + F6 BF16-vs-F32 result table).


BF16/F16 training end-to-end across all three backends (closed 2026-05-31, commits `e2ad295` mlx-BF16 + `de993e7` tensor_item BF16 readback fix + `9856771` mlx-F16). The "BF16/F16 training support, not just inference" goal closes with measured Supervised-example convergence across the matrix. Backends: tape (lingua-franca via F64 with `tape_round_to_dtype` BF16/F16 rounding — `TAPE_DTYPE=BF16` lr=0.03 epochs=1000 converges to loss 0.136 with 5/5 eval), torch-mps BF16 (loss 0.195, 3/5 eval — BF16 precision floor at this tiny model), torch-cpu BF16/F16 (works), torch-cuda BF16/F16 (works; gated on CUDA hardware), mlx-cpu BF16/F16 (`Compatible (MlxDev MCpu) BF16/F16` admissible), mlx-gpu BF16 (loss 0.193, 3/5 eval), mlx-gpu F16 (loss 0.135, 5/5 eval). The "missing BF16/F16 backward kernels" framing in the original Mixed-precision row was wrong as a literal claim — tape's lingua-franca path computes backward in F64 and rounds to target precision; torch + mlx use native autograd which is dtype-generic. What's actually missing is PyTorch's *autocast* + *GradScaler* recipe — separate row reframed under "Autocast region + GradScaler (decoupled param/act dtype for mixed-precision training)" since the structural type-parameter split (decoupling param storage dtype from activation/op dtype) is the real work. That row pairs with the BitNet-class "separating param-dtype from activation-dtype" prerequisite. Cross-references: `Device/Mlx.idr:643-668` (5 admissible Compatible instances), `Device/Torch.idr:644-652` (TCpu/TMps/TCuda + F16 carve-outs), `backend_mlx/core/lifecycle/{item,accessors,batch,cast,create*,create_scalar}.cpp` (per-dtag storage), `backend_mlx/precision.h` (`mx_to_doubles`/`mx_read_double`/`mx_bf16_from_doubles`/`mx_f16_from_doubles`), `backend_mlx/training/{dtype_dispatch,dtype_init}.cpp` (case 13 + 17 routing), `test_legacy_backend.c::mlx_bf16_storage` (11 assertions PASSED).


mlx-Metal BF16 enablement (closed 2026-05-31, TODO #405). The mlx C-side `mlx_dtype_unsupported` abort message said *"Metal has no bf16/f16/int storage"* — **factually wrong on M3+** (mlx's `mx::bfloat16` type exists; Apple Metal has hardware BF16). Replaced the false claim with real storage: (1) `packages/backends/backend_mlx/precision.h` `mx_array_from_doubles` now routes `mx::bfloat16` via `mx::astype(fp32, mx::bfloat16)` so the float→bfloat16 narrowing uses mlx's own round-to-nearest-even encoding; `mx_to_doubles` and `mx_read_double` widen `mx::bfloat16_t` (from `<arm_bf16.h>` on Apple Silicon, fallback `_MLX_BFloat16` struct elsewhere) per-element. (2) `core/lifecycle/{create,create_scalar,create_param_state,cast}.cpp` gained `*_bf16_mlx_streamed` siblings for every shape (scalar / 1d / 2d generic + 1d/2d/3d/4d param + 1d/2d state) plus `tensor_cast_dtype_bf16_mlx_streamed`. `OP_CAST_DTYPE` replay's scalar_arg encoding extended `0.0=f32, 1.0=f64, 2.0=bf16`. (3) `training/dtype_dispatch.cpp` and `training/dtype_init.cpp` route dtag 17 → bf16 across all 11 dispatch entries; the abort message now lists the three real storage dtypes (f32/f64/bf16) and points at torch for the wider surface. (4) `core/lifecycle/accessors.cpp` `tensor_dtype_name` returns `"BF16"` (was previously a binary F32-or-F64 branch — would lie about bf16); `tensor_to_floats` widens bf16 per-element. (5) `core/lifecycle/batch.cpp` `tensor_one_hot` routes dtag 17 to bf16. (6) `tape.h` `OP_CAST_DTYPE` doc updated. (7) `packages/idris-ml/src/Device/Mlx.idr` added `Compatible (MlxDev MGpu) BF16` and `Compatible (MlxDev MCpu) BF16` instances. Existing `Compatible (MlxDev MGpu) F64` deliberately stays missing (Metal has no fp64). (8) Behavioural test `legacy_backend::mlx_bf16_storage` (`packages/backends/test/common/test_legacy_backend.c`) asserts dtype name + readback tolerance + add preserves dtype + F32↔BF16 roundtrip — all 10 assertions PASS under `BACKEND=mlx MLX_DEVICE=gpu make test-backend-criterion-mlx` (194/194-43 = 151 PASS, 43 pre-existing failures unchanged). RED before this commit: `Can't find an implementation for Compatible (MlxDev MGpu) (BFloat 16)` on `MLX_DTYPE=BF16 BACKEND=mlx MLX_DEVICE=gpu make install`. **Note on rank-3 broadcast µs/op**: F32 mlx-gpu 53.75 / BF16 49.36 — within noise (~9%, well below the 20% VM noise threshold). At small tensor sizes mlx-gpu is kernel-launch-bound regardless of dtype; the BF16-vs-F32 runtime story at HfLlama-inference scale is still TBD pending the `MLX_DTYPE=BF16 BACKEND=mlx MLX_DEVICE=gpu` HfLlama measurement. The 72 hardcoded `mx::float32` constants in fused-op kernels (TODO "Audit mlx fused-op + constant pool dtype handling") are not addressed here — that audit was the larger original framing of this row, intentionally deferred. Cross-references: `packages/backends/backend_mlx/precision.h`, `Device/Mlx.idr:643-668`, `test_legacy_backend.c::mlx_bf16_storage`.


"Investigate rank-3 broadcast per-op cost on torch-mps" closed as refuted (2026-05-31, TODO #402, commits `40acffa` + `63f3847` + the closing commit). Four-layer microbench ladder built to verify the original premise that our wrapper costs ~10–26 ms/op on rank-3 broadcast muls vs PyTorch Python's ~2 ms/op: (a) `packages/backends/bench_rank3_broadcast.cpp` links libtorch directly (no FFI), (b) `packages/backends/bench_rank3_broadcast_wrapped.cpp` links `libidrisml.dylib` and calls `tensor_mul_torch` through the C wrapper, (c) `packages/idris-ml-examples/src/Example/RankBroadcastBench.idr` calls `primMul` through the typeclass-dispatched device instance (full Idris path), (d) `packages/idris-transformers/scripts/time_rank3_broadcast.py` matches in PyTorch Python. All on the same `mul([6, 32, 32], [6, 1, 32])` shape, warmup=10, measure=100. **All four lanes converge on 15–26 µs/op** on the MPS device (libtorch direct 25 µs, C wrapper 26 µs, Idris-level 24 µs, PyTorch Python 12 µs). The original "10–26 ms/op" figure was arithmetic, not measured: `runGenerate wall (~317 s) / total ops (~21K) ≈ 15 ms/op` collapses to "per-op cost" only if every op is the same size, but after `c09d374` (all-heads RoPE) the post-fix op mix has 32× more data per call so total compute is constant, op count drops 7×, and `wall/op_count` *appears* to rise. Conclusion: the rank-3 broadcast at every layer of our stack is already at libtorch's own floor. Originally-planned Commits 2A (slim `from_tensor`), 2B (MPS optimization flags), 2C (pre-materialize cos/sin) are all out of scope — none would harvest more than ~1 µs/op on a workload that's already at ~25 µs/op. Cross-backend benefit (per the user's session-opening flag): the µs/op measurements span tape (16), torch-mps (24), mlx-cpu (26), mlx-gpu (75 — kernel-launch bound). Microbenches stay as permanent regression gates for the layers they exercise. The actual HfLlama torch-mps gap to PyTorch Python is filed as a fresh Medium TODO row "HfLlama torch-mps decode wall: gap to PyTorch Python beyond op-count and rank-3 broadcast" with the new candidates: op-count reduction via more fusion (TODO #42 already on file), per-op cost on LARGER shapes (matmul-shaped microbench needed), and per-`ioRerun` smart-constructor overhead. Cross-references: `docs/develop/perf-changes.md` 2026-05-31 entries "Rank-3 broadcast microbench localises the gap to OUR wrapper" (Commit 1), "Wrapper-direct microbench rules out the C boundary" (Commit 1b), "Idris-level microbench refutes the '10 ms/op rank-3 broadcast' premise" (Commit 1c); `docs/develop/perf-log-ref.jsonl` Commit 1 entries.


Tape F32 HfLlama mid-decode crash fixed via `PrimIO Int` (closed 2026-05-31, TODO #401). Since commit `26a0d56` introduced `primPerfOpCount : PrimIO Bits64` for the #393 op-submission counter, `BACKEND=tape TAPE_DTYPE=F32 make example-hf-llama-inference` crashed with `invalid memory reference` / `Illegal instruction: 4` after ~step 8 of the 8-token greedy decode. The crash triggered specifically on the typeclass-routed `perfOpCount {d=ExampleDevice}` call inside `genLoop` — even though `tensor_perf_op_count` is a no-op stub on tape (`return 0`). Three hypotheses on file (TODO #401): (1) `PrimIO Bits64` shape, (2) typeclass dispatch, (3) Chez `unsigned-64` FFI marshalling. Tested the cheapest first: switched the FFI declaration from `PrimIO Bits64` to `PrimIO Int` in `Device/{Tape,Torch,Mlx}.idr`, the typeclass method in `Device/Core.idr`, the `perfOpCount` smart constructor in `Tensor.idr`, and the call-site type in `HfLlamaInference.idr`. The C side already returns `long` (= `int64_t` on macOS, which fits both Idris `Bits64` and `Int`), so no C change. Verified: `scripts/perf-run.sh hf-llama tape` on F32 now runs all 8 decode tokens to completion (runGenerate 1m 00s, all 8 `[perf] step N: 0 ops` lines printed). Hypothesis verdict: (1) confirmed as the trigger; (2) and (3) not independently isolated — Idris-2's chez codegen for `unsigned-64` returns through `PrimIO` in tight loops corrupts something on tape, exact mechanism unknown but `Int` (= `int64_t`) sidesteps it on the same workload. Documented as `docs/develop/gotchas.md` "`PrimIO Bits64` FFI returns corrupt state in tight loops — use `PrimIO Int`". Lesson: for FFI counters / sizes / handle indices returning values that fit in `Int64`, default to `PrimIO Int` not `PrimIO Bits64`. Unblocks tape F32 as a first-class lane for the #399/#402 op-count investigations. Cross-references: `docs/develop/perf-changes.md` 2026-05-31 entry; `perf-log.jsonl` 2026-05-31 tape F32 hf-llama success.


LLM-class showcase example shipped (closed 2026-05-29). The row "LLM-class example (Llama-7B-shape inference)" closes at the 1B scale. Working: `Example/HfLlamaInference.idr` loads `meta-llama/Llama-3.2-1B` (1.24 B params) from a HuggingFace `.safetensors` checkpoint, decodes 8 greedy tokens given "The capital of France is", prints "Paris" as the first token. Architecture pieces — RMSNorm (`Layer/RmsNorm.idr`), RoPE with Llama-3 NTK scaling (`Layer/RoPE.idr`, matches PyTorch within 1e-12), SwiGLU (`Layer/SwiGLU.idr`) — landed in core `idris-ml`; the HF-name-aligned adapter (per-block storage shapes, forward, param naming) landed in `packages/idris-transformers/src/HfLlama.idr` per the package's CONVENTIONS.md rule 3. Weight loading is plain `loadModel` (no remap table). Tokenizer integration via `packages/idris-transformers/src/Tokenizer.idr` + the Python `scripts/hf_tokenize.py` subprocess (a Rust FFI upgrade row stays open under perf, gated on user reports rather than as a correctness blocker). The mlx GPU > CPU showcase claim is demonstrated: mlx-gpu wall **45.5 s** vs mlx-cpu **2 m 10 s** on hf-llama (2.86× speedup; 20× pre-#394 fix). torch-mps F32 ~5 min (the deferred-op-tape row #399 explains why); tape F32 ~1 m 18 s (post #398). New deliverable doc `docs/develop/llm-inference.md` walks through model shape, the type-checking story (open `d : Device` + `dt : DType`, `Linked d` linkage gate, GQA's multiplicative-Nat type-level reductions, why `believe_me` doesn't appear, where `TVec`/`TMat` aliases dodge the type-checker hang), and the decode loop's `resetForEval` + `perfReset/perfOpCount` wiring. Caveats explicit in the doc: KV cache is filed as a separate row, 7B-class is out of scope on a 16 GB VM (re-investigate at ≥32 GB), tape F64 hf-llama OOMs (TODO #400 — use `TAPE_DTYPE=F32`), torch-mps dispatch ceiling (TODO #399). The four-piece deliverable in the original row — model impl, weight loading, forward-only inference, tokenizer — is met. Cross-references: `docs/users/idris-transformers.md` (user-facing), `packages/idris-transformers/CONVENTIONS.md` (CONVENTIONS for the package).


torch-mps per-op submission overhead diagnosed (closed 2026-05-29, commits `e41a011` + `26a0d56`). Two-phase work under TODO #393: (Phase 1, `e41a011`) guarded `tensor_embedding`'s `indices.to(kLong + weight.device())` on the identity case so the common path skips the per-forward `.to()` no-op submission — measured forward wall on torch-mps `hf-bert 1:27 → 41s` (-53%), `hf-gpt2 4:35 → 1:56` (-58%), `hf-llama 6:59 → 5:07` (-27%). (Phase 2, `26a0d56`) wired a per-forward op-submission counter into `from_tensor()` (the single choke point every per-op kernel passes through to wrap its `at::Tensor` result), exposed `perfReset` + `perfOpCount` on `UserDeviceTraining` with no-op stubs on tape + mlx, and printed `[perf] step N: K ops` lines from `HfLlamaInference`'s `genLoop`. Measured torch-mps Llama-3.2-1B F32: **~19,400 ops per 8-token forward** (linear at +297/token without KV cache) × **~1.89 ms/op average** dispatch wall = ~36.7 s/forward, matching the observed ~36.6 s wall to within rounding. **Structural ceiling confirmed** — libtorch's MPS path submits each op as its own `MTLCommandBuffer` and exposes no graph-capture / fusion primitive in C++ that we can call from the backend wrapper, so the only fix is wrapping every smart constructor to defer into our own tape and materialise as one batched submission at sync boundaries. That's an XL architectural change (touches every per-op wrapper plus autograd integration), filed as a new TODO row "torch-mps deferred-op tape (graph mode)". The diagnostic harness stays: future regressions to the per-op count are now visible without re-running the full investigation. Pairs with the planned KV cache work (halves the per-step op count to ~constant; KV cache + deferred-op tape is the path to torch-mps competitive Llama inference).


tape HfLlama exit-cleanup tail fixed (closed 2026-05-29, commit `ff483c0`). The follow-up row to #397 closes: `BACKEND=tape TAPE_DTYPE=F32 bash scripts/perf-run.sh hf-llama tape` wall **20m 52s → 1m 18s** (~16× faster, ~95% of wall removed), post-main tail **17m 23s → ~7s**. Root cause: `backend_release_all_persistent_tape` was the no-op pair `param_clear(); tape_reset();`. On tape, `param_clear` calls `tensor_release_handle` which is a literal no-op (params live in malloc'd buffers from `param_create.c` outside the bump arena; tape's handle ABI doesn't refcount), and `tape_reset` only zeroes per-chunk `used` counters in the bump arena (`arena.c`). So ~5 GB of malloc'd param storage plus the bump arena chunk list (last forward's intermediates) all lived until process exit, where macOS `libmalloc` walked them through a slow per-allocation teardown path. Fix runs the same work inside `main`, in three phases: (1) `tape_reset()` first — drains the autograd tape (frees per-entry `op_meta`, `inputs`, non-persistent grad arrays) while `e->result` pointers into the bump arena are still valid; (2) walks `param_registry_arr`, `free()`s each param's `data + shape + grad + struct`, then `param_clear()` to zero the count; (3) new `arena_free_all()` entry point (`arena.{h,c}`) walks the bump arena chunk list `free(c->data) + free(c)` per chunk and nulls `arena_head + arena_current`. No Idris-side change — `releaseAllPersistent` was already routed through the `UserDeviceTraining` typeclass to suffixed backend symbols when #394 landed; only the C body of `backend_release_all_persistent_tape` changes. Mechanism note: the `releaseAllPersistent done` stage stamp lands at the same second as `drain + GC done` (both at 1:11 of total 1:18), so the new free pass completes in well under a second despite freeing multi-GB of memory — the 17 min was paid by libc *at exit* (a slower allocator path), not by the work itself. Verified: tape BERT roundtrip `4.120473e-04` and GPT-2 roundtrip `2.570164e-05` unchanged; tape criterion 202/202 PASS; `make test` PASS; torch-mps BERT roundtrip `4.118532e-04` unchanged (untouched lane spot-check).


tape HfLlama OOM during decode fixed (closed 2026-05-29, commit `e924b5e`). The only existing arena-reset paths on tape were `optimizer_step` (training only) and explicit `tape_reset` calls. For multi-token greedy decode without a KV cache, every token re-runs the full 16-layer forward and allocates ~hundreds of MB into the arena; without a reset between tokens the arena grows unboundedly, hitting the 16 GB Tart VM ceiling around 4-8 tokens on Llama-1B F32 → SIGKILL by the OS OOM killer. New typeclass-routed primitive `resetForEval : {0 d : Device} -> UserDeviceTraining d => IO ()` on `UserDeviceTraining` wraps the existing C `backend_reset_for_eval` symbol — tape resets the arena + re-registers params on a fresh autograd tape; torch + mlx free intermediates + zero param grads (mild beneficial, no semantic change inside `withNoGrad`). Called from `genLoop` in `HfLlamaInference.idr` between tokens. **UNSAFE in training** (clobbers param grads); pure-inference path inside `withNoGrad` is safe. Verified: HfLlama-1.2B tape F32 wall `1m 55s (SIGKILL) → 20m 52s (exit 0)` — first successful tape-cpu Llama run. Output text "The capital of France is Paris. It advocating the French language and" — `Paris` correct; "advocating" gibberish past token 2 is F32 precision drift vs the BF16 reference on a 1.2 B model. The 17 min post-main tail is the same exit-cleanup behaviour the other CPU lanes show on large models (libc free of GB-scale arena chunks); filed as TODO #398.


Core `Layer/*` refactor to fused-init primitives (closed 2026-05-28, commits `61a9638` through `099a01f`). 14 layers + HfBert MLM decoder bias migrated from the old `traverse + packDoubles + tparam<rank>` / `fillConst + dtCreateParam<rank>` patterns to the fused-init surface (`tparam<rank>Normal` / `tparam<rank>Const`). The new primitives allocate + initialise in a single C-side call instead of filling per-element from Idris. Per-layer commits, topo order (constant-init layers first: **RmsNorm, LayerNorm, BatchNorm, HfBert.idr:715**; then random-init: **Embedding, Linear** (with NTM/DNC call-site updates), **SwiGLU, Rnn, Gru, Lstm, Conv** (1D + 2D), **Transformer, Ntm, Dnc**). The `Init` typeclass surface goes away for layer smart constructors — `mkLinearWith` now takes numeric `(weightStd, biasStd)` parameters; power users wanting non-normal distributions wire FFI directly. Xavier-uniform → its normal equivalent (`std = sqrt(2/(fan_in + fan_out))`) and PyTorch's `nn.Linear` default (`U(-1/sqrt(fan_in), 1/sqrt(fan_in))`) → `N(0, 1/sqrt(fan_in))` matches the new fused-init surface; the small distribution shift is below the noise floor of every training-from-scratch example. State tensors (running mean/var for BatchNorm, positional encoding + causal mask for Transformer, initialReadOut for NTM/DNC, etc.) keep the old `dtCreateState<rank>` path — no `tstate<rank>Const` / `tstate<rank>Normal` surface yet (filed as a follow-up TODO). All 14 commits build clean on tape; tape BERT/GPT-2 roundtrips unchanged (`4.120473e-04` / `2.570164e-05`). Multi-seed anchors in `docs/develop/reference-alignment.md` (GPT/LSTM/GRU/A2C/PPO) need re-anchoring against the new RNG values — deferred to a consolidated anchor-update commit (covers all 5 anchor blocks at once rather than 5 separate per-layer updates).


tape `tensor_cat2` now rank-preserving (closed 2026-05-28, commit `5524d87`). tape's `tensor_cat2` always emitted a rank-1 result regardless of input rank — `[1, hidden] ++ [1, hidden]` came back as `[2*hidden]` instead of `[2, hidden]`. The silent flattening broke HfLlama's `applyRmsNorm2d` row-fold which builds `[seq, hidden]` by repeated cat of `[1, hidden]` rows; downstream `primNarrow(qFull, 1, _, _)` then saw a rank-2 view of the (still-flat) buffer with `qFull->shape[0]=12288` instead of `6`, surfacing as `binop_elementwise: incompatible shapes op=3 a=[12288,32] b=[6,32]` on tape F32 hf-llama. Matched torch's `torch::cat({a,b}, 0)` / mlx's `mx::concatenate({a,b}, 0)` behaviour: concat along axis=0, preserve trailing dims, abort on rank or trailing-shape mismatch. Backward unchanged — `scalar_arg = a->numel` gives the correct flat-grad split offset for any rank under row-major. New criterion test in `test_cat2.c` asserts rank-2 + rank-2 → rank-2; the old impl would have returned rank-1. Verified: tape BERT roundtrip `4.120473e-04` + GPT-2 roundtrip `2.570164e-05` unchanged; tape F32 hf-llama now clears the broadcast crash. (Decode runs further but then hits a separate arena-allocator OOM on the 1.2B-param model — filed as TODO #397.)


HfLlama exit-cleanup hang fixed across CPU lanes (closed 2026-05-28, commits `81e3caa` + `3411c8c`). Inference programs that returned ~hundreds of MB of live tensor handles to `main` hit a post-main libtorch CPUAllocator destructor cascade of 14-22 minutes on the CPU lanes. Measured 2026-05-28 on HfLlama-1.2B BF16 torch-cpu: `runGenerate done` at 0:43, process exit at 23:22 — 22 min of pure teardown after `main` returned. Diagnostic instrumentation confirmed the work was C-side, not Idris-side (explicit `drainManagedHandles + forceMajorGc` at end-of-main fired in 0 s, leaving the full 22 min still in post-main territory). New typeclass-routed primitive `releaseAllPersistent : {0 d : Device} -> UserDeviceTraining d => IO ()` (Tensor.idr) with per-backend C implementations: **torch** runs `free_intermediates()` (the existing bulk-delete of the `intermediates_torch` vector, normally only fired by `optimizer_step`) then walks `param_registry_arr` deleting each `at::Tensor*`, then `param_clear()` to reset the registry count — **gated on `first->is_cpu()`** so the MPS/CUDA paths bail early (per-tensor `delete` on MPS forces a Metal stream sync; ~146 syncs at ~7 s each regressed torch-mps from 6:42 → 24:07, fixed in `3411c8c`); **mlx** is a no-op (initial attempt with `param_clear + mx::clear_cache` regressed mlx-cpu from 15:17 → 20:24 because `tensor_release_handle` on mlx only decrements refcount; the proper fix would expose `mlx_sweep_generation` publicly + walk `all_tensors`, but the Idris-side drain chain alone is enough — see below); **tape** runs `param_clear` + `tape_reset` (cheap, no per-tensor heap). Wiring: new `primReleaseAllPersistent` method on `UserDeviceTraining` (`Device/Core.idr`) + per-backend FFI bindings to the suffixed symbols (`backend_release_all_persistent_torch` etc) since the unified-name alias machinery was retired earlier in 2026-05. `HfLlamaInference.main` calls `drainManagedHandles + forceMajorGc + releaseAllPersistent` before `pure ()`; stage stamps confirm each phase fires. Measured outcome (HfLlama-1.2B): **torch-cpu BF16 23m22s → 1m21s** (95% faster), **mlx-cpu F32 15m17s → 2m10s** (86% faster), **torch-mps F32 6m42s → 7m56s** (within VM noise band), **tape unaffected** (didn't show the hang). Sibling gotcha in `docs/develop/gotchas.md` "Large-model inference needs explicit `releaseAllPersistent`".


Zero-dim BLAS guard landed for tape matmul-family kernels (closed 2026-05-28, commit `fde1827`). The original closure of TODO #395 — fixes the `cblas_sgemm: lda=0 K=0` crash that surfaced when running `BACKEND=tape TAPE_DTYPE=F32 bash scripts/perf-run.sh hf-llama tape` (the first `runGenerate` matmul on F32 hit a `[seq, 0]` slice from an empty KV-cache narrow → transpose `[0, seq]` → `cblas_sgemm` with `lda=0`, which Apple Accelerate rejects). New helper `tape_zero_tensor(shape, rank, dtype_tag, rg)` in `backend_tape/arena.{c,h}` returns a properly-shaped, fully zero-filled tensor; the 6 BLAS-backed kernels (`linear/linalg/{mm,mv,linear,linear_2d,bmm,bmm_3x3}.c`) now short-circuit to it when any of `{m, n, k}` (or `B`) is zero. `linear` / `linear_2d` preserve their bias-broadcast contract in the `n=0` case (matmul drops out, bias still adds). Constant-zero outputs skip `tape_append` — gradient through a zero matmul is identically zero so the backward chain stops harmlessly at the leaf. Verification: new criterion suite `test/common/linear/linalg/test_zero_dim_matmul.c` (12 tests covering all 6 kernels × F32/F64) RED before the fix (6 crashing + 6 grad-mismatch on tape), GREEN after. The F32 path uses the streamed `tensor_create_streamed(NULL, _, _, _, _, dtag=14)` entry — the legacy `tensor_create_f32` is a no-fp32-arena abort stub on tape. Same suite passes on torch (191/191) and mlx (192/192), pinning the cross-backend behaviour. End-to-end re-run: `BACKEND=tape TAPE_DTYPE=F32 bash scripts/perf-run.sh hf-llama tape` now clears the matmul guard, completes tokenizer + `hfLlamaModel` (16s) + load (36s) + RoPE, and crashes further downstream on a different bug (tape's `binop_elementwise` on `[1,2048] + [2048]` broadcast — separate row in TODO.md). No regression: tape BERT roundtrip `4.120473e-04` and GPT-2 roundtrip `2.570164e-05` unchanged. Paired script tweak (commit `e1f2fc1`) makes `compare_inference.py` skip `[stage]` diagnostic lines so the float-dump roundtrip harness stays clean against the stage-timed HF examples.


Fused param create + init ported to tape backend (closed 2026-05-28). The third and final Phase-7 follow-up row closes; tape now wires the `create_param_<rank>_<init>` + `set_init_seed` port slots. New file `packages/backends/backend_tape/training/dtype_init.c` ships an in-file xoshiro256++ PRNG (4 × uint64 state, seeded by splitmix64 expansion — BigCrush-passing, public domain, ~30 LOC) + Box-Muller paired-output normal sampler with one-sample cache. The 8 dtag dispatchers (`tape_create_param_<rank>_<normal,const>_dtag`) build a host doubles buffer + fill it on the C side, then route through the existing `tape_create_param_<rank>_dtag` for storage-variant dispatch, inheriting F64-direct / F32-real-storage (4-byte) / lingua-franca-retag-round behaviour for free. No new tape storage logic; the dtype_dispatch path already handles every dtag. Adapter wiring (`backend_tape/training/adapter.c`) adds 9 trampolines + 9 `g_active_port` slot fills, mirroring torch's adapter exactly. Determinism: process-global xoshiro state (single-threaded; param construction happens once at model build time, before any forward pass); default seed = 0 → splitmix64 well-mixed initial state. Verified: BERT roundtrip on `BACKEND=tape` PASS max-abs-diff `4.120473e-04` (matches mlx-cpu's `4.120473e-04` to the last digit, matches torch's `4.118532e-04` within F32 quant noise); GPT-2 roundtrip on `BACKEND=tape` PASS max-abs-diff `2.570164e-05` (within F32 noise of torch's `2.670288e-05`); Idris unit tests 189/189 PASS; C-side criterion T1..T32 PASS. The HF model values come from `loadModel` overwriting the init, so the RNG-provider switch doesn't affect output — the same property that held for the torch/mlx ports. With tape's port slots wired, all three backends now expose the fused-init surface; the loud-abort path is unreachable for `tensor_create_param_*_<init>_streamed` calls on any supported (BACKEND, MLX_DEVICE, TORCH_DEVICE) tuple.


CLAUDE.md rule — no expensive run without a perf log (closed 2026-05-28, commit `062adbb`). Added a paragraph under "Performance optimization workflow" stating that any inference / training run taking more than a few seconds — including ad-hoc verification, debugging, eyeballing, cross-backend smoke — MUST land in `docs/develop/perf-log.jsonl`. Operationalised: use `scripts/perf-run.sh <example> <backend>` instead of `make example-*` directly; if the example isn't wired into `perf-run.sh`, wire it in first (one-line case arm). Build-only invocations exempt; correctness gates (`test-hf-*-roundtrip`, etc.) need a paired `perf-run.sh` call. Surfaced after the `.contiguous()` Llama win on torch-mps (11:45 → 6:42) was measured by hand and not logged. The rule lives in `CLAUDE.md` "Performance optimization workflow" and a paired user memory `feedback_no_expensive_run_without_log.md`.


BF16 Llama inference on torch-mps via `TORCH_DTYPE` opt-in (closed 2026-05-28, commit `ab5386a`). New env knob `TORCH_DTYPE=BF16` overrides the per-cell default `(ExampleDevice, ExampleDType)` mapping in `BuildConfig.idr` for torch builds. Default unchanged: `BACKEND=torch TORCH_DEVICE=mps` still maps to `(TorchDev TMps, F32)`; opt-in switches to BF16. BUILD_KEY extends with `$(if $(TORCH_DTYPE),-tdt$(TORCH_DTYPE),)` so the cache trees don't collide. The deliberate `Compatible (TorchDev TMps) BF16` exclusion in `packages/idris-ml/src/Device/Torch.idr` (comment "MPS reduced-precision support is version-dependent and untestable in this VM") was stale — VM's libtorch has working BF16-MPS kernels for the HF model op set; instance restored. C-side: added `cast_and_migrate` (atomic cast + device migrate, sibling of `make_param_leaf`) and `make_state_persistent` (the state-tensor analogue) in `backend_torch/training/dtype_dispatch.cpp`; fixed all `default:` arms of `torch_create_state_{1d,2d}_dtag` (which previously called `tensor_cast_dtype_f64` cast-without-migrate, then inherited CPU placement on MPS via F64-fallback). The latent bug surfaced only on the BF16 path where state tensors got rerouted to the `default:` arm; cost was the Llama forward crashing with `Expected all tensors to be on the same device, mps:0 and cpu!` on the first state-vs-param op. Also fixed the embedding-side device mismatch (`embedding.cpp`: move indices to weight's device, not just cast). And one C++ `*/`-comment-end trap (`Int*/Bool` in a list closed the block); rewrote with commas. Measured outcome: Llama-3.2-1B inference on torch-mps, 8 greedy tokens, post-P1: `F32 8:43 → BF16 7:35` (~13% additional reduction, dominated by embedding + projection storage halving). End-to-end output text matches F32 within the first ~2 greedy tokens. mlx-gpu BF16 still blocked on mlx upstream (no BF16 storage in mlx 0.31); filed as deferred row. Design narrative in `docs/develop/design-decisions.md` "TORCH_DTYPE opt-in dtype override + BF16 torch-mps gate"; sibling gotcha in `docs/develop/gotchas.md` "State tensors need atomic cast+migrate on non-F32/F64 dtags".


HF examples wired into perf-run.sh + stage-line extraction (closed 2026-05-28, commit `e5c4bba`). Three new case arms in `scripts/perf-run.sh` for `hf-bert` / `hf-gpt2` / `hf-llama` mapping to the corresponding `example-hf-*-inference` targets with `AVAR=_HF_NOARGS` (no `--seed` / `--epochs` / `--lr` switches on inference examples). DEVICE column now records the actual torch device (`cpu` / `mps` / `cuda`) rather than always `cpu` for the torch lane. Added `torch_dtype` optional JSONL field for builds with `TORCH_DTYPE=BF16`. Stage-line parsing: any example that emits lines matching `^[stage] [HH:MM:SS] <label>` (HfBert/HfGpt2/HfLlama do this) has its stage timings extracted into JSONL `stages: [{label, elapsed_s}]`, so cross-stage perf comparisons across commits don't depend on conversation memory. Implementation: ~15 LOC of Python in the inline analyzer block, ~5 LOC of bash for the new case arms + DEVICE/TORCH_DTYPE_STATE wiring. Schema documented in `docs/develop/perf-log.md`. Retrofit motivated by the gap surfaced during the P1 torch-mps work (the `.contiguous()` win measurement was done via `make example-hf-llama-inference` direct, leaving no log entry).


Drop spurious `.contiguous()` in narrow / expand_mask / unbatch (closed 2026-05-28, commit `51df3cb`). Three sites in `backend_torch/` that called `.contiguous()` on view results in forward-path hot loops, forcing a Metal command-buffer submission per call to materialise tens of MB of strided view into row-major storage that no downstream op required. `linear/shape/narrow.cpp:11` (the smoking gun: ~16K such calls per Llama 8-token forward — RmsNorm row split × 128 + attention QKV × 12K + RoPE table × 4K); `linear/shape/expand_mask.cpp:8` (mask broadcast that downstream `masked_fill` / `+` accept strided); `core/lifecycle/batch.cpp` (`tensor_unbatch` — `unbind` slices along axis 0 are already memory-contiguous along that axis, the `.contiguous()` was a no-op that still queued a Metal submission). The remaining nine `.contiguous()` sites in `backend_torch/` are legitimate host-side reads (`accessors.cpp`, `optimizer.cpp` momentum/variance buffer load, `diagnostics.cpp` gradient-norm logging) that need row-major byte layout for `data_ptr<T>()` access — kept. mlx audit confirmed clean (all `mx::contiguous` calls are in host-side read paths). Verified: `test-hf-bert-roundtrip` PASS (max-abs-diff `4.118532e-04` unchanged); `test-hf-gpt2-roundtrip` PASS (`2.670288e-05` unchanged); `test-examples` torch lane clean. Measured outcome: torch-mps Llama-3.2-1B wall **11:45 → 8:43** (~26% reduction); BERT roundtrip wall **1:23 → 1:09** at the same scale. Sibling gotcha in `docs/develop/gotchas.md` "`.contiguous()` on a libtorch op result is never free".


Fused param create + init ported to mlx backend (closed 2026-05-28). The Phase-7 follow-up row "Port fused param create + init to MLX backend" closes same-day: new `packages/backends/backend_mlx/training/dtype_init.cpp` defines `tensor_create_param_{1d,2d,3d,4d}_{normal,const}_streamed` + `tensor_set_init_seed_streamed` directly (mlx isn't in `SHARED_BACKENDS_dtype_streamed`, so the in-file pattern matches mlx's existing `tensor_create_param_*_streamed` dispatchers). Forward kernels are mlx-native: `mx::random::normal(shape, dtype, mean, std)` for normal init, `mx::full(shape, value, dtype)` for const fill, `mx::random::seed(seed)` for the seed setter. No leaf-discipline rewrite needed — mlx arrays are immutable/functional, so the random/fill call returns a fresh initialised array in one mlx kernel; we just wrap in a `Tensor(arr, requires_grad=true)` and `tape_append(OP_CONST, ...)`, matching the existing `tensor_create_param_*_<dtype>_mlx_streamed` impls. dtag dispatch covers F32 + F64 (mlx storage); other dtags abort loud via the existing `mlx_dtype_unsupported` defence-in-depth backstop. `stream_tag` honoured via `WITH_STREAM` (CPU vs Metal). Verified end-to-end on both lanes via `test-hf-bert-roundtrip`: `BACKEND=mlx MLX_DEVICE=cpu` PASS at max-abs-diff `4.120473e-04` (matches torch's `4.118532e-04` within F32 noise); `BACKEND=mlx MLX_DEVICE=gpu` PASS at max-abs-diff `4.110634e-04`. Tape adapter still leaves the new port slots unwired — calls land in the loud-abort path until the tape-side follow-up row lands.


C-side fused param create + init — 158× speedup on Llama state construction (closed 2026-05-28). New FFI family `tensor_create_param_{1d,2d,3d,4d}_{normal,const}_streamed` + `tensor_set_init_seed_streamed` allocates the parameter tensor in the backend and runs an in-place init kernel (libtorch's `torch::nn::init::normal_` or `t.fill_`) instead of filling element-by-element on the Idris side via `traverse normalSample` + `packDoubles` (per-element `prim__setDouble` FFI). At Llama-3.2-1B scale that bottleneck dominated 88% of binary wall (57:53 / 1:05:28 of `hfLlamaModel` state construction on the previous run, dominated by ~30 min of Box-Muller in Chez + ~28 min of per-element FFI through 1.24B elements). Headline measurement (torch-mps, F32, prompt='The capital of France is', 8 tokens, post-refactor): `hfLlamaModel` stage **57:53 → 22 sec** (158× speedup), binary total **1:05:28 → 11:45** (5.6× faster end-to-end), identical output (`"The capital of France is Paris. It is the most populous city"`). Surface: shared `port.h` gained 8 fused-init function-pointer slots + 1 `set_init_seed` slot; `shared/training/dtype_streamed.c` got 9 trampolines that abort loudly at the FFI boundary if a backend hasn't wired the slots (per `feedback_no_torch_only_fallback` — not silent fallback); `backend_torch/training/dtype_init.cpp` (new file) implements the 8 dtag dispatchers via a templated `make_param_leaf_empty` helper mirroring the existing `make_param_leaf` leaf-discipline (cast/move BEFORE `requires_grad_` — required else `.grad` never populates). Idris side: 8 new `dtCreateParam<rank>{Normal,Const}` typeclass methods + 8 user-facing `tparam<rank>{Normal,Const}` smart constructors + `tsetInitSeed`. HfBert / HfGpt2 / HfLlama smart constructors (`makeBertLinear`, `makeGpt2Conv1D`, `makeLlamaLinear`, etc.) refactored to use the new primitives. Roundtrip gates stayed bit-identical (BERT max-abs-diff `4.118532e-04`, GPT-2 `2.670288e-05`, both unchanged from pre-refactor anchors — the model checkpoint load overwrites init values so the RNG-provider switch doesn't affect the output). Mlx and tape adapters leave the new port slots `nullptr`; runtime calls trip the loud-abort path until the paired Low-priority follow-up rows ("Port fused param create + init to MLX backend" / "…to tape backend") land. Cross-references: commits `b38e71c` (C side), `085348d` (Idris side + manifest + regen), `6cae799` (HfBert), `89349cd` (HfGpt2), `15af203` (HfLlama). Investigation memo `packages/idris-transformers/scripts/time_inference_llama.py` is the canonical perf harness; keep it around for future regressions.


Per-backend-set build cache (closed 2026-05-27). All build artifacts (ttc, installed library, dylib, example executables, stamps, `idris2 --build-dir` output) move under `build/$(BUILD_KEY)/` where `BUILD_KEY := <backend-list>-mlx<MLX_DEVICE>-torch<TORCH_DEVICE>` (e.g. `tape-mlxcpu-torchcpu`, `torch-mlxcpu-torchmps`, `tape-torch-mlxcpu-torchmps`). Each distinct `(BACKEND, MLX_DEVICE, TORCH_DEVICE)` tuple gets its own warm cache; `BACKEND=tape make test` ↔ `BACKEND=torch TORCH_DEVICE=mps make example-hf-llama-inference` no longer triggers cascading re-elaboration. **Phase 0 verification** (`/tmp/idris-mtime-test`): Idris 2 uses **source mtime** to decide "is this `.idr`'s own ttc stale" (re-elabs `Aux.idr` after a `touch`), but uses **interface hash** for downstream cascade (`Main.idr` does NOT re-elab when `Aux.idr` re-elabs to the same interface). So per-set ttc dirs suffice: cross-set switches do re-elab the four generated `.idr` files (HwConfig, HwDevices, BuildConfig, TestConfig — their mtimes bump because content differs across sets) for ~4 s, but downstream modules in idris-ml / idris-gym / idris-transformers / idris-ml-examples sit on per-set ttc that retains its interface-hash-matched freshness. Round-trip-back (`tape → torch → tape`) is near-free instead of 60 min. The 4× generated `.idr` recipes also gain write-if-different (cmp-then-mv) so same-set reruns within one BUILD_KEY don't bump mtimes either. Conventional `clean` now removes ALL backend sets' trees (plus `build-cov/` + legacy `.idris2/`), `clean-set` is the per-active-set niche utility, `clean-all` cascades to `clean-models` + removes `vendored/` + `data/` for full disk-space reclaim. Per-package install/build invocations pass `--build-dir $(CURDIR)/$(BUILD)/ttc-<pkg>`; example/test builds at repo root pass `--build-dir $(BUILD)` (folded into `IDRIS_FLAGS`); test-package builds gain per-set output dirs at `$(BUILD)/test-<pkg>/exec/<name>`. The `test-multi` recipe split into `test-multi` + `_test-multi-build` so the `BACKEND=torch,tape,mlx install` sub-make and the example-build sub-make resolve `$(BUILD)` / `$(LIB)` consistently under the multi-link set, not the outer-make's BACKEND context.


HfLlama module + Llama-3 RoPE + greedy-decode example (closed 2026-05-27). Third HF-aligned model, targeting `meta-llama/Llama-3.2-1B` (gated; ~2.5 GB BF16). 146-param catalogue matching HF on-disk exactly (`model.embed_tokens.weight`, `model.layers.{i}.self_attn.{q,k,v,o}_proj.weight`, `…input_layernorm.weight`, `…post_attention_layernorm.weight`, `model.layers.{i}.mlp.{gate,up,down}_proj.weight`, `model.norm.weight`; 1 embed + 16 layers × 9 + 1 final-norm, with `lm_head.weight` tied to `embed_tokens.weight`). State records mirror HF subtree (`LlamaAttentionState` with SEPARATE q/k/v/o linears — Llama doesn't fuse QKV the way GPT-2 does; K/V projections explicitly narrower than Q under GQA; `LlamaMlpState` for SwiGLU; `LlamaBlockState`; `LlamaModelState`). All linears bias-free (matches Llama's `nn.Linear(..., bias=False)`). **`Layer/RoPE.idr`** ships the Llama-3 NTK-aware rope_scaling formula (pure-Idris port of HF's `_compute_llama3_parameters` — three frequency bands: high-freq no-scale, low-freq /factor, mid-freq smooth interpolation) and the `[maxPos, headDim/2]` cos/sin table builder. Paired Python oracle (`scripts/save_rope_oracle.py`) emits inv_freq + cos/sin slices; 6 Idris unit tests pin the table values against PyTorch's F64 reference within 1e-12. `applyRope` rotation forward composes existing 2D primitives (`primNarrow` axis-aware + `primMul` + `primSub` + `primAdd` + `primConcat2dAxis1`) — no new C kernel needed. **`hfLlamaForward`** composes pre-norm RMS → multi-head causal attention (with GQA expand per query head sharing a KV-head projection slot, RoPE applied to Q and K) → residual → pre-norm RMS → SwiGLU MLP → residual; 16 layers, final RmsNorm, tied LM head reuses `embed_tokens.weight`. The originally-planned typed-wrapper layer in Tensor.idr (rank-3+ batched-attention ops) stays unbuilt — the per-head-loop pattern from HfBert / HfGpt2 generalises cleanly, so the existing 2D primitives are enough. **`Example/HfLlamaInference.idr`** compiles end-to-end and ships two modes (`--dump-final-hidden` for CI gate, default greedy generation with `--prompt` / `--num-tokens`). End-to-end Idris-vs-Python validation pending user HF_TOKEN + license-accept on huggingface.co. KV cache deferred to a follow-up row (no-cache prefill works for the example; each generated token reruns forward on the growing sequence). Two new gotchas captured in `docs/develop/gotchas.md`: (1) `total` is an Idris-2 keyword bites again in let-bindings; (2) Idris's Peano elaboration of large-Nat auto-proofs (`{auto qPrf : hidden = numHeads * headDim}` at Llama's 32 × 64 = 2048) chokes — dropped the auto-proofs from HfLlama's forward signatures, caller is now responsible for passing coherent dims.


Core layer primitives: RmsNorm + SwiGLU (closed 2026-05-26). Two new core layers in `packages/idris-ml/src/Layer/` for the Llama family. **`Layer/RmsNorm.idr`** — root-mean-square layer normalisation (Zhang & Sennrich 2019); composes `primMul` / `primSum` / `primMulScalar` / `primAddScalar` / `primSqrt` / `primDiv` with scalar broadcast through `primDiv` (verified on all three backends). One learnable param (`weight`), no bias; default `eps = 1e-5` (Llama 3 default); pass-through `applyRmsNormEps` for HfLlama to thread the config's exact `rms_norm_eps`. **`Layer/SwiGLU.idr`** — Llama's gated SiLU MLP block: three bias-free linear projections (`gate_proj` / `up_proj` / `down_proj`) + `tsilu`. `SwiGLUState` holds the three weight tensors directly (no LinearState wrapper — matches HF on-disk shape per CONVENTIONS rule 2). `LayerLike` instance via `SwiGLUStateAnyI` which pins `intermediate` at construction and exposes `(hidden, hidden)` to the LayerLike surface (mirrors how Dropout / LayerNorm / RmsNorm handle `i = o = n`). Tests in `test/src/Test/{RmsNorm,SwiGLU}.idr` value-pin against PyTorch F64 references — both layers match Idris F64 output bit-for-byte (max-abs-diff 0.0). **Deferred from this phase to Phase 4 (HfLlama)** by user direction: the typed-wrapper layer in `Tensor.idr` (`tnarrow` / `tcat` / `treshape3d` / `treshape4d` / `tbmm` / `tsoftmax3d` / `tmaskedFill` / `ttile` / `ttransposeLast2` / `tunsqueeze` / `tsqueeze`) + `Layer/RoPE.idr` — these are coupled with HfLlama's attention path and ship together with it rather than as separate Phase 3 commits. Layer.RmsNorm + Layer.SwiGLU are unblocked for any from-scratch Llama-shaped model regardless.


Typed tokenizer subprocess + BERT/GPT-2 retrofits (closed 2026-05-26). New `packages/idris-transformers/src/Tokenizer.idr` wraps HuggingFace's Python `AutoTokenizer` via a subprocess shim (`scripts/hf_tokenize.py`, three subcommands: `vocab` / `encode` / `decode`). The Idris API is fully typed: `Tokenizer vocab` GADT carries the vocab size at the type level; `mkTokenizer` probes the on-disk vocab and validates against the type-level claim (surfaces `TokVocabMismatch` if mismatched); `tokenize : Tokenizer vocab -> String -> IO (Either TokError (n ** Vect n (Fin vocab)))` returns IDs with statically-bounded values + runtime-determined-but-type-tracked length; `detokenize` round-trips. Feeding GPT-2 IDs to a BERT model is now a *type error* at the call site, not a silent run-time bug. 6 unit tests in `Test/Tokenizer.idr` pin IDs across BERT WordPiece (vocab=30522), distilgpt2 BPE (vocab=50257), and assert vocab-mismatch detection. Retrofits: `Example/HfBertInference.idr` drops its hardcoded `[101, 3000, ...]` ID lists + the `vocab.txt` lookup table, accepts arbitrary string prompts via the tokenizer; same predictions (france/paris/spain/madrid/brussels), same `test-hf-bert-roundtrip` gate at max-abs-diff 4.12e-4. `Example/HfGpt2Inference.idr` swaps target from `hf-internal-testing/tiny-random-gpt2` (random init, ~150 KB) to `distilgpt2` (~350 MB pretrained, 6 layers / hidden 768) — the example now produces real generated text ("The quick brown foxes are a common sight in the wild", etc.) via a greedy-decode demo and keeps the `--dump-final-hidden` CI gate passing at max-abs-diff 2.57e-5. The Python-subprocess backing has ~1 s per-call startup; a future row in TODO.md tracks the Rust-FFI upgrade in a new `packages/idris-tokenizers/` package if perf or a non-HF use case forces the issue (API is Liskov-substitutable so callers wouldn't change). Investigation note: traced through the nixpkgs idris2 v0.8.0 `bootstrap-stage2.sh` patch (`MAKE all` → `MAKE idris2-exec`) as a candidate fix for the large-Nat-pattern OOM that surfaced while writing the tokenizer tests; ruled out — the stdlib `.ttc` files are built by the *separate* `mkPrelude.nix` derivations using the stage-2 binary, so the patch doesn't change stdlib quality. Full memo in `gotchas.md`'s "Pattern-matching Nat literals" entry.


HfGpt2 module shipped — second HF-aligned model after BERT (closed 2026-05-26). `packages/idris-transformers/src/HfGpt2.idr` covers the GPT-2 family; `Example/HfGpt2Inference.idr` loads `hf-internal-testing/tiny-random-gpt2` (vocab=1000, hidden=32, n_layer=5, n_head=4, head_dim=8, intermediate=128 [= 4 × hidden; the `intermediate_size: 37` in config.json is a stray BERT-style field GPT-2 ignores when `n_inner` is null], max_pos=512; ships safetensors on disk where `sshleifer/tiny-gpt2` does not) and matches HF transformers' Python forward output to **max-abs-diff 1.06e-6** on the 32-dim last-position hidden state (`make test-hf-gpt2-roundtrip` is the cross-language gate, wired into the Ubuntu CI lane alongside the BERT one). Two HF quirks validated that the BERT module deliberately doesn't exercise: (1) **fused QKV** — `transformer.h.{i}.attn.c_attn.weight` is one `Tensor [hidden, 3*hidden]` stored fused, split via `primNarrow` along axis=1 at forward; (2) **Conv1D transpose wart** — HF stores `c_attn` / `c_proj` / `mlp.c_fc` / `mlp.c_proj` as `[in, out]` (the transpose of `nn.Linear`'s `[out, in]`), so the module bypasses `tlinear2d` for a module-local `applyConv1D2d` doing `y = x @ W + bias` via `primMm` + `primAdd` broadcast. 64 params total; 16/16 unit tests pass; param-name catalogue exact-matches HF on disk; FFI registry asserts the constructor adds the same 64 names. Side-fix in the same epic: the latent axis=1 narrow bug on torch + mlx (kernels had `(void)dim;` then flattened to axis-0) was hidden by BERT's 1e-3 oracle tolerance — now pinned by `linear_shape_narrow::axis1_correctness_rank2` in the common-backend Criterion suite; both backends carry real multi-axis narrow + correct backward scatter. Follow-ups (in `TODO.md`): tokenizer integration (Python-subprocess shim with typed `Tokenizer vocab` handle); HfLlama (Llama-3.2-1B base) — needs RMSNorm + RoPE + SwiGLU + KV cache in core first.


HuggingFace checkpoint compat landed (closed 2026-05-26). Rather than the originally-planned generic remap + shape-split machinery, idris-ml now ships a separate `packages/idris-transformers/` package mirroring HuggingFace's `transformers` Python layout: one Idris module per HF architecture, with param names and storage shapes matching HF on-disk exactly, so loading is plain `loadModel "model.safetensors"` from `Checkpoint`. First module `HfBert.idr` covers the BERT family; `Example/HfBertInference.idr` loads `google/bert_uncased_L-2_H-128_A-2` and matches HF transformers' Python forward output to **max-abs-diff 4.12e-4** on the 128-dim pooled `[CLS]` output (`make test-hf-bert-roundtrip` is the cross-language gate, wired into the Ubuntu CI lane). Side-fix in the same epic: tape's `tensor_narrow` was hardcoded to axis=0 + rank=1, fired BLAS K=0 crashes from any axis=1 narrow on a 2D tensor (which multi-head attention does per-head); now dispatches on (rank, dim) with a copy-based axis-1 path for 2D and proper backward scatter for both axes. Conventions for `Hf*` modules locked down in `packages/idris-transformers/CONVENTIONS.md`; user-facing guide at `docs/users/idris-transformers.md`. Follow-ups (in `TODO.md`): `HfGpt2` module (fused QKV via `c_attn` + Conv1D transpose wart), and `HfLlama` (folded into the LLM-class example row; needs RMSNorm/RoPE/GQA/SwiGLU in core first).


Post-refactor perf sweep + transformer mlx-gpu crash re-measurement (closed 2026-05-25). Full canonical sweep at `7633a54` covering 6 examples × 5 cells (tape / torch-cpu / torch-mps / mlx-cpu / mlx-gpu); all 30 cells within ±10% of the prior `54c8dba` baseline (VM-noise envelope per `feedback_vm_perf_noise`), confirming the multi-link refactor + `primCreate1d` retire (`f7a690b`) + 5-sibling dtype-blind creator retire (`f29ae47`) + torch-mps streamed-path migration (`8507e50`, `c7cfd5b`, `d1beb7c`) were perf-clean. **One visible improvement**: transformer / mlx-gpu now produces a number (3.11× PyTorch, 89.58 ms) where the prior snapshot recorded `crashed`. Investigation note on the residual TODO row 42 crash rate: re-measured at 1% (6/600 runs, well below the originally-recorded 4%); three "touch ArrayDesc fields beyond eval" fix attempts (post-eval `detach()`, post-eval `wait()`, husk-then-delete) each made the rate WORSE (5-5.75%), so the corruption is created earlier — eval is forgiving of `Status::available` arrays with stale `inputs/siblings/primitive` chains, while detach/wait reach into those fields and trip the stale-shared_ptr. Refreshed snapshot in `docs/develop/perf-baseline.md`; full per-cell delta table + investigation findings in `docs/develop/perf-changes.md` 2026-05-25 entry. TODO row 42 updated with the new measured rate + failed-fix log + investigation continuations.


Retire 5 sibling dtype-blind `UserDeviceTraining` creators (closed 2026-05-25). Companion to f7a690b's `primCreate1d` retire. The remaining dtype-blind training-interface creators — `primCreateParam{1,2,3}d` + `primCreateState{1,2}d` — had zero non-interface Idris callers (every layer + tparam* helper already routes through `dtCreateParam*` / `dtCreateState*`), but their existence let any future caller default to F64 storage regardless of `ExampleDType`. Removed in one paired commit: 5 interface methods in `Device/Core.idr`; 5 method assignments + 5 FFI bindings in `Device/Tape.idr`; 5 method assignments + 5 dead FFI bindings in `Device/Torch.idr`; 5 method assignments + 5 legacy F64-only `_MlxStreamed` FFI bindings in `Device/Mlx.idr`. C-side: renamed the tape unsuffixed creators in `training/param_create.c` to their `_f64` counterparts and dropped the now-redundant aliases in `per_dtype_legacy.c`; dropped the unsuffixed creators from `backend_torch/core/lifecycle/create_param_state.cpp` (the `_f64` versions in `dtype_dispatch.cpp` already have real impls with HW migration from `8507e50`); dropped the unsuffixed + legacy `_mlx_streamed` creators from `backend_mlx/core/lifecycle/create_param_state.cpp`. Tests: migrated ~30 call sites across `test_legacy_backend.c`, `test_param_registry.c`, `test_optimizers.c`, `test_ntm_grad.c`, `test_safetensors.c`, `bench_ops.c` to the `_f64` suffix. Dropped 6 unsuffixed decls from `backend.h`; rename headers regenerated (230 → 224 exports). RED before this commit: 30+ references to unsuffixed `tensor_create_{param,state}_*` plus the 5 Idris-side `primCreateParam*` / `primCreateState*` interface methods, FFI bindings, and method assignments per backend. GREEN after: 0. Verified: tape F64 byte-identical anchor preserved (`Example.Supervised seed=42 epochs=1000 → loss=0.13801130234059747`); transformer on torch-mps still passes (`sort_acc=6/6`); tri-link clean. The torch-mps streamed-path migration prerequisite that gated this work landed in `8507e50` + `c7cfd5b`.


`TORCH_DEVICE=mps` streamed-path HW migration (closed 2026-05-25). Bug surfaced 2026-05-25 while auditing the legacy `primCreate1d` retirement (`f7a690b`): the streamed creation path on torch (`dtCreateParam*` / `dtCreate*` → `primCreate*Streamed` → `tensor_create_*_streamed_torch` → `g_active_port.create_*` → `torch_create_*_dtag` → `tensor_create_*_{f32,f64}` / `make_param_leaf`) had no device-migration step, so every layer parameter and input created via the streamed path silently stayed on CPU under `BACKEND=torch TORCH_DEVICE=mps`. The legacy non-streamed `UserDeviceCore.primCreate` + `UserDeviceTraining.primCreate{Param,State}*` methods DID migrate (explicit `prim__toDeviceTorch` wrap), but had zero non-interface callers — they were dead-but-correct paths. Probe at the creator return confirmed: `dtype=Float device=cpu` before the fix; `dtype=Float device=mps:0` after. The perf-baseline observation "torch-cpu and torch-mps tie at this scale" (`docs/develop/perf-baseline.md:385`) was consistent with this: tied because both were effectively CPU. **Fix** in three commits: (1) `8507e50` adds a process-wide `c10::Device g_torch_target_device` set at dylib load from `TORCH_DEVICE` env (cpu / mps / cuda[:N], default `at::kCPU`); `make_param_leaf` combines cast + move into a single `.to(opts)` call before `requires_grad_` (preserving the leaf-discipline); F32 + F64 non-param creators (`tensor_create_{scalar,,_1d,_2d}_{f32,f64}` in `dtype_dispatch.cpp` + the `_impl`s in `create_scalar.cpp` and `create.cpp`) migrate after creation; state creators (`tensor_create_state_{1,2}d_{f32,f64}`) likewise. (2) `c7cfd5b` extends the migration to `tensor_one_hot` — its accessor<>-cell-write requires CPU storage during construction, so the migration is the same cast-then-move step at the end. The first downstream op on a one-hot target (typically the loss's elementwise mul against the F32-on-MPS prediction) was aborting with "Expected all tensors to be on the same device, mps:0 and cpu" before this. **Verification**: tape F64 byte-identical anchor preserved (`Example.Supervised seed=42 epochs=1000 → loss=0.13801130234059747`); torch-cpu F64 anchor preserved (same); torch-mps end-to-end on rnn / lstm / transformer / gpt / ntm-copy (NTM-copy was a `crash` cell in the perf-baseline; now reaches 95.4% bit accuracy). The 5-sibling retirement (`primCreateParam{1,2,3}d` + `primCreateState{1,2}d`) that was blocked on this fix is now unblocked — filed as a fresh refactor row.


Retire legacy `primCreate1d` (F64-default dtype-blind FFI) (closed 2026-05-25). Follow-up to the `f7354bd` `primCreate1d` → `dtCreate1d` call-site migration. The dtype-blind `primCreate1d` interface method on `UserDeviceTraining` was the bug class itself: any future caller would silently default to F64 storage regardless of `ExampleDType` and break again under `MLX_DEVICE=gpu`. Removed in one paired commit: the `UserDeviceTraining.primCreate1d` interface method in `Device/Core.idr`, the three `prim__create1d{Tape,Torch,Mlx}` Scheme FFI bindings, the three `primCreate1d = …` instance assignments. C-side: dropped the unsuffixed `tensor_create_1d` from each backend (`backend_torch/core/lifecycle/create_param_state.cpp`, `backend_mlx/core/lifecycle/create_param_state.cpp`, `backend_tape/core/lifecycle/lifecycle_ext.c`); flipped the F64 alias `tensor_create_1d_f64` from a one-line wrapper into the real implementation in torch + tape (mlx already had `tensor_create_1d` aliasing `_f64`, so the deletion sufficed). Dropped the legacy decl from `backend.h`; rename headers regenerated (230 → 230, just lost the now-orphan unsuffixed entry). The streamed surface `tensor_create_1d_streamed(..., int dtag)` — the only one Idris-side `dtCreate1d` calls — is unchanged. Pre-removal RED: 11 references to `primCreate1d` / `tensor_create_1d` in `packages/`; post-removal: 0. Verified: tape, torch, mlx, and tri-link builds clean; F64 byte-identical anchor `Example.Supervised seed=42 epochs=1000 → loss=0.13801130234059747` preserved; Idris unit tests green. Follow-up open for the six sibling dtype-blind `UserDeviceTraining` creators (`primCreate2d`, `primCreateParam{1,2,3}d`, `primCreateState{1,2}d`) — same footgun shape, zero non-interface callers, but each removal is independent.


Post-Phase-6 perf sweep (closed 2026-05-25). Full canonical sweep on an idle Tart VM after the harness `|| true` silent-failure fix (`eff8737`) and the transformer F64 leak fix (`f7354bd`). 29/30 cells inside the ±15% VM-noise envelope vs the pre-Phase-6 baseline at `461ad12`; one new transformer/mlx-gpu crash at 200 epochs (`Exception: invalid memory reference`) filed as a TODO row — distinct from the F64-on-Metal crash that this session fixed. The morning sweep on the same day was invalidated by concurrent editing + the harness silent-failure (which substituted stale binaries for lstm/mlx, faking a 40× speedup). Refreshed snapshot lives in `docs/develop/perf-baseline.md`, full delta table in `docs/develop/perf-changes.md` 2026-05-25 entry.


Perf-sweep harness — surface make failures (closed 2026-05-25). `scripts/perf-sweep.sh:128` (+ torch/tape variants at lines 132/136) used to end each `make ... --no-print-directory` call with `>/dev/null 2>&1 || true`. A failing build was silent and the next stage ran whatever binary was left in `build/exec/<name>` from the previous cell — producing fake ratios that looked like real backend speed swings. The 2026-05-25 morning sweep reported `lstm/mlx-cpu` as 2.99 ms/epoch (40× faster than baseline) because the mlx cell silently re-ran the torch binary built moments earlier. Capture stderr to a temp log, return the make exit code, and have `measure_idris` emit `crashed` on build failure with a `[BUILD FAIL]` diagnostic line to stderr. The cell renderer already handled `crashed` correctly.


Transformer mlx crashes — F64 leak from dtype-blind creator (closed 2026-05-25). The 2026-05-25 perf sweep surfaced two transformer/mlx crashes both rooted in the same bug: `Generate.idr` (reversalTensorPoint + sortingTensorPoint) and `Example/Gpt.idr` (three sites) used the legacy `primCreate1d` interface method to build token-input tensors. `primCreate1d` routes through the non-streamed `tensor_create_1d_<b>` path and defaults to F64 storage regardless of `ExampleDType`. Under `MLX_DEVICE=gpu` (where the build pins ExampleDType to F32 because Metal is F32-only), pushing an F64 buffer onto the GPU stream aborted with `std::invalid_argument: float64 is not supported on the GPU`. Under `MLX_DEVICE=cpu` the F64 buffer mixed with F32 params and SIGTRAP'd inside an op's dtype-mismatch guard. Migrated the five call sites to `dtCreate1d {d=ExampleDevice} {t=ExampleDType} ... (deviceStreamTag {d=ExampleDevice})` — the same dtype-aware path the state/param creators already use. On tape (F64) and torch the generated tensor stays F64, so behaviour is unchanged. Follow-up TODO row filed to retire the dtype-blind `primCreate1d` interface method entirely so future callers can't trip the same footgun.


tape `tensor_view` chain segfault (closed 2026-05-25). The Criterion-port `legacy_backend::tensor_view` test failed with a NULL-pointer crash on tape after `optimizer_step` → `tape_reset` → `arena_reset`. Two aliasing paths: (1) the arena bump-pointer rewind reissued the parent tensor's own struct address back through `tensor_select`, then `memset` zeroed the parent mid-function; (2) even after a defensive struct snapshot, the next `arena_alloc(sizeof(int))` for `r->shape` could reissue the bytes where the param's data buffer lived. Fix at the source: `tensor_create` with `requires_grad=1` now `calloc`s the Tensor + `malloc`s data + shape + sets `persistent=1`, matching `tensor_create_param_*`'s lifecycle. Grad-bearing user tensors live outside any arena address range; the aliasing can't fire. The lighter arena path remains for `requires_grad=0` per-epoch inputs. Defensive snapshot in `tensor_select` kept for any future arena-resident grad-bearing path. Drops `.disabled = 1` on the test. Verified GREEN: tape 158/158, torch 149/149, mlx 148/148. F64 byte-identical anchor preserved. Commit `6578b81`.


mlx `avg_pool2d` backward distribution (closed 2026-05-25). `OP_AVG_POOL2D`'s replay closure in `backward.cpp` hardcoded `kH=kW=sH=sW=2` (with a comment admitting "only k=2 s=2 common case tested"). For the test input `[[1,2],[3,4]]` pooled with a 2×2 kernel stride-1, the hardcoded stride-2 produced non-overlapping windows and accumulated gradients on the slice+add ladder — returning `[0.25, 0.5, 0.5, 1.0]` instead of the expected uniform `[0.25, 0.25, 0.25, 0.25]`. Mirrored the `max_pool2d` / `avg_pool1d` meta-driven pattern: introduced `AvgPool2DReplayMeta` (C, H, W, kH, kW, strH, strW, oH, oW) attached on `tape_append` in `avg_pool2d.cpp`, decoded in the replay switch, freed in `tape_reset`. Drops `.disabled = SKIP_ON_MLX` on the test. Commit `5d4f58e`.


mlx softplus backward returned 0 at x=0 (closed 2026-05-25). mlx's `tensor_softplus` forward keeps the numerically-stable `max(0,x) + log(1 + exp(-|x|))` composite, correct for all `x`. But the same form in the `OP_SOFTPLUS` replay closure passed into `mx::vjp` produced `d/dx softplus(0) = 0` instead of `sigmoid(0) = 0.5`: the `mx::maximum(a, 0)` term is non-differentiable at `a=0` and mlx picks subgradient 0 there. Switched the replay-only path to the smooth `log(1 + exp(a))` form, differentiable everywhere with gradient `sigmoid(a)`. Forward unchanged. Drops `.disabled = SKIP_ON_MLX` on the test. Commit `831c6f7`.


mlx `conv1d_circular` forward kernel reversal (closed 2026-05-25). Circular convolution applies the kernel in reverse: `out[i] = sum_j in[(i - k/2 + j + n) % n] * kernel[k-1-j]`. Tape shipped the standard reversed-kernel form; mlx applied weights in forward order (`j` instead of `k-1-j`), producing `[1.1, 1.4, 1.1]` for the documented case (`in=[1,2,3], k=[0.1,0.2,0.3]`) instead of `[1.3, 1.0, 1.3]`. Backward gradients happened to coincide because the reverse-order forward cancels out across the per-`j` accumulation when summed with `d_out=[1,1,1]`. The replay closure carried the same bug; fixed both. Drops `.disabled = SKIP_ON_MLX` on the test. Commit `83d87c9`.


mlx backward dispatch — per-op file ownership (closed 2026-05-25). `backend_mlx/training/backward.cpp`'s 354-line forward-replay switch (60 `case OP_*:` arms inside the closure passed to `mx::vjp`) lifted into per-op functions sitting alongside their forward kernels, mirroring `backend_tape`'s `TAPE_REGISTER_OP` pattern. New infrastructure: `backend_mlx/training/autograd/op_dispatch.{h,cpp}` with the `MLX_REGISTER_REPLAY` macro and the function-pointer table indexed by `OP_*`. Each per-op `.cpp` now registers a `static void mlx_replay_<op>(std::vector<mx::array>& pool, TapeEntry& e)` at file scope; the closure inside `tensor_backward` shrinks to a 3-line dispatch loop. 61 ops migrated across 54 files (shared-body cases like `OP_MM`/`OP_BMM`/`OP_BMM_3X3` collapsed into one function with three REGISTER macros). Bit-identical convergence verified post-cutover (mlx rnn seed=42 epochs=20 → `loss=0.13426678042376022` matched pre-cutover), tri-link green, F64 anchor preserved. Three commits: Phase 1 scaffold, Phase 2 generated-and-committed registrations, Phase 3 cutover + switch deletion. One-shot generator script (`scripts/migrate-mlx-replay.py`) deleted in Phase 3.


Vendor cJSON out of VCS (closed 2026-05-25). `packages/backends/cJSON.{c,h}` (3,143 lines of canonical v1.7.18, MIT) moved to `vendored/cJSON/` and gitignored. Makefile rule fetches the pinned URL + verifies SHA256 on first build (and on demand thereafter); the cached copy is reused for offline rebuilds. Removes ~3.1k lines of vendored upstream from clone size and from `find . -name *.c | xargs wc -l` rankings; safetensors compile path picks up the new include via `-I$(CJSON_DIR)`. New target: `make vendor-deps`.


Modular backends — Phase 6 per-op split for torch + mlx (closed 2026-05-25). The structural split deferred during Phases 3-4 of TODO row 32, finishing the original plan's per-op-file goal. ~25 paired commits over multiple sessions; one logical extraction per commit, each maintaining F64 byte-identical + tri-link green (`BACKEND=tape,torch,mlx`) + Criterion suites green throughout.

Final monolith shrinkage (Phase 6 baseline → close):

| File                  | Start  | End  | Reduction |
|-----------------------|--------|------|-----------|
| `backend_torch.cpp`   | 2,323  |   62 |   -97%    |
| `backend_mlx.cpp`     | 4,655  |   78 |   -98%    |
| Total                 | 6,978  |  140 |   -98%    |

Both files are now header-comment skeletons that index into their per-op trees; all definitions live under `packages/backends/backend_<b>/`, mirroring the tape backend's tree adopted in Phases 1-4.

Per-op tree shape (identical between `backend_torch/` and `backend_mlx/`):

```
backend_<b>/
├── tensor.h          (Tensor handle helpers / Tensor struct)
├── tape.{h,cpp}      [mlx only] OP_* enum + ReplayMeta + tape_append/reset
├── precision.h       [mlx only] F32↔F64 + scalar_like helpers
├── stream.h          [mlx only] cpu_stream / gpu_stream / WITH_STREAM
├── init.cpp          [mlx only] mlx_backend_init + terminate gate
├── mps_init.cpp      [torch only] MPS allocator eager-init constructor
├── device.cpp        tensor_to_device + tensor_device
├── core/
│   ├── lifecycle/    create/cast/clone/free/item{,1d,2d}/accessors/
│   │                 lifecycle_core/create_param_state/batch/view
│   ├── elementwise/  add/sub/mul/div/neg/abs/exp/log/sqrt/pow/sigmoid/
│   │                 tanh/softplus
│   ├── scalar/       add_scalar/mul_scalar/clamp_min
│   └── backend_meta.cpp
├── linear/
│   ├── linalg/       matmul/mv/mm/linear/dot/outer/bmm/transpose/tile
│   ├── reduction/    sum/mean/min/max[/sum_dim]
│   ├── shape/        reshape/squeeze/narrow/select/expand_mask
│   ├── concat/       cat/stack/concat_2d_axis1
│   ├── index/        gather/scatter_add
│   └── sort/         argsort/cumprod
├── nn/
│   ├── softmax/      softmax/log_softmax
│   ├── mask/         masked_fill
│   ├── norm/         layer_norm/batch_norm/group_norm/dropout
│   ├── attention/    cross_attention/embedding/cosine_similarity
│   ├── loss/         bce_with_logits/cross_entropy/mse_loss
│   ├── recurrent/    lstm_cell/lstm_gates_pair/gru_cell/pair_helpers
│   └── activation/   gelu/leaky_relu/silu
├── conv/             conv1d{,_circular}/conv2d{,_batched}/conv_transpose/
│                     conv_grouped/max_pool1d/max_pool2d{,_batched}/
│                     avg_pool1d/avg_pool2d
└── training/
    ├── optimizer.cpp        Adam / AdamW / RMSprop / SGD + clip
    ├── adapter.cpp          BackendPort + per-backend port shims
    ├── dtype_dispatch.cpp   dtag → torch::ScalarType / mx::Dtype
    ├── autograd.cpp         grad/detach/no_grad_begin/end
    ├── backward.cpp         [mlx only] replay-based tensor_backward (~570 LOC)
    ├── diagnostics.cpp      DEBUG_LSTM_TRAJ + DEBUG_PARAM_GRADS
    ├── intermediates.cpp    [torch only] intermediates list + from_tensor
    ├── ntm_specific.cpp     tensor_lstm_gates + subtract_scalar_inplace
    ├── profiling.{h,cpp}    prof_*_ms counters + _wall_ms_<b>
```
