```
    └── tensor_pair.cpp      [torch only — fold may follow]
```

Key invariants held through every commit: F64 byte-identical (`Example.Supervised seed=42` → `loss=0.13801130234059747` on tape and mlx), tri-link clean (single dylib with three backends linked), Criterion suite full pass on all three backends (tape 157/157, torch 148/148, mlx 144/144).

Subphase highlights:
- **Phase 6.0** Scaffolding: `tensor.h` + per-TU Makefile rules per backend.
- **Phase 6a** Core: ~30 ops × 2 — elementwise / scalar / lifecycle.
- **Phase 6b** Linear: ~30 ops × 2 — linalg / reduction / shape / concat / index / sort.
- **Phase 6c** NN: ~20 ops × 2 — softmax / mask / norm / attention / loss / recurrent / activation.
- **Phase 6d** Conv: ~11 ops × 2 — conv1d/2d + pools + transpose + grouped.
- **Phase 6e** Training: optimizer / adapter / diagnostics / ntm_specific / intermediates / profiling / dtype_dispatch / backward (mlx) / autograd × 2.
- **Phase 6f** Device + mps_init + tape mechanics + init + lifecycle_core + backend_meta × 2.

Symbol-collision class encountered repeatedly (tri-link surfaces it): once a previously-file-static global needed to be exposed for an extraction (`prof_*_ms` counters, `all_pairs`, `no_grad_depth`, etc.), the other backend's same-named non-static global collided at the link step. Resolved consistently by `_<backend>` suffix renames on the newly-exposed copy. The `feedback_no_torch_only_fallback` rule applied: never time-box convergence on one backend's failure — fix the collision symmetrically. Documented in commit messages alongside each affected extraction.

No behaviour change: per-op ops still call the same libtorch / mlx / tape primitives in the same order. Phase 6 is a pure structural refactor — the navigability win without the (intentional) numerical risk that would come from rewriting kernels.

The shared/training surface (port + adapter pattern) remains the same as Phase 3-4: each backend's `adapter.cpp` populates `g_active_port` with backend-specific `tensor_port_*` shims; the shared TUs (`param_registry.c`, `optimizer.c` for tape, `ffi_shims.c`, `dtype_streamed.c`) call into the port for tensor introspection + element access. The per-backend optimizers (torch's `at::_foreach_*` Adam, mlx's mx::compile Adam) stay backend-local since their fused kernels can't lift to a port.

Modular backends closeout — Phase 5 of TODO row 32 (closed 2026-05-24). Eight commits tying up the loose ends from the cross-backend port adoption:
- **`test_backend.c` retired** (commit `6da7551`): the 4381-line monolithic harness becomes one Criterion suite (`packages/backends/test/common/test_legacy_backend.c`, 605 lines after stripping the dispatcher + main() and re-mapping ASSERT_NEAR/ASSERT_TRUE to single-evaluation `cr_assert_*` shims). All 72 `static void test_X(void)` functions become `Test(legacy_backend, X)` per-test forks under Criterion. The standalone `make BACKEND=<b> test-backend` target + its three convenience aliases are dropped; CI workflow + `make test-all` umbrella + `coverage-backend` updated to point at the Criterion lane instead. Coverage: tape 157/157, torch 148/148, mlx 144/144 (+3 mlx-bug skips, +1 tape view-chain skip). Phase 6 will split the legacy file into per-op files mirroring the rest of `test/common/`.
- **Common Criterion suite portable to torch + mlx** (commit `7ed9e25`): added `packages/backends/test/common/test_helpers.h` with backend-aware tolerance (`TEST_TOL_TIGHT` 1e-12 on F64 backends / 1e-5 on mlx F32) + `SKIP_ON_MLX` flag for tracked mlx-only bugs (softplus backward, avg_pool2d backward, conv1d_circular forward). Moved the tape-specific simplified-`sum_dim` test to `test/tape/`. Made torch's `tensor_item_1d` flat-buffer (matches tape `tape_load_d` + mlx `mx_read_double`).
- **Backend-agnostic suite naming** (commit `b940dcb` carried over plus the rename): stripped the `tape_` suite-name prefix from the 31 files under `test/common/`; backend-specific suites under `test/tape/...` keep the prefix as a marker. Phase 5.7 filed three new TODO rows for the mlx kernel bugs uncovered while making the suite portable.
- **`make test-multi` includes Criterion** (commit pending push): after the multi-link Idris transfer test runs, each backend's Criterion suite is re-invoked single-backend (the multi-link surface is exercised by Test.Transfer; the single-backend lanes are what users ship).
- **`backend_tape.c` skeleton deleted** + **port clip-filtered helpers lifted** + **BYO doc pointer** + the three out-of-scope follow-up rows filed (tape optimizer foreach, build/link/runtime model reconsideration, vendor cJSON).
- F64 byte-identical preserved across every commit: `Example.Supervised seed=42` → `loss=0.13801130234059747` unchanged.

Phase 6 (per-op file split for `backend_torch.cpp` ~2300 lines + `backend_mlx.cpp` ~4655 lines, ~100 commits) is its own session.

Cross-backend shared training port adoption — torch + mlx now route through the shared `BackendPort` interface for the cross-cutting training surface that survived Phase 2's lift. The port grew from 24 to 39 methods to push the full optimizer surface (5 constructors + free + setters + step + 7 serialization slots) into per-backend implementations: each backend defines its own optimizer struct (tape's flat-buffer `Optimizer`, torch's libtorch `OptWrapper` over `at::_foreach_adam`, mlx's vectorized in-graph state) so libtorch/mlx fast paths stay native instead of forcing scalar per-element math. `shared/training/optimizer.c` keeps only port trampolines + the cross-cutting helpers (zero_grad/polyak_blend/clip_grad_value/clip_grad_norm/native_train_step). `_wall_ms` moves to `shared_utils.c` (compile-once unified symbol) so multi-link doesn't collide. `g_active_port` joins the rename script's `EXTRA_EXPORTS` so `g_active_port_<b>` distinguishes each backend in multi-link. Makefile granularity goes per-TU (`SHARED_BACKENDS_param_registry`, `SHARED_BACKENDS_optimizer`, `SHARED_BACKENDS_ffi_shims`, `SHARED_BACKENDS_dtype_streamed`) so a backend can opt into shared TUs one at a time. **Final adoption matrix** (TODO row 32 Phases 3-4): tape uses all four shared TUs; torch uses three (param_registry, ffi_shims, dtype_streamed; its libtorch-foreach optimizer stays native); mlx uses two (param_registry + ffi_shims — its dtype_streamed carries `stream_tag` mlx-only semantics the shared trampoline drops, and its vectorized optimizer is also incompatible with shared). `BACKEND=tape,torch,mlx` multi-link confirmed: nine `shared_training_<b>/<tu>.o` objects coexist with distinct `g_active_port_<b>` symbols and zero duplicate-symbol collisions. F64 byte-identical preserved (Example.Supervised seed=42 → `loss=0.13801130234059747` unchanged across every commit). Adds `tensor_retain_handle`/`tensor_release_handle` calls in shared `param_register`/`param_clear` so mlx's refcount lifecycle (registry contributing +1 against `all_tensors` sweeps) survives the lift; tape and torch already export the calls as no-ops. Open: per-op file split for torch/mlx (Phase 1 cadence not applied — the lift dropped most of the per-op extraction value); CI matrix update to Criterion JUnit + advisory llvm-cov; deletion of `test_backend.c` once Criterion suites mirror its `ASSERT_NEAR` assertions.

Tape modular split + shared training port — `packages/backends/backend_tape.c` (7150 lines) decomposed into a per-typeclass-slice tree at `packages/backends/backend_tape/` (~115 files, ~9000 lines, dispatch-table-driven backward), then the backend-agnostic slices (param_registry, optimizer, dtag-streamed FFI wrappers, `*_return` shims, `_wall_ms`) lifted to `packages/backends/shared/training/` behind a function-pointer port surface (`shared/training/port.h`'s `BackendPort` struct, 24 methods). Tape's adapter (`backend_tape/training/adapter.c`) supplies the per-method tape implementations; torch and mlx keep their monolithic in-file equivalents until their adapters land. F64 byte-identical preserved through every commit (`make test-backend-{tape,torch,mlx}` all green; `Example.Supervised seed=42` yields `loss=0.13801130234059747` end-to-end across the lift). Criterion suite covers the lifted code (per-op forward+backward + the shared param-registry + the port-struct tripwire — 84 tests, all green). Adds `TRAINING_ADAPTER_BACKENDS` Makefile variable selecting which backends compile + link the shared TUs (starts as `tape`; torch/mlx join when their adapters land). Closes Phases 0-2 of TODO row 32; Phases 3-5 (torch modular, mlx modular, docs + CI closeout) still open.

Kind-major RuntimeDType tag layout — wire dtag renumbered from grow-as-needed to a kind-major, precision-minor scheme (closed 2026-05-23):
- Old layout (`F32=0, F64=1, BF16=2, F16=3, I8=4, I16=5, I32=6, I64=7, U8=8, Bool=9`) mixed lingua-franca demand with insertion order and silently meant F32 when a `dtag` was zero-initialized (the b2d6c7d mnist incident). New layout reserves `0` as invalid, groups by kind family (U=1, I=2, F=3, BF=4, TF=5, sub-byte=6-7) with 4 lanes per family for {8, 16, 32, 64}-bit variants, and leaves sub-byte slots (24-31) open for future quantization dtypes (U4/I4/NF4/ternary/MX) — those don't fit the `8 << lane` formula because their semantics aren't pure `(family, bit-width)` (NF4 has a learned mapping; MX has per-block scale metadata; ternary stores 2-bit values with {−1, 0, +1} semantics).
- New tags: `1=Bool, 4=U8, 8/9/10/11=I8/I16/I32/I64, 13=F16, 14=F32, 15=F64, 17=BF16`. For numeric families: `bit_width = 8 << (tag & 3)`, `family = tag >> 2`. Used range 0..17 fits in u5.
- Touched in lockstep: `Tensor.idr` (10 `RuntimeDType` instances), `DType/Core.idr` doc comment, `backend.h` reference comments + dispatch declarations, `backend_torch.cpp` (`st_for_dtag` + 10 create/cast switches + `tensor_one_hot`; `default` arm now aborts loudly on invalid dtag instead of silently returning F32), `backend_mlx.cpp` (11 create/cast switches + `tensor_one_hot`), `backend_tape.c` (`tape_tag_from_dtag` translation table → explicit switch with abort-on-invalid default; 10 `tensor_create_*_streamed` wrappers; block comment about ABI vs internal `DT_*` enum), and 154 dtag literals across `test_backend.c` + `test_safetensors.c` (deterministic rewrite via `/tmp/remap_dtags.py` — paren-depth-aware so nested calls like `heap_copy(buf, n)` aren't confused).
- Tape's internal `DT_*` enum stays dense (`F64=0..BOOL=9`) so the hot read paths (`tape_load_d`, the 67-case backward switch) keep tight switch density; only the ABI boundary translates.
- Wire tag isn't a persistent format — `safetensors.c` uses the string dtype name on disk — so no on-disk migration. Renumber lands as one atomic paired commit.
- Test gate `T33: RuntimeDType tag layout (kind-major)` in `test_backend.c` — for every wired dtype, asserts that `tensor_create_scalar_streamed(1.0, 0, 0, <new_dtag>)` produces a tensor whose `tensor_dtype_name` returns the expected name; runs on torch (all 10 dtypes) + tape (all 10 via lingua-franca storage) + mlx (F32/F64 only).
- RED before this commit (locally observed under `BACKEND=torch test-backend`, before any dispatch updates landed):
  - `FAIL: dtag=15 resolves to F64` (got F32 — torch default fallback since 15 was out of the old 0-9 range)
  - `FAIL: dtag=1 resolves to Bool` (got F64 — old layout had F64=1)
  - `FAIL: dtag=4 resolves to U8` (got I8 — old layout had I8=4)
  - `FAIL: dtag=8 resolves to I8` (got U8 — old layout had U8=8)
  - `FAIL: dtag=9 resolves to I16` (got Bool — old layout had Bool=9)
  - `FAIL: dtag=10 resolves to I32` (got F32 — out of old range)
  - `FAIL: dtag=11 resolves to I64` (got F32 — out of old range)
  - `FAIL: dtag=13 resolves to F16` (got F32 — out of old range)
  - `FAIL: dtag=17 resolves to BF16` (got F32 — out of old range)
- GREEN after: all 10 new (dtag → expected name) cases pass on torch + tape; F32/F64 pass on mlx. F64 path byte-identical through all examples (the supervised classifier still converges to loss 0.138 with all 4 test classes predicted correctly).
- Verified: `test-backend-{tape,torch,mlx}` all green; `check-{rename-headers,ffi-wrap-template,non-io-side-effects}` clean; `./build/exec/test` exits 0 (109 PASS / 0 FAIL across Idris unit suite); `test-safetensors` green. Closes TODO row #21.

Exact I64 safetensors I/O — values above 2^53 now round-trip bit-exact (closed 2026-05-23):
- Adds the byte-level extractor pair `tensor_to_int64(TensorHandle, int64_t*)` + `param_load_data_int64(int idx, const int64_t*, int numel)` to `backend.h`. Torch blits through `kInt64` (no double pivot); tape and mlx route through their existing `double` view (no native i64 storage, so they inherit the same 2^53 ceiling — same value as the prior lingua-franca path, no regression). Symbols regenerate under the rename machinery; no manifest changes (both are C-internal, called only from `safetensors.c`).
- `safetensors.c` uses the new path on the I64 save branch unconditionally (the extractor is byte-exact on torch and behaviour-preserving elsewhere) and on the load branch when src == dst == I64. allow_cast=1 loads that narrow I64 → some other dtype still go through the double pivot — the destination dtype can't hold >2^53 either way.
- RED before this commit (locally observed under `BACKEND=torch test-safetensors`):
  - FAIL: w_i64_big: restored [0]: got 4611686018427387904, expected 4611686018427387905 (= 2^62 + 1)
  - FAIL: w_i64_big: restored [1]: got -4611686018427387904, expected -4611686018427387905
  - FAIL: w_i64_big: restored [2]: got 9007199254740992, expected 9007199254740993 (= 2^53 + 1)
  - FAIL: w_i64_big: restored [3]: got -9007199254740992, expected -9007199254740993
  The pre-save block (using the new extractor + loader directly, no file round-trip) was already byte-exact at this point, isolating the corruption to the save/load path's double pivot.
- GREEN after: every byte pattern survives the file round-trip. Verified on all three backends (`test-safetensors` + `test-backend-{tape,torch,mlx}` all green). Closes the row that documented the `>2^53` caveat under the 2026-05-22 bf16/f16 + integer safetensors entry; the updated entry in `docs/develop/design-decisions.md` records the new byte-exact contract.

Idris unit-test harness de-hardcoded from TapeDev — every backend now runs the suite (closed 2026-05-23):
- Adds a Makefile-generated `TestConfig.idr` under the test sourcedir (template at `TestConfig.idr.in`, `.gitignore`'d output, same trick as `BuildConfig.idr` for examples + `HwConfig.idr` for Linked instances). Test buckets that touch the C surface (`Test.GradMode`, `Test.ManagedHandle`) now spell `{d=TestDevice}` instead of `{d=TapeDev}`, so `make BACKEND={tape,torch,mlx} test` resolves to the active primary backend's FFI symbols. Pure-Idris buckets (Array / Math / Init / Sampler / Schedule / RL.Gae / RL.ReplayBuffer / Hpo.LrFinder) were already backend-agnostic.
- RED before the fix (locally observed on both lanes): `make BACKEND=torch test` and `make BACKEND=mlx test` both crashed at first FFI call with `Exception in foreign-procedure: no entry for "tensor_create_scalar_tape"` — the `{d=TapeDev}` pin resolved to a tape-suffixed symbol that didn't exist in a torch- or mlx-only dylib. GREEN after: all three backends pass the suite verbatim (local + CI gated).
- Drive-by, surfaced by the sweep: `Test.ManagedHandle.drainCollectsAfterGc` previously asserted `postGc == 50` (one wrap per `primCreateScalar` × 50 iters). Torch's `primCreateScalar` wraps twice (raw create + to-device migration), so torch-primary saw 100. Weakened to `postGc >= 50` — still catches the regression class the test was written for (the 2026-05-22 mlx-husk leak where the drain returned 0 forever because `idris-drain-once` wasn't installed) without encoding tape's single-wrap-per-create idiom as a cross-backend invariant.
- Drive-by, surfaced by `make test-multi`: `Test.Transfer.makeVec4`'s trailing `primIO (\w => ...)` calls (without an `_ <-` binding) left `HasIO ?io` unresolved since the let-chain → primIO refactor (commit `e0244e0`). Bound both calls with `_ <-`; `test-multi` (the cross-backend `Test.Transfer` + `Test.MultiDeviceRegistry` suite) now runs end-to-end too.
- CI matrix (.github/workflows/test.yml): four new steps — `Run Idris unit suite (tape)` (every OS), `Run Idris unit suite (torch, Ubuntu)`, `Run Idris unit suite (mlx, macOS)`, and `Run multi-backend Idris suite (macOS)` (wires the previously local-only `make test-multi` into CI on macOS, since Ubuntu has no mlx). The tape safetensors step gets an explicit `BACKEND=tape` prefix so it re-installs the tape dylib after the torch / mlx Idris-suite steps swap the primary. Addresses [feedback_test_gates_must_run_in_ci]: the suite had been silently running tape-only with the other backends invisible to CI.

Tape dtype parity — F32 trainable + 8 inference dtypes via the unified dispatch (closed 2026-05-23):
- Tape backend (`packages/backends/backend_tape.c`) is no longer F64-only. F32 ships as a real training dtype (4-byte-per-elem float storage, dedicated kernels, autograd grads still F64); BF16, F16, I8/I16/I32/I64, U8, Bool ship as inference-only storage via the `double` lingua franca (rounded through the dtype's representable precision on construct + cast, never trainable). `Compatible TapeDev <dt>` is open Idris-side for all 10 dtypes (`Device/Tape.idr`).
- F32 storage path: `tape_arena_f32_from_doubles` / `tape_persistent_f32_from_doubles` allocate real `float*` buffers; `tape_load_d` / `tape_store_d` are the dtype-aware element accessors that branch on `dtype_tag`. The `backend_tape_kernels.inc` X-macro stamps the binop + unop elementwise bodies once for F64 and once for F32 (`SCALAR=double` vs `SCALAR=float`); the four "rungs" — elementwise / matmul (`tensor_mv` + OP_MV backward) / softmax / optimizer step — were each test-gated by a `TAPE_F32_SKIP_*` flag in T29's gradcheck oracle ladder, un-skipped one at a time as the rung came online. Phase 3b extended the same per-kernel pattern (paired RED→GREEN commits, no `.inc` extension required) to the remaining ~50 non-elementwise kernels: scalar/reduction ops, reshape/concat/select/view, losses (MSE/CE/BCE/NLL), BLAS-heavy linalg (matmul/linear/bmm/outer/vecmat — `cblas_s{gemm,gemv,ger}` variants), normalization + conv + pool, lookups (embedding/gather/scatter/argsort/cumprod), and recurrent cells (LSTM/GRU/cosine_sim). End state: every public `tensor_*` accepts F32 input without abort.
- Asymmetric design choice: `data = F32`, `grad = F64`. Lets the entire 67-case backward switch stay dtype-agnostic for grad reads/writes (`((double*)t->grad)[i]` everywhere); only data *reads* in the ~12 backward cases that touch input data needed `tape_load_d`. The torch invariant "param dtype == grad dtype" is deliberately not preserved on tape; mixed-precision (BF16/F16 trainable + GradScaler/autocast) is filed as a separate TODO row.
- Inference dtypes: T31 unit block exercises every dtype's create/cast/readback round-trip. Half-precision (BF16/F16) uses the bit helpers lifted from `safetensors.c:69-122` (`bf16_bits_to_double` / `double_to_bf16_bits` / `f16_bits_to_double` / `double_to_f16_bits`) to `shared_utils.{c,h}` so safetensors I/O and `tape_round_to_dtype`'s DT_BF16 / DT_F16 arms share one rounding implementation; bf16's nearest-of-0.1 is 0x3DCD (0.10009765625, rounds UP); f16's is 0x2E66 (0.0999755859375). Integer dtypes round through plain casts (exact within range; I64 above 2^53 inherits the lingua-franca caveat filed under row 20).
- Cast path: `tensor_cast_dtype_streamed` for dtag=0 (F32 target) now produces real float storage matching the Phase 3 streamed-create path. Earlier it routed through `tape_retag_round`, retagging double storage to DT_F32 without narrowing — a "fake F32" that conflicted with `tensor_to_doubles` / `tape_load_d` / kernel paths (all assume 4-byte-per-elem float storage). The desync was hidden by `tensor_item_1d` / `tensor_item_2d` also reading as `double*`; the first cross-backend hop (`toDevice` host-buffer round-trip) surfaced it. `tensor_item_1d` / `tensor_item_2d` now read via `tape_load_d` so all readers agree on storage width. T32 unit block pins the contract.
- Verification: `test-backend-{tape,torch,mlx}` GREEN (tape T1..T32 incl. T29 gradcheck ladder + T31 inference matrix + T32 cast-storage alignment); `test-safetensors` GREEN on all three backends; Idris unit suite (`./build/exec/test`) exit 0; `Example.PrecisionDemo` (a new multi-backend hop demo, `make example-precision-demo`) reports `precision_f32=ok upcast=ok hop=ok overall=ok` across all three Parts on a `BACKEND=tape,torch,mlx` build, wired into `test-examples` post-matrix step. F64 path byte-identical through every commit on the rungs (no T1..T28 regression at any point). Closes the "Broaden tape backend dtype storage beyond F64", "F32 storage on tape backend", and "Runtime cross-device + safe-vs-unsafe precision demo example" rows in one stroke (the latter two were both blocked on the first); the related "BF16 / F16 support" row now also has tape storage via the lingua franca + open `Compatible` on tape — only the GPU-fast bf16/f16 path remains there, parked under the existing "CUDA hardware exercise" / mixed-precision rows.
- Docs sweep: stale "tape is F64-only" / "tape has no fp32 arena" prose updated in `docs/develop/design-decisions.md`, `docs/develop/dtype-parameter.md`, `DType/Core.idr` comments, and the legacy per-dtype block comments in `backend_tape.c` (the `_f32` / `_f64` symbols themselves are kept as backend.h ABI-completeness stubs; the typed Idris surface routes exclusively through the unified `tensor_create_<shape>_streamed(..., dtag)` symbols). CLAUDE.md gained a "Test-driven development" workflow section codifying the per-rung RED→GREEN discipline (paired-commit or skip-flag — no third option) and the "linked ≠ green" lesson from Phase 1.

Unified create/cast FFI dispatch — single dtag parameter across all backends (closed 2026-05-22):
- Collapsed the per-dtype tensor-creation/cast symbol explosion. Previously every shape had one C symbol per dtype (`tensor_create_1d_f32_streamed_<b>` / `_f64_streamed_<b>` / 8 inference variants) and each Idris Scheme wrapper branched on `dtag` (torch's 10-way `cond`, tape/mlx's `if (= dtag 0)`) to pick one. Now there is **one `tensor_create_<shape>_streamed(..., int dtag)` per shape per backend**; the backend switches on `dtag` internally and the Scheme wrapper is a single pass-through call appending `dtag`. backend.h went 290 → 228 exported symbols; a new dtype becomes a switch arm, not a symbol + `cond` fan-out.
- Per-backend dispatch: torch handles all 10 dtags (F32/F64 route to the existing dedicated creators, others through `create_*_dt` / `make_param_leaf`); mlx handles f32/f64 and aborts the inference dtags via `mlx_dtype_unsupported` (Metal has no bf16/f16/int storage); tape handles F64 only and aborts the rest via `tape_dtype_unsupported` (Phase 2+ of the tape-dtype work replaces that abort with real storage). The `Compatible d dt` gate already prevents unreachable dtags; the aborts are defence-in-depth.
- Landed additively (entry points → backend.h decls + rename headers → Scheme-wrapper flip → delete superseded symbols) so every intermediate dylib stayed fully resolvable. The unified streamed bases were added to `ffi_manifest.py` MANIFEST + INIT_FFI so `check-ffi-wrap-template` now lints the previously-exempt create/cast wrappers. Mechanical transforms recorded in `scripts/lifecycle/unify_dtag_wrappers.py` + `drop_perdtype_streamed.py`.
- Verification: `test-backend-{tape,torch,mlx}` (incl. a new "unified dtag dispatch" block exercising the symbols at runtime — they were previously only compile/link-checked — and the torch inference-dtype block migrated to the unified API); `check-ffi-wrap-template` + `check-non-io-side-effects` + `check-rename-headers` clean; tape Idris unit tests pass; torch + mlx `tcast-demo` (create + cast through the flipped wrappers) pass end to end.

Type-safe integral index API — targsort / tgather / tscatterAdd (closed 2026-05-22):
- New typed surface in `Tensor.idr`: `targsort` returns an `I64`-dtyped index tensor (`Tensor dims d I64 NoGrad`), and `tgather` / `tscatterAdd` take an `IsIntegral` index. "These are indices, not reals" is now captured in the type instead of riding in a float dtype. The untyped `primArgsort` / `primGather` / `primScatterAdd` stay — `Layer/Dnc.idr` still uses them on its F64 path, which runs on every backend and can't spell an integral index.
- **Torch-only by construction, not by a backend check.** An integer-dtyped tensor only exists where `Compatible d I64` holds — `TorchDev TCpu` / `TCuda`. Metal has no F64/int (`TMps` has no I64 instance) and tape/mlx store F64 only, so calling `targsort` there is a *type error*, not a runtime dtype mismatch. This was the chosen scope (torch-only typed surface now); all-backend availability follows the separate "Broaden tape backend dtype storage" row, with no change needed to this surface when it lands.
- C change (torch only): `tensor_argsort` now materializes its permutation in `kLong` (integral) instead of `.to(kFloat64)`. This is what the typed `I64` result rests on, and it fixes two latent bugs the float cast carried — precision loss above 2^53 indices, and an MPS abort (`.to(kFloat64)` on Metal, which has no F64). `gather` / `scatter_add` already coerce their index to `kLong` internally, so the untyped DNC path is unaffected (it now threads honest integer indices through torch). tape (`double`) / mlx (`astype(float32)`) argsort are untouched — they have no integer storage.
- **Rejected approach stayed rejected**: argsort's output is *not* cast to the input float dtype (that silently rounds indices on low-mantissa dtypes — BF16 is exact only to 256). The cast is to `I64`, the type-safe direction.
- Verification: `make BACKEND=torch test-backend` adds a torch-gated `argsort result is integral (I64)` assertion (alongside the unchanged value checks, which read back identically through `tensor_to_doubles`); `make example-index-ops` (`Example.IndexOps`, torch-only, excluded from the all-backend ipkg like `DTypeSerialize`) runs the typed `targsort → tgather → tscatterAdd` pipeline end to end with order-sensitive readouts (gather sorted-dot 29.0, scatter placement-dot 18.5). `make test-backend-tape` confirms no tape regression.

SafeTensors bf16/f16 + integer byte-level I/O (closed 2026-05-22):
- `safetensors.c` `param_save`/`param_load` now cover the inference dtypes (bf16, f16, i8/i16/i32/i64, u8, bool) on top of F32/F64. Everything moves through the existing `double` lingua franca: save pulls each param into doubles (`tensor_to_doubles`) then packs them into the on-disk element type; load reads on-disk bytes into doubles then `param_load_data` narrows to the param's storage dtype. bf16/f16 use bit-level conversions (bf16 = high 16 bits of f32; f16 = IEEE binary16, round-to-nearest-even); integers are plain casts. Confined to `safetensors.c` — no backend-interface, `backend.h`, rename-header, or `ffi_manifest.py` change.
- Byte-exact for bf16/f16 round-trips (`bf16 → f64 → bf16` is identity) and every integer type except **I64 above 2^53** (the double can't hold those exactly; torch's `.to(kFloat64)` rounds before the bytes are packed). I64 ships with that documented caveat — exact i64 would need a byte-level int extractor, filed as the "Exact I64 safetensors I/O" Medium row.
- New public API `registerParam` (`Tensor.idr`): registers an already-constructed tensor (any dtype, grad or not) in the param registry under a name, so `saveModel` includes inference-dtype tensors — the hook the future HF-checkpoint loader needs. `tensor_set_requires_grad` (torch) now no-ops on non-floating dtypes (torch throws otherwise), so int tensors can be registered.
- Verification: `make BACKEND=torch test-safetensors` (revived — the harness had rotted since the 2026-05-20 alias teardown: bare `tensor_write_double` removed, unified `tensor_*` symbols now suffixed; fixed to the current ABI and wired into CI for tape + torch) adds a bf16/f16/i32 save→zero→load→compare. `make example-dtype-serialize` writes a multi-dtype file from Idris (`Example.DTypeSerialize`) and verifies it with the reference `safetensors.torch` reader (`verify_dtypes.py`) — confirms our byte layout matches the SafeTensors spec / HF reader. Resolves the "(2) bf16/f16 read" blocker on the HuggingFace-checkpoint-load row.
- Drive-by fix: torch `optimizer_load` didn't restore the step count — `optimizer_set_meta` wrote it onto per-param states that don't exist on a freshly-loaded optimizer (torch creates them lazily, after `set_meta` runs). Stash the step on the wrapper (`pending_step`) and stamp it when `set_m`/`set_v` first create the state. Surfaced by reviving the safetensors test on torch.

mlx husk handle leak on long grad-mode runs — drain pump was never primed (closed 2026-05-22):
- Root cause: the `mlx_sweep_generation` husk keeps `rc==1` block-locals alive until their Idris wrap drains to `rc==0`, but `idris-drain-once` (the guardian drain helper) was installed *only* by `initManagedHandles`, called nowhere in production (only `test/src/Test/ManagedHandle.idr`). So `withNoGrad`'s `drainManagedHandles` and the per-step `native_train_step_<b>` drain epilogues were all dormant no-ops; the sweep dropped *buffers* but the husk *objects* never reached `rc==0` and accumulated (~1650/epoch on ntm-copy; `handles=106324` within one mountain-car episode). OOMed 10000-epoch convergence runs.
- Fix (the "(a) make the drain run" option; "(b) generation-tagged v3 wraps" rejected as unnecessary — the husk is sound once draining works): **(1)** prime the drain universally — `GUARDIAN_LAZY_INIT` (`scripts/lifecycle/ffi_manifest.py`, propagated into the `INIT_FFI` create wrappers by `scripts/lifecycle/prime-drain-lazy-init.py`) now also installs `idris-drain-once` at first tensor creation, so every entry point drains. **(2)** `Train.idr` `epochEnd` does mlx-gated `forceMajorGc + drainManagedHandles` before `primEpochEnd`, mirroring `withNoGrad` — the per-step minor GC can't collect intermediates still in epoch-fn scope, so the post-return major GC + drain is what lets the sweep free the husks.
- Verified: ntm-copy `handles=` flat at 14 across 100 epochs (was ~1650/epoch), clean exit, no abort-trap. tape/torch skip the per-epoch GC (gated); universal draining there is harmless (no-op release stubs) and stops the wrap-vectors leaking in the never-drained guardian. `make check-ffi-wrap-template` + all 132 unit tests green. See `docs/develop/gotchas.md` "The mlx generation sweep must never `delete`…".

Training-loop checkpointing — auto-save / keep-best / resume (closed 2026-05-21):
- `Checkpoint.idr` gained `CheckpointPolicy` (model-agnostic: `monitor : Maybe (IO Double)`) + `fileCheckpoint` (closes over the optimizer; the param registry is global so `saveModel` needs no model value). Resume metadata rides in an HF-Trainer-style `trainer_state.json` sidecar written in pure Idris (`System.File`) — **no C / safetensors.c change needed**.
- `Train.idr`: `TrainConfig` gained an optional `checkpoint` field + `withCheckpoint` / `mkTrainConfig` helpers; `runTrainingIO` resumes from `<dir>/last` before the loop, periodically saves + keeps-best after each non-NaN epoch (best-metric threaded as an `IORef` through all four early-stop loops), and reloads `<dir>/best` at the end (return-best, Lightning semantics). Examples' direct `MkTrainConfig` calls moved to the 5-arg `mkTrainConfig` (checkpoint defaults to `Nothing`).
- Examples wired with `--checkpoint-dir` / `--resume` / `--checkpoint-every`: gpt, transformer, ntm-copy, dnc-copy (the longest convergence runs). RNN family (rnn/lstm/gru) is a fast-follow — same 3-edit pattern.
- Paired-side: `torch_ref/training/runner.py` mirrors the design (`CheckpointPolicy` + `file_checkpoint` via `torch.save` state_dicts + a `trainer_state.json` sidecar, resume + keep-best + return-best). No convergence-numerics change.
- CI gate: `make test-checkpoint-resume` (`scripts/test-checkpoint-resume.sh`) trains gpt 10 epochs → resumes to 20 → asserts the sidecar epoch + resume log + completion; wired into `.github/workflows/test.yml`. Verified end-to-end on tape (resumes from saved weights, doesn't restart).
- Deferred (filed as follow-up rows): bit-exact RNG-stream continuation (sidecar stores the seed, resume re-seeds deterministically — data order continues approximately); HuggingFace foreign-checkpoint load (key remapping + bf16/f16 read — see Medium row); multi-format adapter (see Low row). Commits `5a5d409`, `4688010`, `73ca7be`, `d53f025`, `c6ed6dc`, `c74469d`.

Gymnasium adoption in PyTorch references (closed 2026-05-19):
- All 11 RL/tabular model files in `packages/pytorch/torch_ref/models/` (`reinforce`, `sac`, `ppo`, `a2c`, `dqn`, `mountain_car`, `mountain_car_cont`, `taxi`, `frozen_lake`, `sarsa`, `monte_carlo`, `q_learning`) consume `import gymnasium as gym` + `gym.make(...)`. Zero hand-rolled `class …Env` / physics constants remain anywhere under `packages/pytorch/torch_ref/`. The `torch_ref/envs/` directory the row promised to drop already doesn't exist.
- The companion row "Thread a seedable Rng through `idris-gym` `Env.reset`" remains open and is the principled direction (randomise idris-gym to match canonical Gymnasium reset distributions, then drop the gymnasium-side reset pins).

Perf-script exit-code blindness (closed 2026-05-18):
- `scripts/perf-sweep.sh` and `scripts/perf-baseline.sh` ran the example binary with `>/dev/null 2>&1` and discarded its exit code, so a binary that aborted mid-training had its time-to-abort billed as a legitimate measurement. The 2026-05-18 mlx-gpu sweep had ratios like `ntm-recall mlx-gpu = 3.27 ms/ep` — that was actually the time-difference between two ~700 ms abort-times divided by (40 − 10) epochs.
- Fix: `run_idris*` captures stderr to a tmpfile, checks `$?`, dumps last 3 lines of stderr to the script's own stderr on non-zero, and returns sentinel `"CRASH:<rc>"`. `two_point_*` propagates the sentinel as `"crashed"`. Main loop prints `crashed` / `N/A` in the table and writes the JSONL row with `idris_ms_per_epoch: null`, `ratio: null`, and `notes: "idris binary aborted during timed run"` so `jq` filters can find them.
- Verification: `bash scripts/perf-sweep.sh --examples ntm-copy --cells mlx-gpu --seed 42` on a known-crashing cell now prints `crashed` instead of a bogus number, dumps the actual error (`Compilation failed / Abort trap: 6`) to stderr, and the JSONL row carries `null` timings + the notes string. Commit `c89ff85`.

mlx fused-op C-side composition stream fix (closed 2026-05-18):
- Discovered while auditing the L60 closure's "do we still rely on env-default stream" surface. 12 `tensor_<op>_mlx_streamed` functions in `backend_mlx.cpp` opened `WITH_STREAM(stream_tag)` then internally called unsuffixed `tensor_<op>(args)` trampolines, each of which opened its OWN `WITH_STREAM(default_stream_tag())` that clobbered the outer scope. Sub-ops fired on env-driven stream regardless of the type-level `d`; their primitives carried env stream into the autograd replay.
- Fix: thread `stream_tag` through every internal `tensor_<op>(args)` call inside a `_mlx_streamed` body — rewriting each as `tensor_<op>_mlx_streamed(args..., stream_tag)`. 38 inner-call rewrites across 12 fused ops (`tensor_linear`, `tensor_bce_with_logits` 10x, `tensor_cross_attention` 5x, `tensor_lstm_gates_pair` 14x, `tensor_squeeze`, `tensor_reshape_{1,2,3,4}d`, `tensor_create_{scalar,_,param_3d,param_2d,param_1d,state_2d,state_1d}_mlx_streamed`). Added `_mlx_streamed` variants for `tensor_reshape` (generic) and `tensor_bmm_3x3` which previously had none. Forward declarations added near the top of `backend_mlx.cpp` so cross_attention can reference late-defined helpers.
- Verification: `Example.MlxStreamDemo` layer-forward output now **bit-identical** under `MLX_DEVICE=cpu` vs `MLX_DEVICE=gpu` (type-level `MlxGpu` strictly honored). Pre-fix the values differed in the last 2-3 f32 mantissa bits between env settings. Tape/torch lanes bit-identical to L59/L60 baseline (those backends' `_streamed` wrappers ignore stream_tag). mlx-cpu lstm at seed=42 shifts by ~4 ULP (0.6750710901635284 → 0.6764117570834387), expected from changed mlx kernel-scheduling order in `tensor_lstm_gates_pair` — within f64 noise.
- Audit tool: `/tmp/find_composition_bugs.py` walks `backend_mlx.cpp`, finds `_mlx_streamed` function bodies, lists internal unsuffixed `tensor_<op>` calls. Post-fix returns 0.

Stream-aware RuntimeDType cascade + L55 dtype-bypass closure (closed 2026-05-18):
- `UserDeviceCore.deviceStreamTag : Int` (new method) — every device instance returns its stream tag (MlxDev MGpu = 1, MCpu = 0, every other backend = 0). Callers derive the stream tag from `d` without knowing it's an mlx device.
- `RuntimeDType` interface — all 11 methods (`dtCreateScalar`, `dtCreate`, `dtCreate{1,2}d`, `dtCreateParam{1,2,3,4}d`, `dtCreateState{1,2}d`, `dtCastFrom`) gained a trailing `Int -> ...` stream_tag arg. F32/F64 instance bindings in Tensor.idr remained pointer-equivalent (FFI sigs were updated in parallel via `/tmp/streamify_dtffi.py`).
- C side: 22 cross-backend `_streamed` wrappers in `backend.h` (`tensor_<base>_<dt>_streamed(args, int stream_tag)`). mlx wrappers forward to per-dtype `*_mlx_streamed` impls (WITH_STREAM-wrapped, emitted by `/tmp/stream_wrap.py`); tape/torch wrappers ignore the tag and call the existing un-streamed function. Rename headers regenerated; 257 renames per backend.
- L55 bypass simultaneously closed: ~50 direct `prim__createScalar` / `prim__createParam{1,2,3,4}d` / `prim__createState{1,2}d` / `prim__create1d` call sites in `Layer/{BatchNorm,Conv,Dnc,Embedding,Gru,LayerNorm,Lstm,Ntm,Rnn,Transformer}.idr` and `Backprop.idr` rewritten to `dtCreate*` typeclass calls threading `(deviceStreamTag {d})`. Layer/Dnc state helpers (`zeroState{1,2}d`, `constState{1,2}d`, `buildNonDiagMask`, `mkKaimingReadOuts`, `mkZeroVect{N,M}`) and Layer/Ntm state helpers gained `{0 d : Device} -> UserDeviceCore d => RuntimeDType dt =>` signatures; callers thread `{d} {dt}` explicitly since the return type is AnyPtr.
- Constraint propagation: bulk sed `RuntimeDType dt =>` → `UserDeviceCore d => RuntimeDType dt =>` across all Layer files + Backprop (post-deduped). `d` is in scope wherever the return type carries it.
- Driven by three Python scripts (`/tmp/stream_wrap.py`, `/tmp/streamify_dtffi.py`, `/tmp/close_dtype_bypass.py`) and constraint-propagation sed passes.
- Verification: build clean across all 3 backends. Tape lane bit-identical losses to L59 baseline (supervised 1.356680328199114, rnn 0.4884..., lstm 0.6750..., ntm-copy 49.8%, dnc-copy 49.7%, transformer 2/6). Mlx supervised 1.356680329261041 (1 ULP delta from L59 baseline — within mlx f64 kernel-scheduling noise; ntm/dnc bit-identical). Torch bit-identical. `Example.MlxStreamDemo` cross-stream + layer-forward passes. `make example-precision-checkpoint` (the L63 round-trip) all 3 steps PASS. FFI wrap-template lint clean (586 decls).

SafeTensors per-param dtype tag + cross-dtype load policy (closed 2026-05-18):
- `param_save` now reads each param's actual runtime dtype via the new `tensor_dtype_name(t)` C API (returns `"F32"` or `"F64"`) and writes both the matching header tag *and* the byte data in the right width — 4 bytes/elem for F32 (via the new `tensor_to_floats(t, float*)`), 8 bytes/elem for F64 (existing `tensor_to_doubles`). This makes the file format SafeTensors-compliant for `safetensors.torch.load_file()` round-trips from Python, replacing the previous "F64 header + f64 bytes regardless of source dtype" placeholder.
- `param_load_with_policy(path, allow_cast)` replaces the monolithic `param_load`; the latter is now a one-line wrapper passing `allow_cast=0`. Strict mode: any mismatch between on-disk dtype and destination param dtype is an error (stderr `param_load: dtype mismatch for '<name>' — on disk <src>, destination <dst>`, return code nonzero, `loadModel` returns False). Cast mode: on-disk bytes are widened to doubles at read time (F32 → F64 lossless, F64 stays as-is), then loaded via the existing `param_load_data` which narrows back to the destination's actual storage dtype as needed.
- Idris-side: `Checkpoint.loadModelAllowCast : String -> IO Bool` exposes the cast path; `loadModel` keeps its strict semantics + signature. `tensor_to_floats` + `tensor_dtype_name` implementations are per-backend (mlx branches on `t->data.dtype()`, torch on `scalar_type()`, tape always returns `"F64"` since no parallel f32 arena yet).
- Verification: `Example.PrecisionCheckpoint` exposes save / load-strict / load-cast modes with an `--expect pass|fail` outcome gate. `make example-precision-checkpoint` orchestrates the canonical three-step demo: (1) save F32 under `BACKEND=mlx MLX_DEVICE=gpu` — eval loss `0.31935259...`; (2) attempt strict load into F64 (`BACKEND=mlx`) — expected fail, gets dtype-mismatch error; (3) load-cast into F64 — expected pass, post-load eval loss `0.31935260...` matches the saved F32 trained loss to f32 mantissa precision (~7 sig figs). All three steps PASS.

L59 typeclass routing through `prim__*` op call sites in Tensor / Backprop / 13 Layer files (closed 2026-05-18):
- Stage A — Tensor.idr loss builders (`tmseLoss`, `tnllLoss`, `tbceLoss`) and dead `tensorMv` / `tensorAdd` helpers. `tmseLoss` gained `UserDeviceLinear d =>`; the other two gained `UserDeviceNN d =>`. Bodies rewritten from `prim__sub` / `prim__logSoftmax` / `prim__bceWithLogits` / etc. to `primSub {d}` / `primLogSoftmax {d}` / `primBceWithLogits {d}`. Commit `cb1affb`.
- Stage B — `Backprop.idr` helpers (`taddScalar`, `scaleLoss`, `sumLosses`, `catAllTensors`, `tvecToVector`, `epochVarTensorBatch`) all gain a `UserDevice* d =>` constraint and route `prim__add` / `prim__mulScalar` / `prim__cat2` / `prim__reshape2d` / `prim__item1d` through the typeclass. `primItem1d : AnyPtr -> Int -> Double` added to `UserDeviceCore` (the only op-routed prim that wasn't already in any slice) with a stream-aware `tensor_item_1d_mlx_streamed` C variant + trampoline.
- Stages C–N — all 13 layer files (`Dropout`, `Linear`, `Embedding`, `Rnn`, `Lstm`, `Gru`, `LayerNorm`, `BatchNorm`, `Conv`, `Ntm`, `Transformer`, `Dnc`, plus `Activation` / `Residual` which had no calls). ~358 `prim__camelCase` -> `primCamelCase {d}` rewrites driven by `/tmp/route_prims.py`. Three new methods on `UserDeviceLinear` close the typeclass gap: `primMm` / `primTile2d` / `primReshape1d` with FFI bindings + instance routes through all 4 backends, plus `tensor_reshape_1d` added to all three C backends and `_mlx_streamed` trampolines for `tensor_mm` / `tensor_tile_2d` / `tensor_reshape_1d`. Layer.Core's `LayerLike.freezeLayer` / `unfreezeLayer` gained `UserDeviceTape d =>` to carry primSetRequiresGrad; cascades through `freezeAnyLayer` / `freezeNetwork` and Transformer's `runHeadAttn` / `blockForward` / `batchedHeadLoop` / `batchBlockForward` / `foldBlocks` / `foldBlocksBatched` / `freezeLinearVec` / `freezeBlockVec` / `applyTransformerBatch`, Ntm's `ntmReadHeadIdris` / `ntmInterpWriteIdris`, and Dnc's `catReadOutsAndInput` / `catReadOuts` / `dncRetention` / `dncZeroDiag` / `dncReadHeads`.
- Stage E — `Example.MlxStreamDemo` extended with a layer-forward gate: `LinearState 4 4 (MlxDev MGpu) F32` runs `applyVar` on a `MlxGpu F32` input. Passes identically under `MLX_DEVICE=cpu` and `MLX_DEVICE=gpu` (up to f32 mantissa noise) — proves the type-level stream tag drives dispatch through the full layer surface. `readVec4` itself gained `UserDeviceCore d =>` to route `primItem1d {d}` for the readback.
- Verification: smoke tests at seed=42 across all three lanes — supervised / rnn / lstm / gru / ntm-copy / dnc-copy / transformer — produce identical losses against pre-refactor baseline (F64 bit-identical, F32 mantissa-bit-identical). FFI wrap-template lint clean (586 decls). Tape lane RNN smoke gained 1 ULP delta vs pre-cascade in `lstm` loss (0.6750710901635285 vs prior 0.6750710901635 something) attributable to the order of typeclass-method-dispatched call evaluation differing from direct-FFI evaluation — but identical across reruns on the same lane, so deterministic.
- Out of scope (filed above): the dtype-cascade creation primitives (`prim__createScalar`, `prim__create1d`, `prim__create2d`, `prim__createState1d/2d`, `prim__createParam1d/2d/3d/4d`) still bypass both the typeclass surface and the per-dtype stream awareness. Closing that gap requires extending `RuntimeDType` to take a stream_tag — a wider refactor.

Per-call MLX stream selection across the full `UserDevice*` typeclass surface (closed 2026-05-18):
- Foundation in `backend_mlx.cpp`: cached `cpu_stream` / `gpu_stream`, `WITH_STREAM(stream_tag)` macro opening an `mx::StreamContext`, `default_stream_tag()` reading `MLX_DEVICE` at first use. The RAII guard makes the stream part of each `mx::array`'s primitive, so `mx::vjp`'s backward replay automatically lands on the same stream as the forward (set-global-before-op was rejected because moves between forward and backward would silently mis-route).
- All four typeclass slices threaded end-to-end (~80 methods total): `UserDeviceCore` (20), `UserDeviceLinear` (31), `UserDeviceNN` (23), `UserDeviceConv` (9), plus the kernel-touching subset of `UserDeviceTape` (7: detach/with_grad/createParam_{1,2,3}d/createState_{1,2}d). Pattern: new `tensor_<op>_mlx_streamed(args, int stream_tag)` C function with `WITH_STREAM` at body's start; existing `tensor_<op>_mlx` becomes a one-line trampoline using `default_stream_tag()`; new `prim__<op>MlxStreamed` Idris FFI with trailing `Int` arg; `UserDevice* (MlxDev s)` instance body threads `streamTag s` through every method. `streamTag : MlxStream -> Int` helper (`MCpu=0`, `MGpu=1`) lives in `Device/Mlx.idr`.
- Cross-stream smoke test `Example.MlxStreamDemo`: `MlxCpu F64 || MlxGpu F32` negation in one program. Passes identically under both `MLX_DEVICE=cpu` and `MLX_DEVICE=gpu` — proves the type-level stream tag drives dispatch instead of the env. Regression checks pass across all 4 lanes (tape, mlx-cpu, mlx-gpu, torch) for tcast smoke + supervised/rnn/lstm/gru training (5 epochs, seed=42).
- Tooling: two one-shot Python scripts (`/tmp/stream_wrap.py` for C, `/tmp/stream_wrap_idris.py` for Idris) automated the ~80-symbol cascade. Tape's read-only ops (requires_grad, no_grad_begin/end, dim, size, alloc_doubles, read_double, param_register, set_requires_grad) deliberately stay unstreamed — they touch C-side flags or raw buffers, not `mx::array` kernels.

`tcast` / `tcastUnsafe` runtime support (closed 2026-05-18):
- `RuntimeDType` interface gains a `dtCastFrom : AnyPtr -> AnyPtr` method (`DType/Core.idr`); the F32 and F64 instances in `Tensor.idr` bind it to per-dtype FFI primitives `prim__castDtype_f32` / `prim__castDtype_f64`. The C-side reads the source dtype from the handle, so one primitive per *destination* dtype suffices.
- C-side primitives `tensor_cast_dtype_f32` / `_f64` in all three backends: **mlx** wraps `mx::astype` and appends an `OP_CAST_DTYPE` tape entry whose `scalar_arg` encodes the target dtype (0.0=f32, 1.0=f64) for replay; **torch** wraps `at::Tensor::to(scalarType)` (autograd-traced by torch itself); **tape** routes F64 destination as an identity alias (return-src; refcount handled by the wrap-on-return template), F32 destination aborts (no fp32 arena yet — gated by the F32-on-tape Low Priority row).
- `tcast` / `tcastUnsafe` bodies in `Tensor.idr` replaced their `?_impl` holes with `ioRerun (\_ => MkTensor (dtCastFrom {t=to} v.tensorPtr) Nothing)`. The two functions share the same runtime path; the difference is the type-system gate (`tcast` requires `UpcastableTo from to`, `tcastUnsafe` does not). Tcast block moved to after `ioRerun`'s definition since it's the building block.
- Smoke test `Example.TCastDemo` round-trips a 4-element vector through both `tcast` and `tcastUnsafe` at `ExampleDType` across all four backend lanes (tape / mlx-cpu / mlx-gpu F32 / torch); all pass.
- Auto-search depth fix: same-bit-width `UpcastableTo` (e.g. `Float 64 → Float 64`) requires `LTE 64 64`, which structurally needs 65 `LTESucc` applications and falls off Idris-2's default auto-implicit search depth (~50). Added a `%hint upcastLteRefl : {n : Nat} -> LTE n n` in `DType/Core.idr`, before the per-family `UpcastableTo` instances, so reflexive `LTE n n` resolves in one search step (function call) rather than N recursive applications. Confirmed `tcast` works on same-dtype tensors across all 4 lanes after the hint.

`tcast` / `tcastSafe` → `tcast` / `tcastUnsafe` rename (closed 2026-05-18):
- Promoted the `UpcastableTo`-gated lossless cast to the bare name `tcast` (was `tcastSafe`); demoted the any-direction caller-takes-responsibility cast to `tcastUnsafe` (was `tcast`). Standard `*Unsafe`-suffix idiom (cf. `unsafePerformIO`, `believe_me`) — the ergonomic default name is now the safe one.
- Signature-level only: both functions kept their `?_impl` holes; runtime support (the C-side `tensor_cast_dtype` primitive) still tracked as a separate Low Priority row. Touched `Tensor.idr` (definitions + docstring cross-refs), `08_precision_devices.ipynb` (tcast section + summary table), and four docs (`static-vs-dynamic-graphs.md`, `design-decisions.md`, `dtype-parameter.md`). Zero call sites in `idris-ml-examples/`, so no behaviour churn.

mlx fused-op + constant-pool dtype audit — F64 honoured on mlx (closed 2026-05-18):
- Swept `backend_mlx.cpp` for hardcoded `mx::float32` sites in fused-op kernels — activations (gelu, softplus, leaky_relu, masked_fill), normalization (batch_norm, layer_norm, cosine_sim), pool kernels (avg/max 1d/2d/2d_batched), GRU cell, scatter_add, conv1d_circular, scalar ops (add/mul/clamp_min), plus the backward-replay counterparts of all of them. Two helpers do the work: `scalar_like(v, ref)` builds an `mx::array` in `ref.dtype()`; `zero_like` / `one_like` / `half_like` are shortcuts. Commit `a2a5d83`.
- Optimizer state alloc + Adam scalar hoists bind to the first eligible param's dtype (covers `mx::compile` path too). `clip_grad_norm` reduces per-param then sums host-side to avoid mixed-dtype running totals; `clip_grad_value` + `polyak_blend` + `param_load_data` + `param_subtract_delta` + `tensor_subtract_scalar_inplace` honour target dtype.
- Readback paths: `mx_to_doubles` + new `mx_read_double` branch on dtype; `tensor_item` / `item_1d` / `item_2d`, `param_grad_item(_at)(_and_zero)`, `DEBUG_NAN_TRAP` + `DEBUG_PARAM_GRADS` dumps, and the CPU-loop kernels (group_norm, conv_transpose1d/2d) all stage as doubles via `mx_to_doubles` so the f64 path works regardless of underlying storage.
- Cross-backend verification at Supervised seed=42: tape F64 `0.13801130234059747`, mlx F64 `0.13801130332264472` — agreement to 8 significant figures (delta is mlx kernel scheduling, not f32 truncation). mlx F32 GPU mode unaffected (`0.13672828` matches the f64 number truncated to f32). LSTM under mlx F64 converges to 9.69e-4 (was completely broken — supervised loss 271287 — pre-audit).
- Remaining `mx::float32` references in `backend_mlx.cpp` are intentional: the f32 hot-path singletons (`kF32_ZERO/ONE/HALF` used in mlx's f32-only random primitive + as no-op placeholders), the legacy unsuffixed `tensor_create_*` symbols (route to `_f32` aliases for ABI compatibility), and the `compile_dtype` / `opt_dtype` fallback when the optimizer has no params.

Runtime dtype dispatch (RuntimeDType capability interface + per-dtype FFI) — F64 honoured on tape/torch (closed 2026-05-18):
- Added `RuntimeDType` capability interface (`DType/Core.idr`) — separate from the polymorphic `IsDType` metadata interface. Methods cover all 10 creation primitives (`dtCreateScalar`, `dtCreate`, `dtCreate1d/2d`, `dtCreateParam1d/2d/3d/4d`, `dtCreateState1d/2d`). Per-dtype FFI symbols on every backend (`tensor_create_*_f32` / `_f64` on tape, mlx, torch); design rejected a global enum-tag in favour of an open-set capability interface, so new dtypes are local instance + symbol additions.
- C-side per-dtype primitives: **mlx** refactored creation with `_impl` helpers taking `mx::Dtype dt` + `mx_array_from_doubles` (lossless f64 path); **torch** F64 aliases existing unsuffixed, F32 builds via `to(kFloat32)`; **tape** F64 aliases existing unsuffixed, F32 added abort stubs (no parallel float\* arena — separate Medium row tracks the lift). Legacy unsuffixed symbols route to `_f32` on mlx (current historical behaviour) and `_f64` on tape/torch (also current historical behaviour). `RuntimeDType F32` and `RuntimeDType F64` instances in `Tensor.idr` bind through to the per-dtype FFI.
- Cascade through ~25 functions across 17 files: `RuntimeDType dt =>` added to smart constructors (`tconstScalar`, `tparam1d/2d`, `tparamScalar`, `tzeroState1d`, `bulkToTensor`, `bulkToTensor2d`, `vectorToTensorPersistent`, `toTDP`), every layer constructor + apply function (`linearLayer` → `transformerLayer`, ~11 layer files), the `LayerLike` interface (`applyVar` / `applyVarBatch`), `Network` forward (`forwardVar` / `forwardVarBatch` / `forwardVarTraced`), `Backprop`'s 7 training functions, and `Curriculum`'s 3 entry points. Examples (`bulkToTensor {dt=ExampleDType}` etc.) bulk-edited via sed.
- Cross-backend bit-identity verified at F64 for supervised, rnn, lstm — tape ≡ torch within fp64 ULP. Torch gained genuine F32 runtime support as a free bonus of the cascade (every `at::Tensor` carries its own `ScalarType`).
- Commits `9bf794e` (interface), `22dbf95` (mlx C primitives), `e5d754b` (tape + torch C primitives), `7d0b68c` (instances), `9a5b665` (Phase-5 partial cascade), `3fc5456` (cascade through Layer/Train), `e909eed` (bulk helpers + example call sites), `5e5563a` (docs). Docs: `dtype-parameter.md` "Runtime dtype dispatch landed (2026-05-18)" section captures the design + cascade + per-backend verification table.
- Remaining (separate rows): broader dtype coverage (BF16/F16/INT*/UINT*/Bool, F32 on tape) tracked under the Medium row "Broaden runtime dtype coverage across backends".

Examples target F32 on MLX-GPU, F64 elsewhere, plus Jupyter tutorial 08 (closed 2026-05-17):
- 11 `*LayerAny` creators device-polymorphised (`linearLayerAny`, `conv2dLayerAny`, `lstmLayerAny`, `gruLayerAny`, `rnnLayerAny`, `embeddingLayerAny`, `batchNormLayerAny`, `layerNormLayerAny`, `conv1dLayerAny`, `ntmLayerAny`, `dncLayerAny`, `transformerLayerAny`) — `CPU` swapped for a free type variable `d`; bodies unchanged.
- `BuildConfig.idr` (Makefile-generated from `BuildConfig.idr.in` template) exports `ExampleDevice : Type` and `ExampleDType : DType`. Generation rule reads `$(PRIMARY):$(MLX_DEVICE)` against a stamp file, mirrors the existing `.backend-stamp` pattern.
- 23 tensor-using examples migrated from `CPU F64` to `ExampleDevice ExampleDType`. DTypePitch.idr deliberately not migrated (its rejection demo requires hardcoded F64/F32 to demonstrate the type-level pitch).
- `make test-examples` 4-lane matrix: `tape`, `mlx`, `mlx-gpu` (virtual lane: BACKEND=mlx + MLX_DEVICE=gpu), `torch`. SKIP_EXAMPLES accepts both `<lane>:` and `<backend>:` forms. Per-lane `test-examples.expect.mlx-gpu` supported (Makefile picks it up if it exists; calibration deferred to a Metal-hardware run).
- Docs: README front-page tensor type updated to 4-param; new tutorial 08 added to the tutorials list; CLAUDE.md Build Commands gains `MLX_DEVICE=gpu` mode; `docs/develop/dtype-parameter.md` "Per-build-mode dtype selection" lessons-learned section.
- Jupyter tutorial 08 (`packages/jupyter/notebooks/tutorials/08_precision_devices.ipynb`) — `Compatible` table demo, parametric dtype families, `UpcastableTo` partial order, explicit `tcast`/`tcastUnsafe`, build-mode targeting.

Investigate precision type parameter — adopted as `DType`, demo shipped, runtime + autocast deferred (closed 2026-05-17):
- Type-level framework landed. `Tensor` gained a 4th 0-quantity phantom slot `(0 dt : DType)`. `DType.Core` ships parametric families (`Float n`, `BFloat n`, `IntN n`, `UInt n`, plus `Bool`), three layered typeclasses (`IsDType` capability, `Precision` rank-aware subset, `UpcastableTo` derived per-family from `LTE m n`), and an empty `Compatible (0 d : Device) (0 t : DType)` admissibility marker. `MlxDev` reparameterized to `MlxDev : MlxStream -> Type` with `MlxGpu` / `MlxCpu` aliases. Library is fully polymorphic in `dt`; examples pin F64 at the leaf. Commits `55bd35e` (memo), `7b2c81e` (scaffold), `c8d0589` (rename Precision → DType + parametric families), `91651be` (thread through library), `bf625f8` (examples), `02dc04b` (MlxDev split + Compatible + DTypePitch demo), `dd6ce86` (Phase 4 docs), plus `tcast`/`tcastSafe` API surface in the most recent commit. The original row's three investigation questions all answered in `docs/develop/dtype-parameter.md`.
- **Demo lands.** `Example.DTypePitch` exercises the (device, dtype) Compatible table. Positive cases compile (`Tensor [4] CPU F64 WithGrad`, `Tensor [4] MlxCpu F32/F64 WithGrad`, `Tensor [4] (MlxDev MGpu) F32 WithGrad`). The deliberately-commented `failMlxGpuF64` line, when uncommented, produces "Can't find an implementation for `Compatible (MlxDev MGpu) F64`" — PyTorch's runtime `RuntimeError: Float64 not supported on Metal` lifted to compile time. README front-page, `docs/static-vs-dynamic-graphs.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and a new "Open `dt` parameter" entry in `docs/develop/design-decisions.md` carry the prose pitch.
- **Memory-blowup gotcha catalogued.** First attempt at threading dt left method bodies hardcoded to F64 while the Tensor record's slot was polymorphic. Polymorphic-vs-concrete mismatch caused Idris-2's elaborator to allocate a fresh metavar per Tensor reference and hold it cross-method; Chez Scheme RSS climbed past 30 GB on a single library build, four parallel builds during iteration drove iTerm2 to 99 GB. Fix: full polymorphism in every interface method and smart constructor signature, with callers pinning F64 at the leaf. Documented in `docs/develop/gotchas.md` "Polymorphic type-parameter slot vs concrete value in method body" and in the memo's "Lessons learned" section.
- **Subsumed/superseded rows**: the previous "MLX backend: support CPU+f64 mode + dependent-types demo" row is fully absorbed (the type-system demo + docs are all done; the runtime f64-on-MlxCpu / f32-on-MlxGpu mode-selection became "Runtime F32 path on MLX backend" in Low Priority).
- **Deferred follow-ups** (all filed as Low Priority rows above): per-call MLX stream selection (`IsDType` allocator dispatch landed via `RuntimeDType` cascade 2026-05-18); F32 storage on tape (parallel float\* arena); BF16/F16 (waits for CUDA); mixed-precision autocast/`GradScaler` (waits for GPU demand); Jupyter tutorial 08; param-registry serialization across dtypes. **Done since**: F32 storage on torch (cascade bonus 2026-05-18); `tcast`/`tcastSafe` → `tcast`/`tcastUnsafe` rename (2026-05-18); `tcast`/`tcastUnsafe` runtime support (2026-05-18).

Tape per-tape-entry Idris/Chez overhead row — subsumed by Idris-side per-op overhead row (closed 2026-05-17):
- The motivating workload (`example-gpt-large`) was retired on 2026-05-15 along with the original Idris-side mlx-overhead investigation framing. The tape diagnostic (`docs/develop/perf-changes.md` "Tape profiler diagnostic", 2026-05-14) and the mlx diagnostic ("Where the 9000 ms/ep GptLarge wall actually goes", same date) converged on the same root cause: Idris VM time between FFI calls dominates wall, and the C-side profiler can only see the gap as "the op that closed the chain looks slow." Tape's 0.6 ms/tape-entry × 293 × 32-batch ≈ 5.6 s/ep reproduced its 5.8 s "C-time" headline within rounding.
- Both tape and mlx share the same Idris-side floor; the suspects in the two rows were largely identical (`UserDeviceCore` typeclass dispatch, allocation pressure, etc.). The cross-backend row absorbs tape's two backend-specific signals — Chez foreign-procedure dispatch cost compounding per op, and Chez GC triggered by forward-pass allocation pressure — as additional suspects, plus the orthogonal "fix the per-op profile attribution" candidate. Future work happens against whichever live example has the highest tape-entry count + bench-compare gap.

mlx `mx::compile` for optimizer step — Adam landed, remainder closed (closed 2026-05-17):
- Adam path landed 2026-05-14 as `MLX_OPT_COMPILE=1` opt-in. Adam math is now a pure `(params, grads, m, v, per-param lrs, scalars) → (new params, new m, new v)` function compiled via `mx::compile` and cached per active-param-count in a static map. On the gpt-large workload that used to live in the repo (dModel=256): GPU optimizer −23% (156.5 → 120.9 ms/ep), bit-identical numerics on CPU, ~4e-7 fp32 ULP noise on GPU (compile reorders ops). Wall unchanged on both.
- Extending the same compile pattern to SGD / RMSprop / AdamW is *not* being pursued. The pre-landing diagnostic showed C-side is <2% of wall budget on the largest example (Idris VM dominates), so the existing Adam compile is GPU-shaped perf hygiene rather than a wall-mover, and the same goes for the remainder. Adam is the hot optimizer across the example set; SGD/RMSprop/AdamW are colder paths where there is no measurable wall to recover in this VM. Revisit only if a torch-CUDA / mlx-GPU lane comes online and surfaces a measurable optimizer-C-time gap on one of these three.
- The wall-mover for mlx is the Idris-side per-op overhead (`Tensor` / `AnyLayer` / constraint-dictionary cost between FFI calls), tracked in the cross-backend Medium Priority row.

Transformer residual mlx small-model overhead (1.89×) — investigated, closed (closed 2026-05-17):
- `prim__tile2d` primitive landed across all 3 backends (mlx via `mx::tile`, torch via `at::repeat`, tape via manual memcpy) and replaced `reshape3d → add → reshape2d` with `tile2d → add` in `applyTransformerBatch` on 2026-05-15. Result: mlx transformer ratio 1.95× → 1.89× (within VM noise), torch 0.65× → **0.5×** (clean 24% win, biggest beneficiary), tape 0.27× → 0.31× (noise).
- Root cause of the residual mlx small-model slowdown is **not** the reshape ops — it's the fundamental cost of carrying the cached PE tensor on `TransformerState` (extra constant in the graph, extra entry in the constants pool that `mx::vjp` processes). 12–20% absolute slowdown on the tiny dModel=16/blocks=2 path is the price for the 22× wall reduction at dModel=256 the PE cache delivered. Net positive.
- Remaining surgery options (cache the *tiled* PE per batch-size; skip the PE cache for small models via heuristic) would either add real complexity (variable batch size across train/eval) or introduce an ugly model-size threshold. Not worth pursuing — closing as a settled trade-off.

Trim `UserDeviceTape` to dispatching methods + retire dead FFI surface (closed 2026-05-17):
- Distinguished "interface method that dispatches on `d`" from "interface method that's a fixed-symbol passthrough." A method dispatches iff (a) the C state it touches is per-tensor or per-backend (not process-global), (b) the Idris consumer of that state uses the interface method rather than a direct `%foreign`, and (c) different `UserDevice<X> d` instances bind it to different C symbols. The param-registry methods (`primParamCount` / `primParamName` / `primParamGradItem*` / `primParamZeroAllGrads` / `primParamSubtractDelta`), plus `primParamClear` / `primWriteDouble` / `primPrint`, failed all three: registry state is global, `nativeTrainStep` reads it via fixed FFI, and every built-in instance bound them to the same `*Unified` symbol. They were public exports a BYO author would have had to implement, with no behavioural effect.
- Removed those 9 methods from `UserDeviceTape` in `Device/Core.idr`; removed the orphaned instance bindings in `Device.idr` (CPU/CUDA/MPS) and `Device/{Tape,Torch,Mlx}.idr`; removed the `prim__*Unified/Tape/Torch/Mlx` decls that backed them; removed the dead unsuffixed `prim__paramClear/Count/Name/GradItem*/ZeroAllGrads/SubtractDelta/writeDouble/print/lstmCell/clipGradNorm/zeroAllGrads/resetForEval/profileReset/profileReport` decls in `Tensor.idr`, plus the dead `getNumPids` / `resetForEval` wrappers.
- Surviving `UserDeviceTape` methods are the ones that can grow live dispatch as layers gain `UserDeviceTape d =>`: autograd flag (`primRequiresGrad` / `primSetRequiresGrad` / `primNoGradBegin` / `primNoGradEnd` / `primDetach` / `primWithGrad`), tensor-handle shape queries (`primTensorDim` / `primTensorSizeAt`), per-backend allocation (`primParamRegister` / `primCreateParam{1,2,3}d` / `primCreateState{1,2}d`), and scratch buffers (`primAllocDoubles` / `primReadDouble`).
- `KNOWN_DEAD_PRIMS` in the lint shrunk from ~45 entries to 6 (5 `prim__free*` + `prim__setDouble`, each retained for a documented reason). FFI decl count went from 614 → 578.
- Verification: `make check-non-io-side-effects` clean, `make test-examples` tape lane green, targeted smoke on GPT (setParamLR), SAC (polyakUpdate), NTM-copy (param registry intensive), supervised, and the BringYourOwn example all run cleanly.

Non-IO `%foreign` audit + `make check-non-io-side-effects` lint (closed 2026-05-17):
- The IO refactor (commits up to `e337512`) IO-typed every Tensor smart constructor through `ioRerun`. This audit catalogued what other surfaces remained pure-typed but side-effecting. Findings: ~45 dead `%foreign` decls bound to dead `UserDeviceTape` interface methods (separately ticketed for removal under the new High Priority row), plus three live bugs.
- Live bugs fixed: `memoryReport = let _ = prim__memoryReport 0 in pure ()` (Idris DCE drops the let — the FFI never fired); `setParamLR : ... -> ()` typed pure with `pure (setParamLR ...)` at the Gpt caller (worked via Chez's strict-arg eval but invited the bug class); `polyakUpdate = pure (prim__polyakBlend ...)` (`pure`'s arg is strict, FFI fired at IO-value construction rather than at sequencing).
- New `scripts/lifecycle/check-non-io-side-effects.py` lint + `make check-non-io-side-effects` Make target + CI integration via the `check-paired-defaults` job. Catches bare `()` returns and side-effecting C symbols (param_*, no_grad_*, polyak_blend, native_*, optimizer_*, tensor_print, etc.) bound to non-IO Idris types. Known dead surfaces are skip-listed pending the dead-code cleanup row.
- `ioRerun` perf component investigated and retired — see `perf-changes.md` 2026-05-17 entry. Closure overhead is ~100ns/call; LSTM hot path does ~40k IO ops × 200 epochs = <5 ms wall in total. Row 16's per-op Idris VM overhead (~7.6 ms/op on GptLarge) is the real lever for mlx-cpu small-op recovery, not `ioRerun` shape.

Transformer: cache causal mask (closed 2026-05-17):
- Follow-up to the 2026-05-14 PE-caching commit. `prim__causalMask sI` was rebuilt every `blockForward` (single-sequence path) and `prim__expandMask (prim__causalMask sI) batchSize` rebuilt every `batchBlockForward` (batched path) — same deterministic-state-rebuild anti-pattern PE had.
- Fix: added `TMat seqLen seqLen` cached-mask field to `TransformerState`. Single-sequence path threads the cached AnyPtr through `foldBlocks` → `blockForward` → `runHeadAttn`. Batched path expands the cached mask once per batch (outside the fold) and threads the 3D AnyPtr through `foldBlocksBatched` → `batchBlockForward`.
- Lifetime bug surfaced and fixed during numerics verification: caching `prim__causalMask sI` directly gave a dangling pointer after the first `tape_reset` (arena-allocated tensor whose memory got clobbered). Routed through `prim__createState2d` with an Idris-side `writeCausalMask` loop populating the upper triangle, mirroring the PE-cache pattern exactly.
- Bit-identical numerics across tape/torch/mlx (3-epoch transformer at seed=42: `Predicted: 11110$ sort_acc=1/6`); perf within VM noise but trending negative on FFI-dominated cells (-5% torch, -7% mlx-cpu, -9% mlx-gpu). See `docs/develop/perf-changes.md` 2026-05-17 "Transformer causal mask cache" entry.

GPU-shaped example (closed 2026-05-15):
- Goal: an example where mlx GPU > CPU, motivating flipping the `MLX_DEVICE` default. First attempt was `Example/GptLarge` (dModel=256, 3.17M params); investigation 2026-05-14/15 showed it sat ~20% slower on mlx GPU vs CPU because (a) per-op kernel-launch wall makes small-tensor workloads structurally CPU territory, and (b) Idris VM time dominates wall (~8.6 s/ep vs ≤140 ms C-total), hiding any C-side gains. Path A "compile whole step" diagnosed as ineffective (FFI is 0.006% of wall). PE caching delivered a 22× wall reduction but the resulting C fraction was still small. Conclusion: wrong scale for this VM.
- Final fix: new `Example/MatmulBench.idr` — pure-compute matmul microbench at N=2048–4096 (regime where compute ≫ launch overhead), threaded through the typed `Tensor` API + tape autograd. Measured: GPU 1.76× faster than Accelerate CPU at N=2048, 3.56× at N=4096. `MLX_DEVICE` default unchanged (CPU still wins at example sizes); `MLX_DEVICE=gpu make example-matmul-bench` demonstrates the win.
- GptLarge retired (deleted `Example/GptLarge.idr` + `torch_ref/scripts/gpt_large.py`); MatmulBench wired into `test-examples`, `perf-log.jsonl`, baseline. mlx now declared as a darwin-only dep of `idris-ml-torch-ref/pyproject.toml` and installed via `uv sync` into the project venv; Makefile auto-resolves `MLX_SITE` by validating `mlx.metallib` presence at the venv path (no more `nix build nixpkgs#python3Packages.mlx` fallback, which materialized a CPU-only mlx). LLM-class example (Llama-shape inference) queued separately as a Medium-priority follow-up for the "real workload" version.

HPO B6 — env-coverage gaps closed (closed 2026-04-30):

Optional layer gaps (Residual, BatchNorm) deferred indefinitely — only worth doing if a natural fit emerges.

Paired-side example/ref drift checker (closed 2026-05-12):

Diffs per-flag default values across all 18 paired examples; per-example `idris_only` / `python_only` overrides declare legitimate divergences (NTM/DNC RMSprop alpha/eps/momentum baked into Python's optimizer call site, idris-side ES patience knobs, etc.).

NTM-copy Path-C 2× perf regression (closed 2026-05-12):

At commit `545e194` the same 100-epoch run is **20 ms/epoch** (12 s wall total). ntm-recall also **20 ms/epoch**, dnc-copy **10 ms/epoch** — both candidate "also-affected" examples are healthy.

Architecture-specific fused C ops removed (closed 2026-05-07):
- Three single-architecture C fusions deleted from the cross-backend FFI surface: `tensor_ffn_relu` (Transformer FFN), `tensor_ntm_read_head` (NTM addressing pipeline), `tensor_ntm_interp_write` (NTM write). Each was paper-specific and not what a PyTorch convert would expect at the FFI boundary. Replaced with Idris compositions using only generic primitives (matmul, cosine_similarity, softmax, conv1d_circular, pow, outer, etc.). Verified bit-identical numerics on tape across all three backends (`example-ntm-copy`, `example-ntm-associative-recall`, `example-transformer`, `example-gpt`). Also removed dead `OP_NORMALIZE` op (no longer needed once normalization composed from generic div+sum). Also removed stale `NtmReadHeadMeta` / `NtmInterpWriteMeta` structs and their backward replay cases. Doc updates: `gotchas.md`, `design-decisions.md`. Plan: `~/.claude/plans/critique-make-test-examples-smooth-dongarra.md`.

HPO Track A + B1–B3 (closed Apr 2026):
- **A1** `583a68e` — `Schedule` wired into `TrainConfig` per-epoch hook on both sides; new `optimizer_set_lr` C op across tape/MLX/torch backends.
