All others ship-as-is per cross-backend agreement gate (>2× → unreliable). Optimizer pattern: SGD 3/3 informative, RMSprop 4/4 unreliable, Adam/AdamW 8/8 fallback or unreliable — the fastai-vs-modern-optimizer story confirmed across the suite. Two structural `lrFind` limitations exposed (negative-loss handling, fallback detection) — filed as future improvement candidates. Full per-example log in `docs/develop/hyperparameter-tuning-2026.md`.
- **B3-fixes** (2026-04-30) — (1) PPO: env swapped Pendulum (continuous, Gaussian) → Acrobot (discrete, categorical). Both Idris + PyTorch sides rewritten on Acrobot with `lr=3e-4, rollout=1024, K=10, 100 rollouts`. PyTorch 5/5 seeds converge to -63 to -106 (solved is ~-100). Idris 5/6 seeds -63 to -94 (seed=4 reproducible bad-init collapse). Convergence threshold updated `≥ -800` → `≥ -150`; smoke threshold `≥ -2500` → `≥ -550`. Closes Acrobot coverage gap from B1. (2) GPT: default shrunk `--corpus tinyshakespeare --epochs 1000` (~30 min) → `--corpus embedded --epochs 30` (~40 s); warmup proportional to epochs. New `make example-gpt-full` for the canonical char-LM convergence run. Convergence threshold updated `val_bpc < 3.5` → `bpc < 5.0`. See `docs/develop/hyperparameter-tuning-2026.md` "B3-fixes" section.
- **B3-followup library fixes** (2026-04-30) — (1) Sign-stable divergence in `lrFind` (`hasDiverged` helper) — fixes the negative-loss bug from B3. (2) Fallback-curve detection (`isFallbackCurve` helper + `rec ≤ lrMin` gate) — emits `WARNING: fallback recommendation` when the swept curve has no useful descent. +12 Idris tests, +12 PyTorch tests across both fixes.
- **B3-redogfood** (2026-04-30, sessions 4–5) — Re-ran lrFind on Reinforce/A2c/Dqn (session 4) + Ppo (session 5) after the sign-stable fix. All 4 ship-as-is per cross-backend gate (Reinforce 11.2×, A2c 6.4×, Dqn both fallback, Ppo ~2000×). Added missing PyTorch CLI scripts for A2c, Dqn (session 4) and Ppo (session 5); negated `reinforce_epoch` return for the lr_find path (was inverted vs Idris). Closes the negative-loss-bug loop opened in B3 and the gap left by B3-fixes' Pendulum→Acrobot env swap.
- **Skip-decision audit** (2026-04-30, session 5) — User requested verification that all skipped/partial items had defensible reasons. 7 items audited (A3 PyTorch dispatch, B3 Sac warmup-gated, B3 Gpt per-param LR, B3-redogfood PPO gap, B4 NTM/DNC blocked, B6 deferral, B5 Transformer doc gap). 5/7 defensible as documented; PPO redogfood (most actionable) closed in same session; B5 doc cross-link to Transformer added. Audit table in `/Users/admin/.claude/plans/critique-make-test-examples-smooth-dongarra.md` "Skip-decision audit" section.
- **B6 GRU calibration spike** (2026-04-30, session 5) — First B6 ticket. New `Example.Gru` mirroring Lstm pattern-prediction at lr=0.5 (architecture: `GRU(1,4) ~> Linear(4,1)`). Multi-seed: **5/5 Idris (loss 6.06e-4 to 8.76e-4) + 5/5 PyTorch (loss 6.83e-4 to 1.34e-3)** — 50× margin under the 0.05 threshold. lr_find: cross-backend agreement **1.00× exact** (both recommend 0.475 vs default 0.5; ship-as-is). New PyTorch `LinearGRUCell` mirroring the C-kernel simplified-GRU variant. Layer-side: shipped `applyGeneric` for GRU (was crash-only previously) — needed for `evaluateRecurrent`. Filed correctness-improvement TODO for proper r-gate masking in C kernel. **Calibration finding**: pure-port layer gaps are ~1 hour of scope, not 1–2 days; env-gap tickets likely fit the day estimate.
- **B6 FrozenLake** (2026-04-30, session 6) — Second B6 ticket. Closes the FrozenLake env coverage gap from B1. New `Example.FrozenLake` (Idris) + `torch_ref/models/frozen_lake.py` (PyTorch reference) implementing tabular Q-learning on the slippery 4×4 FrozenLake-v1 with the same `(envSeed, noise)` per-episode pattern used by `Example.MonteCarlo` for Blackjack. Defaults `alpha=0.1 gamma=0.99 epsilon=0.3 epochs=10000 seed=42`; tuned up from CliffWalking's `eps=0.1 epochs=500` because the sparse-reward + slippery-dynamics combination needs more exploration to bootstrap. Multi-seed: **5/5 Idris (avg_return 0.66–0.80, mean 0.72) + 5/5 PyTorch (avg_return 0.56–0.75, mean 0.70)** — closely aligned, both well above the 0.4 threshold. Tabular RL is deterministic given seed, so all three backends produce identical avg_return=0.74 at seed=42. Smoke = full convergence run (no epoch override needed, ~2 s/run). Recorded in `docs/develop/{example-coverage,hyperparameter-tuning-2026,reference-alignment}.md`.
- **B6 Taxi** (2026-04-30, session 6) — Third B6 ticket. Closes the Taxi env coverage gap from B1. New `Example.Taxi` (Idris) + `torch_ref/models/taxi.py` (PyTorch reference) implementing tabular Q-learning on Taxi-v3. Mirrors `Example.QLearning` (CliffWalking) almost line-for-line — same fixed-start template, no slip noise. Defaults `alpha=0.1 gamma=0.99 epsilon=0.1 epochs=20000 seed=42`. From the fixed `defaultStart` (taxi (2,2), passenger R=0, destination B=3), the wall layout forces an optimal trajectory of 13 actions yielding +8 reward. Multi-seed: **5/5 Idris + 5/5 PyTorch all hit avg_return=8.0** (the optimum). Smoke = full convergence (~7 s/run on tape; identical RESULT across all 3 backends). Threshold `avg_return >= 5`. Recorded in `docs/develop/{example-coverage,hyperparameter-tuning-2026,reference-alignment}.md`. **Calibration**: ~30 min idea→commit; ports onto deterministic envs that share the QLearning template are sub-hour scope.
- **B5 — Multi-seed validation** (2026-04-30) — Re-ran the convergence target on ≥5 seeds for every example whose defaults changed during HPO. **Lstm** (lr 0.03 → 0.5): 5/5 both backends, all loss < 0.005. **Gpt** (corpus tinyshakespeare/1000 → embedded/30): 5/5 both backends, bpc 4.20–4.54. **Ppo** (env Pendulum → Acrobot): 5/5 PyTorch + 5/6 Idris (seed=4 isolated bad-init reproducible). **Transformer** (dModel 32 → 16, HeadDim 8 → 4): 5/5 both backends at sort_acc=6/6 (perfect). All four cases recorded in `docs/develop/reference-alignment.md`. Closes B5 in full.
- **B4** (2026-04-30) — Four examples explored, one shipped. **Transformer dModel 32→16, HeadDim 8→4: SHIPPED** (5/5 sort_acc=6/6 both backends; first B4 default change). **Dqn hidden 64→32: reverted** (Idris seed=4 regressed 117→13). **Mnist Conv2D (16,32)→(8,16): reverted** (50% wall-time win but Idris seed=3 dropped to 0.832 < 0.85 threshold). **Gpt dModel 64→32: reverted** (Idris seed=3 hit bpc=5.03, just above 5.0 threshold). Pattern: matmul-bound + capacity-margin → ships; matmul-bound + at-edge capacity → regresses. **Remaining**: NTM/DNC memory N (blocked on layer-perf TODO). B4 effectively closed.

SAC actorLoss batched forward (closed):
- Root cause of the original SIGSEGV: tape backend's `tensor_stack_from_array` (backend_tape.c:4242-4305) ignores its `dim` argument and hardcodes `shape[0] = count, rank = 1`. Stacking B `[4]` tensors via `stackRowTensors` produced `[B]` rather than `[B, 4]`, so the next batched Q-net forward read out-of-bounds memory.
- Fix: new `tensor_concat_2d_axis1(A[m,n], B[m,k]) → [m, n+k]` C op across all three backends (tape: hand-rolled forward + backward by column-split; MLX: `OP_CONCAT_2D_AXIS1` enum + replay via `mx::concatenate`; torch: one-liner via `torch::cat`). 3 new test_backend.c cases (forward, batched-vs-per-sample-cat2 equivalence, finite-diff backward) pass on all three backends. New `prim__concat2dAxis1` FFI binding + Idris unit test in `Test.BatchedForward` covering the actor+concat+Q chain at small dims.
- `actorLossBatch` in `Example/Sac.idr` replaces the per-sample `traverse (actorLoss ...)`: bulkToTensor2d → batched actor forward → tensorAdd + tanh + mulScalar for the reparam → prim__concat2dAxis1 → batched Q1/Q2 forward → per-sample loss assembly via prim__select on `[B, 1]` outputs. ~2× speedup on the 2000-ep smoke (~95 ms/epoch → ~47 ms/epoch); `MV` no longer in the top backward ops (was 1573 ms / 1.5M calls before).

MountainCarCont B6 (closed — last B6 env-coverage gap):
- `Example/MountainCarCont.idr` (Idris) + `models/mountain_car_cont.py` + `scripts/mountain_car_cont.py` (PyTorch). SAC with velocity-magnitude reward shaping mirroring discrete MountainCar. Same defaults: `lr=3e-4 gamma=0.99 alpha=0.2 batch=64 buffer=100K warmup=1000 tau=0.005 shaping=10.0 epochs=30000`. Episode length 999 (Gymnasium default). Distinguishes natural termination (goal → buffer done=True) from truncation (999-step → buffer done=False) — correct treatment that Pendulum SAC didn't need
- Multi-seed at threshold ≥ 85: 5/5 Idris (94.86, 95.29, 95.37, 95.35, 95.47; mean 95.27) + 5/5 PyTorch (88.3, 93.7, 93.4, 96.6, 92.8; mean 92.96). 10/10 pass, near-optimal (max ~98-100 from terminal +100 minus action penalty)
- Idris-vs-PyTorch convergence-speed gap: Idris breaks through at ~5-6K env steps and ES-terminates at 6-8K; PyTorch needs ~14-16K to break through and runs the full 30K. Most likely cause is the default Linear init mismatch (Idris Xavier vs PyTorch Kaiming-with-a=sqrt(5)) — for the actor's first layer 2→64 the magnitudes differ ~2.3×, materially affecting initial exploration on sparse-reward tasks. Aligned-defaults policy uses 30K (the slower side); Idris terminates early via WindowedAvg ES so wall-clock stays similar (~13 min)
- Closes the LAST B6 env-coverage gap. Optional layer gaps (Residual, BatchNorm) remain unaddressed — defer indefinitely

MountainCar B6 (closed):
- `Example/MountainCar.idr` (Idris) + `models/mountain_car.py` + `scripts/mountain_car.py` (PyTorch). DQN on MountainCar-v0 with velocity-magnitude reward shaping (`r_shaped = r_raw + 10 * |v_next|`) — provides the dense intermediate signal MountainCar's sparse -1/step reward lacks. Eval reports the raw return so it's directly comparable to standard MountainCar reporting (-200 floor, -110 reliable solver, -100 optimal)
- Multi-seed at threshold ≥ -160: 5/5 Idris (-152, -104, -104, -110, -102; mean -114) + 5/5 PyTorch (-110, -106, -109, -150, -140; mean -123). Cross-backend mean delta ~9 = within DQN seed noise. Wall-clock: Idris tape ~17:48 / 500 episodes / seed=42; PyTorch ~85s / 500 episodes
- Required prerequisite was the batched-forward DQN refactor (commit `c7fe136`); without it the per-epoch cost was ~4 s on tape (200-step episodes × 64-batch per-sample forward), making multi-seed validation infeasible. With batched forward it's ~2.1 s/epoch (~50% reduction). The wall-clock vs PyTorch ratio (~12.6×) is the standard FFI-per-step overhead seen across all RL examples on tape, not specific to MountainCar
- Closes the second-to-last B6 env-coverage gap. MountainCarCont (Gaussian-policy continuous version) remains as a pair-up with PPO or SAC; it's the only remaining B6 ticket
- Notes: shaping isn't strictly policy-invariant in the Ng99 sense (alters Q*) but the optimal trajectory is preserved — kinetic energy is the proven precursor to reaching the goal in MountainCar, so the bonus reinforces correct intermediate behavior

DQN batched forward (closed):
- `Example/Dqn.idr`'s `perTransitionLoss` did one `forwardVarTensor` per batch element. For CartPole this was fine (short episodes, ~50 ms/epoch) but on long-episode envs like MountainCar (200-step truncation × 64-batch) it ran ~4 s/epoch — the wall that blocked MountainCar B6. Refactored to `batchLossBatched` mirroring the SAC `qLossBatch` pattern: `bulkToTensor2d` over the obs vec → one `forwardVarTensorBatch` returning `[B, NumActions]` → per-row `prim__select` to extract Q(s, a) for each transition's action index → wrap as `Var` and assemble the squared-error loss list. Target Q stays per-sample (Double, no FFI per scalar). Online forward goes from B calls to 1 per train step, dropping tape entries by ~B× per train step. Multi-seed CartPole DQN: 5/6 seeds at threshold ≥100 (200, 102, 106, 112, 200, 91) — parity with the pre-batched 4/5 baseline. Smoke gate (`make test-examples`) passes identically across all 3 backends. Unblocks MountainCar B6
- PyTorch reference (`models/dqn.py`) was already batched natively via `q(obs).gather(1, actions.unsqueeze(1))` — no PyTorch changes

Batched Variable forward path for RL (closed):
- New `tensor_linear_2d(W[o,i], X[B,i], bias[o]) → Y[B,o]` C op across all three backends (`backend_tape.c` with hand-rolled fused forward + backward via dgemm; `backend_mlx.cpp` with new OP_LINEAR_2D + LinearReplayMeta replay case; `backend_torch.cpp` one-liner via `torch::nn::functional::linear`). 3 new test_backend.c cases (forward, batched-vs-per-sample equivalence, finite-diff backward) pass on all backends with cross-backend numeric agreement
- `LayerLike` interface gains `applyVarTensorBatch` with a default per-row loop fallback (covers any layer; correctness only, no speedup). Linear and Activation override: Linear dispatches to `prim__linear2d`; Activation reuses the existing shape-agnostic `prim__tanh`/`prim__sigmoid`/`prim__clampMin`/`prim__gelu`/`prim__silu`/`prim__leakyRelu` directly on `[B, n]`. New `forwardVarTensorBatch` recurses through any `Network`. Helpers: `bulkToTensor2d : Vect b (Vector i Double) → AnyPtr` and `stackRowTensors : Vect b AnyPtr → AnyPtr` in Variable.idr
- A2C / PPO / SAC examples refactored to use batched forward in their update phases. Per-sample loss expressions still build via `prim__select` on the [B, ...] outputs (same math, ~B× fewer tape entries). A2C: tape entries last-fwd 12 (was ~80); 29ms/epoch (was ~50ms); avg_return=160 at 5000 epochs / seed=42 still passes the 150 threshold. PPO/SAC: same pattern; partial SAC actorLoss left per-sample (per-sample random eps + grad-tracked qInput stacking is more involved — follow-up)
- Idris unit test (`test/src/Test/BatchedForward.idr`): two MLP shapes (Linear→Tanh→Linear, Linear→ReLU→Linear) verify batched-forward output and gradients match per-sample at 1e-6 tolerance

Package refactor:
- Moved `Generate.idr` (synthetic task-data generators: copy/recall/pattern/reversal/sorting) out of `idris-ml` core and into `idris-ml-examples/src/`, alongside its example consumers. New `packages/idris-ml-examples/test/` harness (mirrors idris-gym's test layout) hosts the moved `Test.Generate` suite; `make test-examples-unit` runs it. New `install-examples` Makefile target installs `idris-ml-examples` as a library so the test harness can depend on it. `Notebook.Prelude` drops the `import public Generate` re-export (the one notebook that referenced Generate did so in prose only, not a live cell). Shrinks the core library's surface and makes the separation between general-purpose training primitives and task-specific scaffolding explicit

RL algorithms suite:
- Tabular (Round 1): Q-learning on CliffWalking (`Example/QLearning.idr`, converges to -13 optimal), SARSA on CliffWalking (`Example/Sarsa.idr`, -19 safer path, classic off-policy vs on-policy contrast), first-visit Monte Carlo on Blackjack (`Example/MonteCarlo.idr`, win_rate ≈ 0.42 vs 0.28 random). Q-table reuses `Tensor [|S|, |A|] Double` to keep storage consistent with deep examples
- DQN on CartPole (`Example/Dqn.idr`, greedy eval 200/200) — online Q + `toDoubleNetwork` snapshot as frozen target, `RL.ReplayBuffer` for experience replay, epsilon-greedy with linear decay
- A2C on CartPole (`Example/A2c.idr`) — separate actor + critic with scope-prefixed paramIds (see below), GAE, mini-step rollouts; Idris 4/7 vs PyTorch 3/7 seed convergence at aligned config
- PPO on Pendulum (`Example/Ppo.idr`) — Gaussian policy with learnable log_std, clipped surrogate, K-epoch mini-batch updates; ships at rollout=400 CI config (greedy=-1572 vs PyTorch -1197 at same) — full convergence needs rollout=2048 (deferred: batched Variable forward)
- SAC on Pendulum (`Example/Sac.idr`) — tanh-squashed Gaussian actor, twin Q-networks, hard target sync (Polyak τ=0.005 deferred)
- Shared RL primitives: `packages/idris-ml/src/RL/ReplayBuffer.idr` (ring buffer with IOArray, uniform sampling; 9 unit tests) and `packages/idris-ml/src/RL/Gae.idr` (Schulman 2015 GAE, pure, 9 unit tests with hand-computed reference values)
- `Train.runTrainingIO` — IO-based epoch-function variant of `runTraining` for examples that need sampling / RNG threading inside the epoch (used by DQN/A2C/PPO/SAC; pure `runTraining` now thin-wraps it)
- **ParamId scoping fix**: A2C/PPO/SAC all construct multiple Variable-CPU networks registered with a single optimizer. Naive `autoName net` on each puts them in conflict: both register `ll0_weights` in the backend param registry, and the second call overwrites the first's entry — silently zeroing the first network's gradient flow. `autoNameScoped "actor_" net` (inlined per-example for now, see `Example/A2c.idr`) threads a prefix through to each layer's `nameLayer` so the consolidated weight tensors get distinct registry keys. This was a latent bug masked by single-seed convergence claims; CLAUDE.md now requires multi-seed convergence testing to prevent recurrence
- Policy docs: `docs/develop/reference-alignment.md` + CLAUDE.md strengthened with "no silent architectural pivots" rule and multi-seed convergence requirement
- PyTorch references: `torch_ref/models/{q_learning,sarsa,monte_carlo,dqn,a2c,ppo,sac}.py` + tests under `correctness/`, all passing

Architecture & infrastructure:
- **Tape-realloc SIGSEGV bug class structurally eliminated**: replaced the contiguous `static TapeEntry* tape` (grown via realloc → free(old_tape) → stale-reader SIGSEGV) with a chunked arena tape — a linked list of fixed-size 64K-entry chunks that are never resized or freed mid-life. New generic `TypedArena<T>` in `backend_tape.c` (sister to the existing byte-level `ArenaChunk`); reusable for any future fixed-size-element chunked collection. `tape_append` now returns `TapeEntry*` (not `int`), refactoring ~21 post-append-write sites mechanically. `tensor_backward`'s reverse walk builds a forward chunk-array and walks tail-to-head; total work stays O(N). Verified: DNC default config (N=128 M=20 H=100 R=1 batch=16 seqLen=1-20) runs through 7.5M tape entries in one epoch with 49 MB peak RSS — previously SIGSEGV'd at the 2M-entry realloc boundary. Past workarounds (`TAPE_INIT_CAP=2M` pre-size, signal-handler override, smoke skipping at `--batch 1`) all moot now
- DNC-on-MLX state-Variable lifecycle: `LayerLike` for `DncState` and `LstmState` now overrides `resetState`, called per-sequence by `epochTwoPhaseTensor`'s `resetNetworkState`. DNC rebuilds its scalar state Variables (linkMatrix/usage/writeWeights/readWeights/readOutputs/precedence/dncMemory) as fresh zeros, so the dangling Tensor\*s left over from the previous epoch's `tape_reset` are never dereferenced. LSTM refreshes its `hT`/`cT` persistent tensor handles to fresh zeros (mirrors GRU's existing pattern). Removes the `mlx/example-dnc-copy|mlx/example-dnc-recall` skip from `test-examples`
- `idris-gym` Gymnasium-parity API: `Env state action obs` interface with 4-tuple `step` (Outcome = Continue/Terminated/Truncated, Info dict), `Space` ADT (Discrete/Box/MultiBin/MultiDisc), `Gym.Rng` pure SplitMix64, `Gym.Wrapper.{TimeLimit, Record, Normalize, Action}` helper-function wrappers, `Gym.Vector` sync vector env. Envs: Classic Control (CartPole, MountainCar, MountainCarContinuous, Pendulum, Acrobot) + Toy Text (FrozenLake, CliffWalking, Taxi, Blackjack). 88 unit tests in `make test-gym`. Box2D/Atari/MuJoCo deferred (need physics engines or ROM emulators)
- Monorepo restructure: 6 packages under `packages/` — `idris-ml` (core lib), `idris-gym` (pure Idris RL environments), `idris-ml-examples` (examples), `backends` (C/C++), `jupyter` (kernel), `pytorch` (reference). Local install via `make install` + `IDRIS2_PACKAGE_PATH`. `Gym.Env` interface + `Gym.CartPole` extracted from Reinforce
- Type-safe device placement: `Variable (0 d : Device)` phantom parameter prevents mixing CPU/CUDA/MPS tensors at compile time. Zero runtime cost (erased). `toDevice` for intentional transfers. FFI bindings for `tensor_to_device`/`tensor_device`. 21 library files + 18 example/test files updated
- General DataLoader (`DataLoader.idr`): `mkGeneratorLoader` for synthetic data, `mkIndexedLoader` for file-backed datasets with shuffled epoch iteration (Fisher-Yates via C, position tracking via IORef). MNIST updated to use shuffled loading
- Jupyter kernel (`jupyter/`): pexpect-based REPL wrapper providing interactive notebook experience. `Notebook.Prelude` re-exports all modules. Cell parser auto-routes `:t`/`:doc`/`:exec`/`:let`/expressions. FFI works via dylib copy to `_tmpchez_app/`. Session recovery on crash. `make jupyter-install` + `make jupyter-lab`. Notebooks in `tutorials/` (01-06: concepts) and `models/` (9 per-architecture walkthroughs). `make test-notebooks` runs all headless
- Batched attention: per-sequence attention loop eliminated. All sequences processed in parallel via 3D ops (`bmm_3x3`, `softmax_3d`, `transpose_last2`). FFI calls reduced from B×H×12 to H×8 per block. New ops: `tensor_bmm_3x3` ([B,m,n]×[B,n,k]), `tensor_softmax_3d`, `tensor_transpose_last2`, `tensor_expand_mask`
- Native autograd: torch already used native autograd (2-line backward). MLX migrated from 480 lines of hand-written backward rules to replay-based native autograd via `mlx::vjp` — zero backward rules, MLX handles all gradient computation. Tape backend keeps manual tape (no framework). Adding a new op on MLX: ~2 lines (forward replay case), backward is free
- Model serialization: SafeTensors format (`param_save`/`param_load`, `optimizer_save`/`optimizer_load`), Idris `Checkpoint` module (`saveModel`/`loadModel`), Python interop verified, optimizer state with Adam/RMSprop buffer round-trip
- Cross-backend transfer example (`Example/Transfer.idr`): train→save→continue→save→infer across tape/MLX/torch, `make example-transfer-demo` orchestrates all 3 phases
- Zero `believe_me` policy: all type conversions proven (Nat proofs, erased record proofs, decEq)
- Pure Idris matrix ops: matrixMultiply, transpose, softmaxMatrix, reshapeToMatrix, flattenMatrix
- README.md with static-vs-dynamic graph motivation
- Transformer (single-head causal self-attention, autoregressive character prediction, pure Idris eval path)
- C tape backend (`backend_tape.c`) with build-time backend selection (`BACKEND=tape|torch`)
- Interface-based layer system (`LayerLike` + `AnyLayer` existential)
- Unified training runner (`Train.idr`: `runTraining`, `TrainConfig`, `EarlyStopConfig`)
- Declarative arg parsing (`ArgSpec` + `parseArgs`)
- Uniform example output formatting (banners, progress, timing, RESULT lines)
- Unified test infrastructure: `make test-all` runs Idris unit tests + C backend tests on all available backends + specialized C tests (safetensors, NTM grad, NTM timestep) + integration tests (`test-examples` validates RESULT lines) + PyTorch reference tests. Backend detection via cached dylibs. Consolidated `test_backend.c` — all tests pass on all 3 backends
- `test-examples` hardening: (a) crash and missing-RESULT FAIL paths now dump the captured output with a `  | ` prefix instead of silently discarding it — the bug that let gpt/mnist/seq-classify "crashes" stay undiagnosable; (b) `EXAMPLE_TIMEOUT` (default 600s) wraps each inner make via `timeout`/`gtimeout`, rc=124 reported distinctly from a crash; (c) `test-examples.expect` sidecar file threshold-checks RESULT values (e.g. `loss < 0.5`), with auto-conversion for fraction values like `sort_acc=6/6` and correct NaN rejection — presence-only RESULT no longer counts as pass. Threshold logic lives in `scripts/check-result.sh`
- `test-examples` word-split fix: `extra_args="GPT_ARGS=--epochs 200"` was passed unquoted to `$(MAKE)`, so shell word-splitting sent GNU Make 3.81 two tokens (`GPT_ARGS=--epochs` with value truncated + stray `200` goal) and Make errored with "No rule to make target 200". Root cause of the tape-backend crashes for every example whose ARGS contained a space. Switched to `case` + `"$$extra_args"` quoting
- Cross-backend Transfer in test-examples: added `example-transfer` (per-backend save/load sanity) and special-cased `example-transfer-demo` to run once per test-examples invocation when all 3 backends built, exercising the real tape → mlx → torch safetensors handoff
- `test-examples-convergence` target: RL subset (reinforce, q-learning, sarsa, mc, dqn, a2c, ppo, sac) at 3 seeds (42, 123, 7) against the same expect-file thresholds, requires ≥ 2/3 passes. Catches "ships at seed=42 only" regressions without the 5-seed runtime cost. NTM/DNC deferred (would need dedicated slower job)
- GitHub Actions workflow: `.github/workflows/test.yml` matrix across `ubuntu-latest` + `macos-latest` (both free unlimited on public repos), all 3 backends covered. Initial cut was tape-only; MLX added on macos-latest via `python3 -m pip install --break-system-packages mlx` + `MLX_SITE` env export; torch added on ubuntu-latest via `make ref-setup` (uv) with `actions/cache@v4` keyed on `packages/pytorch/uv.lock` (saves ~2 min cold-cache install on warm runs). The Makefile's `LIBTORCH_PATH` fallback chain auto-detects torch in the venv. test-examples' inner `for b in tape mlx torch` loop picks each backend up automatically and gracefully skips ones whose dylib failed to build. Result: ubuntu-latest runs tape+torch, macos-latest runs tape+mlx, every backend covered without duplicating the matrix. Idris 2 v0.8.0 built from source with actions/cache
- Torch backend FFI: `tensor_alloc_ints` was defined in `backend_torch.cpp` but undeclared in `backend.h`, so C++ mangled the export to `__Z17tensor_alloc_intsi` and Idris' FFI lookup failed. Tape (pure C) has no mangling; MLX has its own `extern "C"` block wrapping all definitions. Torch relies solely on `backend.h`'s `extern "C"` wrapper — declarations must land there. One-line fix added the declaration; unblocks MNIST and SeqClassify on torch
- Torch backend `tensor_cross_attention` mask dtype: the fused attention op inlined `masked_fill` without the `.to(torch::kBool)` cast that the standalone `tensor_masked_fill` already applies. Idris-side causal masks are float64 (the three backends share one FFI signature and tape/MLX don't want bool), which torch's native `masked_fill_` rejects. Cast at the boundary inside `tensor_cross_attention`; unblocks GPT on torch
- Tape backend DNC-copy SIGSEGV: DNC-copy at batch=16 was crashing with `"Exception: invalid memory reference. Some debugging context lost"`. Pre-sizing the tape buffer to 2M entries so `realloc()` never runs (commit 13ca64d) avoids the crash. **An intermediate commit (42b9e44) reverted this in favour of a SIGSEGV-handler override, after 1-epoch testing suggested the signal was spurious. That conclusion was wrong — multi-epoch testing revealed the same crash happens under SIG_DFL (real coredump). Reverted.** Current state: TAPE_INIT_CAP=2M, no signal handler override. Root cause of the realloc-triggered SIGSEGV still unknown — tracked as a TODO
- DNC in test-examples: Re-added both `example-dnc-copy` and `example-dnc-recall` at `--batch 1 --max-len 3/--max-items 2` smoke configs (30 and 10 epochs respectively, ~2 min each per backend). Thresholds loose (`acc_short/acc_k2 >= 0.40`) because DNC barely lifts above random at smoke epoch counts — these catch NaN / full divergence only. Both fit comfortably under the 2M TAPE_INIT_CAP so they don't hit the realloc SIGSEGV. Full-convergence DNC validation remains a TODO (needs a separate slow lane)
- PyTorch reference benchmarks (`pytorch/` directory)

Layers & models:
- Linear, RNN, LSTM, NTM (copy + associative recall), DNC (copy + associative recall). DNC convergence validated on R=1 and R=4: PyTorch ref 100% on both tasks, Idris tracks PyTorch trajectory. R=4 multi-head verified (compile + run, no code changes needed). See `docs/develop/dnc-convergence-results.md`
- Multi-head Transformer (Pre-LN, embedding lookup, sinusoidal PE, layer norm, per-head weights with sum-not-concat). Input: token indices `[seqLen]`, output: logits `[seqLen * vocabSize]`
- REINFORCE on CartPole (`Example/Reinforce.idr`): pure Idris CartPole environment (Gymnasium-compatible physics), REINFORCE with mean-return baseline, `categoricalSample` in Sampler.idr, tensor-level `applyVarTensor` for tanh/sigmoid activations. Converges to 200.0 greedy eval on all 3 backends. PyTorch reference in `pytorch/torch_ref/models/reinforce.py`
- MNIST CNN (`Example/Mnist.idr`): LeNet-style Conv2D(1->16,k=5) -> ReLU -> MaxPool(2) -> Conv2D(16->32,k=5) -> ReLU -> MaxPool(2) -> Linear(512->10). Type-safe spatial dimension chain via `ConvOutDim`/`PoolOutDim` type-level functions. First example with external data (MNIST .idx files). Conv2D + MaxPool2D ops on all 3 backends. ReLU tensor-level activation. PyTorch reference in `pytorch/torch_ref/models/mnist_cnn.py`
- GPT char-level LM (`Example/Gpt.idr`): character-level language model on embedded Shakespeare corpus (1342 chars, 36-char vocab). Reuses TransformerState with embedding lookup, AdamW, autoregressive text generation. PyTorch reference in `pytorch/torch_ref/models/gpt.py`
- Dropout layer (`Layer/Dropout.idr`): inverted dropout with training/eval mode toggle via `setTraining`/`setNetworkTraining`. C ops on all 3 backends
- Batch norm layer (`Layer/BatchNorm.idr`): per-channel normalization with running stats, training/eval mode. C ops on all 3 backends. Note: Peano Nat ceiling limits use to dims ≤ ~500
- Conv1D + MaxPool1D (`Layer/Conv.idr`): 1D convolution and pooling with type-safe `ConvOutDim`/`PoolOutDim`. SeqClassify example classifies synthetic waveforms (sine/square/triangle)
- ReLU, GELU, LeakyReLU, SiLU/Swish tensor-level activations in `Layer/Activation.idr`
- Embedding layer (`Layer/Embedding.idr`): token index lookup via gather/scatter_add. O(1) per token vs O(vocab) for one-hot
- GRU layer (`Layer/Gru.idr`): 2-gate recurrent unit (lighter than LSTM). C ops on all 3 backends
- Residual layer wrapper (`Layer/Residual.idr`): `output = input + inner(input)`. Enables ResNet-style composition
- Average pooling 1D + 2D (`Layer/Conv.idr`): AvgPool1DState, AvgPool2DState
- Grouped/depthwise convolution: `tensor_conv1d_grouped`, `tensor_conv2d_grouped` with groups parameter
- Transposed convolution 1D + 2D: `tensor_conv_transpose1d`, `tensor_conv_transpose2d` for upsampling
- Cross-attention: `tensor_cross_attention(Q, K, V, mask, scale)` for encoder-decoder architectures
- Tensor function wrappers: gather, scatter_add, squeeze, clone, sum_dim, min/max reductions, dim/size queries, embedding. FFI bindings for previously unbound C ops
- Loss functions: MSE, BCE, BCE with logits, cross-entropy, NLL, L1 (MAE), Huber/Smooth L1, KL divergence (standard + log-space)
- Training/eval mode: `setTraining`/`setNetworkTraining` in LayerLike interface (for dropout, batch norm)

Autograd & optimization:
- Tape-based autograd (Wengert list) — originally Chez Scheme, now C backend
- Fused backward rules: OP_MV, OP_LSTM_GATES, OP_GRU_CELL, OP_NTM_READ_HEAD, OP_NTM_INTERP_WRITE, OP_VECMAT, OP_CAT, OP_NARROW, OP_CONV1D, OP_CONV2D, OP_MAX_POOL1D, OP_MAX_POOL2D, OP_DROPOUT, OP_BATCH_NORM, OP_EMBEDDING, OP_GELU, OP_LEAKY_RELU, OP_SILU
- SGD, RMSprop (with momentum), Adam, AdamW (decoupled weight decay) optimizers (native C + Idris-side)
- Global gradient norm/value clipping
- Learning rate schedules (cosine annealing, one-cycle, warmup + cosine, composable warmup wrapper, step LR, exponential LR)
- Per-element optimizer buffers (RMSprop/Adam/AdamW)
- Gradient accumulation: `nativeBackwardOnly` + `nativeOptimizerStep` for split backward/step, `epochNativeTensorPreAccum` convenience function
- Group normalization: `tensor_group_norm` for per-channel-group normalization
- Softmax, LogSoftmax, Sigmoid activations
- Per-parameter LR overrides: `optimizer_set_param_lr` / `setParamLR` (tape + MLX backends). Enables transfer learning (freeze layers, layer-wise LR)
- Xavier/He/LeCun weight initialization

NTM-specific:
- NTM tensor pipeline (LSTM handle pass-through, direct FC calls, tensor_narrow, tensor_cat2) — 380ms → 110ms/epoch
- Fused NTM addressing ops with gradient chain
- Memory leak fixes (arena_reset, grad array cleanup, persistent scalar tracking)
- Windowed convergence early stopping
- Two-phase training (encode then decode)
- Curriculum training module (multi-stage)

Performance:
- Tensor-level forward path (`applyVarTensor`, `forwardVarTensor`) — eliminates scalar packing at layer boundaries
- `epochNativeTensorPre` + `TensorDataPoint` — zero-copy data flow from generator to C
- C-side one-hot encoding (`tensor_one_hot`) — eliminates per-element FFI for data prep
- Transformer: 160ms → 56ms/epoch (2.9x). C backend: 2ms. Remaining ~54ms is Chez Scheme runtime overhead (GC, thunk evaluation, list allocation), not FFI marshaling — see `docs/design-decisions.md`
- Batched transformer forward (`transformerForwardBatch`): projections/FF/norms batched as `[B*seqLen, dim]`, per-sequence loop only for attention. Fixed double-nameLayer bug (stale weight handles)
- Backend profiling (`backend_profile_reset`/`backend_profile_report`)
- NTM-copy: ~110ms/epoch (faster than old C backend's ~120ms)
- Arena allocator with chunked linked list (no realloc)
- Consecutive-data cache in tensor_stack_from_array
- Memory diagnostics (`backend_memory_report()`)
- Operator-level benchmarks (`csrc/bench_ops.c`): matmul, matvec, element-wise, softmax, conv2d, training step at multiple sizes. PyTorch reference (`bench_ops.py`) + comparison table (`compare_ops.py`). `make bench-ops-compare`. See `docs/benchmarks.md`

MLX backend:
- **MLX backend stubs roadmap (closed)**: 14 STUB() abort functions implemented for tape/torch parity. Phase 1 — shape/reduction (`tensor_squeeze` via OP_RESHAPE reuse, `tensor_sum_dim` with new OP_SUM_DIM + SumDimReplayMeta, `tensor_unbatch` via tensor_select loop). Phase 2 — multi-input (`tensor_stack` / `tensor_cat` / `tensor_batch` / `tensor_cat_from_array` via std::vector<int> meta, new OP_CAT_MULTI; OP_STACK replay fixed to honor dim from scalar_arg; tensor_stack_from_array now frees arr matching tape/torch). Phase 3 — autograd plumbing (`tensor_grad` returns mx::contiguous wrap of t->grad; `tensor_detach` and `tensor_with_grad` clones with appropriate requires_grad). Phase 4 — losses (`tensor_mse_loss` and `tensor_cross_entropy` via primitive decomposition — backward auto-flows through replay). Phase 5 — LSTM (`tensor_lstm_gates` delegates to existing _pair, `tensor_lstm_cell` via mv + add + lstm_gates). Plus 3 missing FFI bindings added to Variable.idr (`prim__cat`, `prim__batch`, `prim__catFromArray`). Side fix: `param_grad_item_at` now goes through mx::contiguous so non-contiguous vjp results (notably sum-axis broadcasts) read correctly. 14 new test_backend.c cases verify forward + backward with cross-backend numeric agreement; tape's documented simplified stubs (e.g. tensor_squeeze=clone, tensor_sum_dim=full reduction, tensor_lstm_cell=clone) detected at runtime
- `backend_mlx.cpp` — Apple Metal GPU via MLX C++ API, tape-based autograd on MLX arrays
- All 6 examples work on all 3 backends (tape, MLX, torch) via tensor path
- NTM Copy: tape 100%/98%, MLX 91%/87%, torch (untested recent fixes)
- NTM ops decomposed into primitives with per-op backward rules
- Fused OP_NORMALIZE for numerically stable attention normalization
- `all_tensors` self-registration: every Tensor tracked, non-persistent freed at `tape_reset()`
- State tensor persistence (`tensor_create_state_*` sets `persistent=1`)
- TensorPair tracking and cleanup on both MLX and torch
- RMSprop optimizer implemented (was missing — NTM used RMSprop, weights never updated)
- OP_SELECT backward, OP_POW exponent gradient, broadcast `reduce_grad()`
- Conv1d_circular d_kernel backward sign fix
- Torch: persistent view tensors, RNN `toDoubleLayer` tensor path
- Multi-element unary ops in tape backend (neg, abs, exp, log, sqrt, sigmoid, tanh)
- OP_LOG_SOFTMAX backward rule added to tape backend
